## TASK-SETUP-001: Initialize Tauri 2.x Project with React/Vite Template

**Found:** Repo already has a fully scaffolded Tauri v2 + React/Vite project, well beyond initial-blank-window state (src-tauri has ~122 Rust files across db/commands/ipc/licensing/extraction/ingestion/reconciliation/statements; src/ has 42 TS/TSX files). `identifier` was already correctly `com.dinero.app`. `bundle.targets` already `["app", "dmg"]` (macOS-only, matches spec).

**Deviation found:** `productName` was `"dinero-app"`; Document 30 TASK-SETUP-001 explicitly specifies `"productName": "Dinero"`.

**What I did:** Corrected `productName` to `"Dinero"` in `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`. Left everything else in the file untouched — CSP (`security.csp`) and window dimensions are TASK-SETUP-002/004's scope respectively, not this task's; noted for later (CSP currently allows direct WebView `connect-src` to Google/GitHub domains, which looks like it will conflict with Document 22 §18.1's "those calls are Rust-only, never WebView" — will re-verify when I reach TASK-SETUP-002).

**Verified:** `cargo check` in `src-tauri/` compiles clean (0 errors). `pnpm typecheck` surfaces pre-existing unused-import errors in `src/pages/Instruments.tsx` — unrelated to this task's scope (TASK-SETUP-001 has no acceptance criteria beyond the initial scaffold boot, which is long since superseded by the app's current state); not fixed here to avoid scope creep into TASK-SETUP-007/019.

## TASK-SETUP-002: Configure Strict Content Security Policy

**Found:** `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`'s `security.csp` deviated from Document 30's spec in two concrete ways: (1) `connect-src` let the WebView reach `accounts.google.com`, `oauth2.googleapis.com`, `www.googleapis.com`, `github.com`, and `objects.githubusercontent.com` directly — contradicting Document 22 §18.1's requirement that Google/GitHub calls happen exclusively from the Rust backend, never the WebView (Document 15 Core Principle 3, Core Principle 4); (2) the licensing-backend origin was `https://api.dinero-app.com` instead of the spec's `https://license.dinero.app`. Confirmed via `grep` that no frontend code (`src/`) makes any `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`axios` call at all, and the one `googleapis.com` string match in `Onboarding.tsx` is disclosure copy in JSX text, not a network call — so removing these origins breaks nothing.

**What I did:** Rewrote `security.csp` to match Document 30's exact string: `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' ipc: http://ipc.localhost https://license.dinero.app; img-src 'self' data:; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`. Dropped the unused `asset:` img-src scheme and the standalone `font-src 'self'` directive (no code references either — `default-src 'self'` already covers font fallback under CSP inheritance rules). Kept Tauri's own `ipc:`/`http://ipc.localhost` connect-src entries: these are Tauri v2's own internal IPC transport (not third-party network egress) and removing them would break `invoke()` itself — this is the standard Tauri v2 scaffold CSP entry, a framework requirement, not a deviation from Document 22 §18.1's intent (which is about third-party destinations, not the local Rust↔WebView bridge Document 15 Core Principle 3 mandates as the *only* channel).

**Flagged, not silently resolved:** the task's acceptance criterion ("CI check: production build CSP contains no `localhost` reference") is written assuming only the dev-mode `ws://localhost:*` allowance the task text describes. Read completely literally it would also flag Tauri's own required `http://ipc.localhost` virtual host, which has nothing to do with the dev-server concern the criterion is guarding against and is present in every Tauri v2 scaffold. Treating this as a resolvable engineering-convention judgment call (Document 49 §4 precedent), not a product/business tradeoff worth stopping for. The CI check itself (TASK-SETUP-010) should be written to check for the dev-only `ws://localhost:*` pattern specifically, not a bare `localhost` substring match.

**Verified:** `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('src-tauri/tauri.conf.json'))"` confirms valid JSON. No frontend code depends on the removed origins (grep above).

## TASK-SETUP-003: Configure Tauri IPC Allowlists (Disable Generic Shell/FS Access)

**Found:** Document 30's task text uses Tauri v1 terminology (`tauri.allowlist.shell/fs/http` with `"all": false`) which no longer exists in Tauri v2 — v2 replaced the allowlist model with an opt-in capabilities/permissions system where nothing is exposed to the WebView unless a plugin is added as a Cargo dependency *and* granted a permission in `capabilities`. Checked `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`: no `tauri-plugin-fs`, `tauri-plugin-shell`, or `tauri-plugin-http` dependency exists. Checked `tauri.conf.json`'s inline capability (`src-tauri/capabilities/` directory itself is empty — permissions are declared inline): only `core:default`, `opener:default`, `dialog:default` are granted. Checked all `fs::`/`std::fs` usage in `src-tauri/src` — every call site is Rust-native (`std::fs`, `tokio::fs`) inside backend modules, never exposed to the frontend. The `opener:default` permission is a deliberate, scoped exception for `tauri_plugin_opener::open_url()` (used once, in `src-tauri/src/ingestion/oauth.rs`, to launch the system browser for Google OAuth per TASK-AUTH-001) — not a generic shell-open capability.

**What I did:** No code change. This is the Tauri-v2-equivalent of "disabled" already, structurally: the WebView has no path to raw filesystem, shell execution, or generic HTTP that doesn't already run through a typed `invoke()` command. Confirmed match, no quality gaps found.

**Verified:** `grep` confirms no fs/shell/http plugin dependencies and no matching permission strings anywhere in `tauri.conf.json` or `src-tauri/capabilities/`.

## TASK-SETUP-004: Configure Window Dimensions and Startup Behavior

**Found:** `tauri.conf.json`'s `app.windows[0]` deviated from Document 30's exact spec on four fields: `title` was `"dinero-app"` (spec: `"Dinero"`), `width` was `1200` (spec: `1280`), `minWidth` was `800` (spec: `900`), and `transparent` was `true` (spec: `false` — the doc explicitly calls out non-transparent as a deliberate choice to avoid alpha-compositing overhead on older Macs). `resizable`, `decorations`, and `center` were absent (relying on Tauri defaults, which happen to match `resizable: true`/`decorations: true`, but `center` defaults to `false`, not `true`).

**What I did:** Corrected all four deviating fields and added explicit `resizable: true`, `decorations: true`, `center: true` so the config states its intent rather than relying on implicit defaults.

**Verified:** `python3 -c "import json; json.load(...)"` confirms valid JSON. No acceptance criteria beyond manual launch verification (per doc); config now matches spec exactly.

**Found:** Nearly every dependency Document 30 lists is already present in `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (tauri, tokio full, rusqlite bundled-sqlcipher, deadpool-sqlite, serde/serde_json, reqwest json/rustls-tls, keyring, thiserror/anyhow, tracing/tracing-subscriber, uuid v4, sha2, argon2, aes-gcm, regex, chrono, strsim, sysinfo, oauth2). Adding an unused `once_cell` dependency alongside an already-idiomatic stdlib solution would be pure noise — not adding it.
2. **`sqlx`** — genuinely absent. Document 30's own TASK-DB-002 explicitly specifies `sqlx::migrate!` as the migration runner, but the existing, already-built migration system (`src-tauri/src/db/migrations.rs`) uses the `rusqlite_migration` crate instead — a real architectural substitution, not a missing-dependency oversight. This is a substantive Area 2 decision (keep `rusqlite_migration` and treat Document 30 as superseded-in-practice, or migrate the already-built system to `sqlx`), not something to resolve by silently adding an unused dependency inside a manifest-listing task. **Flagged for TASK-DB-001/002**, not resolved here.

**What I did:** No `Cargo.toml` change. `cargo build`/`cargo check` already succeed (verified under TASK-SETUP-001), which is this task's only stated acceptance criterion.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean (see TASK-SETUP-001/002 verification runs, dependency set unchanged since).

## TASK-SETUP-006: Add macOS RAM Check on App Startup

**Found:** No RAM-gating logic existed in the Tauri `setup()` hook at all. `check_system_ram` (in `src-tauri/src/commands/mod.rs`) is an on-demand IPC command returning RAM in GB — unrelated to this task, which requires an automatic startup check that sets an app-wide `llm_eligible` state and emits a warning event. `llm_manager.rs` has the 5-tier model catalog (`min_ram_gb` per tier, matching Document 16 §12.3) but nothing reads actual system RAM against it at launch. The `AppEvent::SystemWarning` enum variant (`"system_warning"`) already existed in `src-tauri/src/ipc/events.rs` but was never emitted anywhere in the codebase.

**Naming note:** Document 30's task text says emit `system.warning` with fields `{ type, available_gb }`; Document 19 §15.1 (the authoritative Tauri Events catalog) names the event `system_warning` with fields `warning_type`/`message`. Followed Document 19 since it is the authoritative source for all Tauri event names and is explicitly self-described as such — added `available_gb` as a third payload field since Document 19 doesn't prohibit additive fields (§19 versioning policy: additive changes are fine).

**What I did:** Implemented from scratch — new module `src-tauri/src/startup.rs`: `compute_llm_eligibility(ram_gb)` (pure, unit-testable), and `check_ram_and_set_llm_eligibility(app)` which reads RAM via `sysinfo::System::new_all().refresh_memory()`, calls `app.manage()` with an `LlmEligibility { eligible, total_ram_gb }` state (eligible iff RAM ≥ 16 GB, matching Document 16 §12.3's auto-eligible tier — the 8–16 GB tier's smaller models remain available only via a manual settings override that TASK-TXN-006 will wire in when it actually consumes this state), and emits `system_warning` if RAM < 8 GB. Wired into `lib.rs`'s `setup()` hook, running before DB init (which can itself exit the process on several error paths) so the RAM check is never skipped. The function is synchronous, infallible, and never panics or blocks.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. 5 new unit tests in `startup.rs` (`low_ram_is_not_llm_eligible`, `mid_tier_8_to_16gb_is_not_auto_eligible`, `sixteen_gb_is_eligible`, `high_ram_is_eligible`, `boundary_just_below_sixteen_is_not_eligible`) — all pass (`cargo test --lib startup::`).

## TASK-SETUP-007: Configure TypeScript Strict Mode and Path Aliases

**Found:** `tsconfig.json` already had `strict`, `noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters` — but `target` was `ES2020` (spec: `ES2022`), `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` was absent, and only one path alias (`@/*`) existed against the spec's five (`@/*`, `@components/*`, `@hooks/*`, `@types/*`, `@stores/*`). `vite.config.ts` only mirrored the single `@` alias, so the other four wouldn't have resolved at build/runtime even if added to `tsconfig.json` alone. Also confirmed (from TASK-SETUP-001's note) that `npm run tsc` did **not** pass cleanly — this task's own acceptance criterion — due to unused imports in `Instruments.tsx`.

**What I did:** Bumped `target`/`lib` to `ES2022`, added `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true` and the four missing path aliases to `tsconfig.json`, and mirrored all five in `vite.config.ts`'s `resolve.alias` (`@types`/`@stores` point at directories that don't exist yet — harmless for now, needed once TASK-FE-002's Zustand store and any dedicated types module land). Fixed the pre-existing `Instruments.tsx` unused-import errors (`CardHeader`, `CardTitle`, `CardDescription` — confirmed unused via grep). `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` then surfaced one genuine, previously-latent type error in `src/hooks/use-toast.ts`: `dismiss()`'s `dispatch({ type: "DISMISS_TOAST", toastId })` call passes a possibly-`undefined` value into a field typed as bare-optional (`toastId?: string`), which the new flag correctly rejects as different from "field absent." Fixed by widening `DISMISS_TOAST`/`REMOVE_TOAST`'s `toastId` to `?: string | undefined` (matching the TS error's own suggested fix) rather than changing the dispatch call, since the value genuinely can be explicit `undefined` there.

**Verified:** `pnpm typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) passes with zero errors. `pnpm build` (`tsc && vite build`) succeeds — pre-existing chunk-size/dynamic-import warnings are unrelated to this change, not addressed here.

## TASK-SETUP-008: Configure ESLint and Prettier

**Found:** The repo uses ESLint 9's flat config (`eslint.config.js`), not the `.eslintrc.cjs`/`.eslintignore` files Document 30 names — those formats don't exist for this ESLint version; flat config's own `ignores` array (already present: `{ ignores: ['dist'] }`) is the direct equivalent, and flat config has built-in default ignores for `node_modules` etc. Not a deviation, a tooling-generation difference (same situation as TASK-SETUP-003's Tauri v1→v2 allowlist terminology). `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`'s `recommended` config already sets `rules-of-hooks: error` and `exhaustive-deps: warn` by default — matches spec without change. Two real deviations: `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` was `'warn'` (spec: `'error'`), and `.prettierrc` had `semi: true` (spec: no semicolons), `trailingComma: "all"` (spec: `"es5"`), and no `printWidth` (spec: `100`, default is 80).

**What I did:** Set `no-explicit-any` to `'error'` and rewrote `.prettierrc` to `{ semi: false, singleQuote: true, tabWidth: 2, printWidth: 100, trailingComma: "es5" }`, matching spec exactly.

**Flagged, not silently resolved:** running `pnpm lint` with `no-explicit-any: error` now reports **76 errors** (up from 6 pre-existing, unrelated `no-useless-assignment` errors in `Settings.tsx`/`Transactions.tsx`) because ~81 existing `any` usages across `src/pages/*.tsx` are promoted from warning to error. Fixing all of them properly requires per-usage type investigation across files that mostly belong to Area 9 (Dashboard, Instruments, Settings, Statements, Transactions, etc.) — well beyond a lint-config task's scope, and premature before those pages' own TASK-FE-* tasks are reached. Setting the rule to `error` now (matching spec) makes this pre-existing debt visible rather than silently tolerated; **`pnpm lint` will not run cleanly until each Area 9 page's own task fixes its `any` usages** — recommend fixing incrementally as each owning TASK-FE-* is reached, not as a mass fix now. Did **not** apply a project-wide Prettier reformat (`semi:false`/`trailingComma: es5` now differ from how the whole existing codebase is formatted) — that's a repo-wide, ~250-file diff disproportionate to a config-correctness task; the config now states the intended style, actual reformatting is a separate, explicit action if wanted.

**Verified:** `pnpm lint` runs (config loads, no crash) — reports 87 problems (76 errors, 11 warnings) as explained above, not a clean pass. This is a known, flagged gap, not an oversight.

## TASK-SETUP-012: Define AppError Enum and IPC Error Plumbing

**Found:** `src-tauri/src/error.rs` (doc names `errors.rs`, plural — cosmetic) already had `Db`, `Network`, `Auth`, `LicenseLocked(String)`, plus two non-spec variants (`Unknown`, `FileAccessDenied`) used extensively — `AppError::Unknown` alone has 72 call sites, `AppError::Db` 62, across 6 files (`commands/mod.rs`, `commands/debug.rs`, `licensing/commands.rs`, `licensing/gate.rs`, `ingestion/historical_scan.rs`). Spec's `Parse`/`Io`/`Internal`/`Validation` variants were entirely absent. Spec says `LicenseLocked` should be a unit variant; existing code carries a message and has 9 call sites.

**Real bug found (not just a naming gap):** the existing `Serialize` impl emitted a bare JSON string (`"Database error: ..."`), but `src/lib/ipc.ts`'s `invokeCommand()` wrapper — already written, already in use — explicitly checks `'code' in error && 'message' in error` (Document 19 §3.4's structured contract) and falls through to a generic `UNKNOWN_ERROR` when that check fails. Since a bare string never satisfies `'code' in error`, **every single Rust command error was silently arriving at the frontend as `UNKNOWN_ERROR`, losing its real code and message entirely** — a genuinely broken contract, not a stylistic mismatch.

**What I did:** (1) Added `Parse`, `Io`, `Internal`, `Validation` variants additively — zero call-site breakage. (2) Left `Unknown`, `FileAccessDenied`, and `LicenseLocked(String)`'s message untouched — renaming/removing them would require migrating 90+ call sites across 6 files against Document 19 §4's full ~25-code catalog, which is explicitly **TASK-API-010's scope** ("Standardized Error Response Contract Across All Commands"), not this setup task's; flagged for that task rather than resolved here. (3) Added `AppError::code()` mapping each variant to one of Document 19 §4's existing generic codes (`INTERNAL_ERROR`, `NETWORK_ERROR`, `UNAUTHORIZED`, `LICENSE_LOCKED`, `VALIDATION_ERROR`) — deliberately not inventing new codes or assigning the catalog's many domain-specific codes (`SCAN_NOT_FOUND`, `CLUSTER_NOT_FOUND`, etc.), which only make sense assigned per-command. (4) Rewrote `Serialize` to emit `{ "code": ..., "message": ... }`, fixing the real contract bug above. (5) Did **not** write `impl From<AppError> for tauri::ipc::InvokeError` as the doc's task text says to — checked Tauri 2.11.3's actual source (`tauri-2.11.3/src/ipc/mod.rs`): it has `impl<T: Serialize> From<T> for InvokeError`, a blanket impl already covering every `Serialize` type. Writing a second, overlapping impl for `AppError` specifically would be a Rust coherence violation (E0119, conflicting implementations) and fail to compile — the doc's requirement is already satisfied automatically by `AppError: Serialize`.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. 10 new/updated unit tests in `error.rs`, one per variant, asserting the exact `{code, message}` JSON shape — all pass. Ran the full `cargo test --lib` suite before and after (by temporarily reverting `error.rs` and re-running) to confirm the 4 pre-existing failures (`commands::data::tests::test_seed_and_fetch` — unrelated DB schema/column drift; three `phase10_rigorous_tests` — missing fixture files on disk) are identical with and without this change, i.e. genuinely pre-existing and not introduced here. 309 passed both times.

## TASK-SETUP-013: Build Core IPC Framework — Typed Structs and React Hooks

**Found:** `src-tauri/src/ipc/responses.rs` already had `Payload<T> { data: Option<T>, error: Option<String> }` — functionally identical to the spec's `IpcResponse<T>`, just named differently (not renamed, would touch existing call sites for no behavioral gain). `src-tauri/src/ipc/args.rs` already has several typed argument structs with `Serialize`/`Deserialize`/`Debug` derives. What was genuinely missing: (1) any global IPC panic boundary at all (`catch_unwind` only appears once, narrowly, inside `extraction/llm.rs` as an OOM guard for the local LLM — not a command-wide boundary); (2) both React hooks (`useIpcInvoke`, `useIpcListen`) — didn't exist; existing components (`AppLayout.tsx`, `GlobalStateContext.tsx`, `Transactions.tsx`, `SpendingLimits.tsx`) each hand-roll their own `useEffect` + `listen()`/`unlisten()` boilerplate instead; (3) `src/types/ipc.ts` — didn't exist; `AppError`'s shape was a private, unexported `interface` duplicated only inside `src/lib/ipc.ts`.

**Async-safety correction (flagged, not silently followed):** Document 30's task text says implement the boundary via `std::panic::catch_unwind`, matching Document 19 §3.4. That mechanism only catches *synchronous* panics inside its own closure and does not work across `.await` points — but nearly every command in this codebase is `async fn`. Implemented the actual async-safe equivalent instead: `ipc::with_panic_boundary<F, T>()` spawns the future via `tokio::spawn` and inspects the resulting `JoinError::is_panic()`, which Tokio guarantees isolates a panicking task and reports it back as an `Err` — the correct analog to `catch_unwind` for this codebase's real (async) command shapes. Maps a caught panic to `AppError::Internal` per spec; logs via `tracing::error!` (flows into `app-logs.log`, which the diagnostic bundle export already reads) rather than writing an `audit_log` row directly, since the latter would require threading a DB pool into this generic, DB-agnostic primitive.

**What I did:** Implemented `with_panic_boundary()` in `src-tauri/src/ipc/mod.rs` (new file content — 3 unit tests: ok-passthrough, err-passthrough, actual-panic-caught-and-mapped). **Not wired into any of the ~53 existing command handlers** — retrofitting every already-built command (`commands/mod.rs`, `licensing/commands.rs`, etc.) is a wide, invasive change spanning most of Area 8's already-built IPC surface; flagged for TASK-API-001 ("IPC Request Validation Middleware") as the natural integration point, since a DB pool is already in scope there for the `audit_log` write too. Created `src/types/ipc.ts` (canonical `AppError`, plus `IpcResponse<T>` mirroring the Rust `Payload<T>`); updated `src/lib/ipc.ts` to import `AppError` from there instead of its own private copy, and exported its existing `invokeCommand()` helper so the new hook can reuse it rather than duplicate the error-normalization logic. Added `src/hooks/useIpcInvoke.ts` and `src/hooks/useIpcListen.ts` — both additive; existing call sites are not migrated to use them (out of scope, would touch several unrelated component files for a non-functional refactor).

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean; 3 new Rust unit tests pass; full `cargo test --lib` still shows 312 passed (up from 309 — the 3 new ones) against the same 4 pre-existing, unrelated failures. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` both pass clean with the new files and the `lib/ipc.ts` changes.

## TASK-DB-001: Implement SQLCipher Connection Manager with Keychain-Derived Key

**Found:** `src-tauri/src/db/{mod,crypto}.rs` already implement almost everything this task asks for, and go well beyond it (hardware-migration re-keying, recovery-phrase restore, key-mismatch vs. Keychain-access-denied error classification, integrity check, pre-migration backup — much of DB-002/DB-019/DB-021's territory already built). Confirmed via `keyring`/`deadpool-sqlite`/`argon2` usage that the core shape matches: `DbPool` via `deadpool-sqlite` ✓, base key retrieved-or-created via `keyring::Entry` ✓, Hardware UUID via the `machine-uid` crate (cleaner than the doc's literal hand-rolled `ioreg`, an intentional documented upgrade — "I14 fix") ✓, key derivation via Argon2id ✓, `PRAGMA integrity_check` on startup with fail-closed handling ✓.

**Real bug found — Argon2 salt was a hardcoded constant, not hardware-UUID-derived:** `derive_database_key_with_hw_uuid` computed a `padded_salt` from `hw_uuid` bytes and then never used it — the actual salt passed to `hash_password` was `SaltString::from_b64("SGFyZHdhcmVVVUlEU2FsdA")`, base64 for the literal string `"HardwareUUIDSalt"`, **identical for every installation on every Mac**. This defeats a core purpose of salting (per-target precomputation resistance) and doesn't match Document 12/18/22's "Argon2id from the base key and the Hardware UUID" description, where `hash_raw(password, salt, ..)`'s signature means the UUID should be the *salt*, not folded into the password string as the surrounding (confused, self-admittedly-uncertain — "we don't have a base64 crate" while `base64` was already a Cargo.toml dependency) comments ended up doing instead. Device-binding still worked before this fix (hw_uuid was concatenated into the password input), so no functional regression existed, but the salt itself carried zero device-specific entropy — a real, if narrow, cryptographic weakness.

**What I did:** Rewrote `derive_database_key_with_hw_uuid` to use the already-computed `padded_salt` bytes via `SaltString::encode_b64()` (not the deprecated `b64_encode`) as the actual Argon2id salt, with `base_key` alone as the password — matching Document 30's literal `hash_raw(base_key, hardware_uuid_bytes, ..)` shape exactly. Confirmed via `~/Library/Application\ Support/com.dinero.app/` not existing on this machine that no real on-disk database depends on the old (buggy) derivation, so this is a safe correction, not a breaking migration.

**Other gaps found and fixed:**
- **`auto_vacuum = INCREMENTAL` was entirely missing** from the PRAGMA batch (Document 30's own TASK-DB-001 list explicitly includes it; DB-019's future daily `PRAGMA incremental_vacuum(500)` background task would silently no-op forever without it, since auto_vacuum mode can't be changed after tables exist without a full `VACUUM`). Added it — but discovered via a new test (`test_auto_vacuum_is_incremental`) that setting the pragma alone doesn't actually take effect: SQLCipher's own header/salt write during `PRAGMA key` means the file is no longer "virgin" by the time the pragma runs, so the mode change silently fails without an explicit `VACUUM`. Added a guarded `VACUUM` (only runs when `PRAGMA auto_vacuum` doesn't already read back as `2`/INCREMENTAL) so it fires exactly once — without the guard, every new pooled connection created later (e.g. as the Transaction Queue's 2–8 workers scale up) would trigger a full `VACUUM` against an already-large financial database, which would have been a real performance bug.
- **File name: `data.db` → `finance.db`.** Document 18 §7.2 names the file `finance.db`; the app was using `data.db` in `lib.rs`, `commands/mod.rs`, and `commands/data.rs` (2 sites) — 4 production call sites total (3 more `"data.db"` occurrences are arbitrary test-fixture names inside `#[cfg(test)]` modules, left alone). This drift was already partially visible: `migrations.rs`'s pre-migration backup naming (`create_pre_migration_backup`) already hardcoded `finance.db.bak.{timestamp}` regardless of the live file's actual name — meaning the live file and its own backups were already inconsistently named before this fix. Renamed all 4 production sites to `finance.db`, aligning them with the pre-existing backup convention and Document 18. No existing on-disk `data.db` exists on this machine to migrate.
- **Keychain service/account naming**: Document 18 §8 says service `"dinero"`, account `"sqlite-key"`; actual code uses `"com.dinero.app"`/`"dinero-base-key"` — consistent with the Gmail-token Keychain naming already used in `oauth.rs` (itself a documented "H6 fix" rename to match the app's bundle identifier). Not changed — this is a self-consistent, already-deliberate convention across the whole codebase (`crypto.rs`, `oauth.rs`, `statements/password.rs`), and renaming it now would need Keychain-entry migration handling with no corresponding security or spec benefit. Flagged, not treated as a defect.

**Unrelated pre-existing findings surfaced while verifying (not fixed, out of this task's scope, reported for visibility):** `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` — the exact command TASK-SETUP-009's CI runs — currently fails with 21 pre-existing errors across ~9 files (`commands/data.rs`, `commands/mod.rs`, `statements/pending_bytes.rs`, `diagnostics.rs`, `reconciliation/scorer.rs`, `phase8_telemetry_tests.rs`, `phase9_rigorous_tests.rs`, `phase9_security_tests.rs`, `phase10_quality_gates_tests.rs`), none in files this task touches. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` likewise fails across ~48 files (284 diffs, plus rustfmt hard-erroring with "left behind trailing whitespace" on 3 files: `licensing/worker.rs`, `statements/password.rs`, `statements/duplicate_check.rs`). This means Rust CI (`rust.yml`) has likely never actually passed — a materially different fact than my TASK-SETUP-009 assessment ("workflow file matches spec"), which verified the YAML's shape but did not run the commands it invokes. I fixed exactly one of these (a `build.rs` `manual_strip` clippy error) because it was a compile-blocking error preventing me from running clippy at all to verify my own changes; the rest is a large, pre-existing, codebase-wide hygiene debt well beyond TASK-DB-001's scope — not fixed here.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. Full `cargo test --lib`: 313 passed (up from 312 — 1 new test), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. New/updated tests: `db::crypto::hardware_migration_tests::*` (3, unchanged, still pass with the corrected salt derivation) and `db::connection_tests::test_auto_vacuum_is_incremental` (new). Confirmed the `derive_database_key_differs_across_hw_uuid`/`derive_database_key_is_deterministic_per_hw_uuid` tests still pass with the corrected salt-based derivation (hw_uuid changes the salt now, not the password, but still produces different/deterministic keys as required).

## TASK-DB-002: Implement `sqlx` Migration Runner with Pre-Migration Backup

**Found:** The pre-migration backup mechanism (`create_pre_migration_backup`, retention-of-3, `VACUUM INTO`) already matched Document 18 §12.1 exactly (verified under TASK-DB-001) and needed no change. The migration *engine* itself was the flagged deviation carried over from TASK-SETUP-005/012: `src-tauri/src/db/migrations.rs` used `rusqlite_migration` with 19 migrations inline as Rust string constants, not `sqlx::migrate!` against separate `<timestamp>_<description>.sql` files in a `migrations/` directory as Document 30 specifies.

**This was too large a decision to make unilaterally** — asked the user directly (via `AskUserQuestion`) whether to keep `rusqlite_migration` (treating Document 30 as superseded-in-practice) or migrate to `sqlx` as specified, since switching engines would mean rewriting the foundation every one of the remaining 16 DB-* tasks builds on. **User chose: migrate to `sqlx`.**

**Feasibility blocker found and resolved before writing any migration code:** sqlx's SQLite driver has no official SQLCipher support — a serious risk given SQLCipher encryption is a non-negotiable Core Principle (Document 15 §10, Document 18 §7.2). Investigated empirically rather than assuming either way: `cargo tree -p sqlx-sqlite` showed `sqlx-sqlite` and `rusqlite` both resolve to the exact same `libsqlite3-sys v0.36.0` — Cargo's feature unification means enabling `bundled-sqlcipher` via rusqlite's `Cargo.toml` entry compiles that *shared* `libsqlite3-sys` artifact with SQLCipher support, which sqlx then also links against for free. Verified with a throwaway test (written, run, then deleted — not kept in the tree): created a SQLCipher-encrypted file via rusqlite, then successfully opened and queried it via `sqlx::SqliteConnection` with the same `PRAGMA key`. (One real gotcha along the way: sqlx's `.pragma(key, value)` does not auto-quote string values — `write!(string, "PRAGMA {key} = {value}; ")`, verified by reading `sqlx-sqlite-0.9.0/src/options/connect.rs` — so the key must be pre-quoted by the caller or SQLite's parser chokes on a bare hyphenated string.)

**What I did:**
1. Converted all 19 `rusqlite_migration` migrations 1:1 into `src-tauri/migrations/20260101000001_*.sql` through `...000019_*.sql` (sequential fake timestamps — real historical timestamps don't exist for already-written migrations, and sqlx only needs monotonic uniqueness). Dropped each migration's `.down()` SQL entirely rather than porting it to sqlx's paired-file reversible-migration format: Document 18 §13.1 already states forward-only, no supported rollback, making the down-migrations dead weight to carry forward. Preserved the full historical sequence faithfully, including the 0015→0016 add-then-remove of the forbidden `banks` table — a safe, conservative choice given the whole existing schema is validated by hundreds of passing tests already; no editorializing.
2. Rewrote `migrations::run_migrations()`: now `pub async fn run_migrations(db_path: &Path, sqlcipher_key: Option<&str>) -> Result<()>`, opening its own dedicated `sqlx::SqliteConnection` (not sharing the app's rusqlite/`deadpool_sqlite` pool — a structurally separate connection stack) and running `sqlx::migrate!("./migrations")`. `sqlcipher_key: None` skips the key/cipher pragmas entirely for schema-only test databases — added specifically to avoid needing to re-thread key state through dozens of test call sites that never cared about encryption in the first place.
3. Restructured `db::init_db()` (`db/mod.rs`): the old single `conn.interact()` closure did integrity-check + backup + migration + seeding all together, synchronously. Split into: (A) rusqlite `interact()` for integrity check + `create_pre_migration_backup`, returning `Result<PathBuf, String>`; (B) the new async `migrations::run_migrations(&db_path, Some(&db_key)).await`, mapping failure to the same pre-existing `DbInitError::MigrationFailed { source, backup_path }` (the lib.rs recovery-dialog/rollback UX is completely unchanged); (C) a second small `interact()` for the post-migration `local_profile` seed row and stuck-checkpoint reset. Removed the now-dead `MigrationOutcome` enum.
4. Removed the `rusqlite_migration` Cargo dependency (confirmed zero remaining references).

**The much bigger discovery, mid-implementation:** making this real broke the test-bootstrap pattern in every DB-table test module. ~19-20 files (`pattern_rules_tests.rs`, `audit_log_tests.rs`, `sessions_tests.rs`, `recurring_payments_tests.rs`, `categories_tests.rs`, `tags_tests.rs`, `commands/data_tests.rs`, `db/tests.rs`, `reconciliation/engine_tests.rs`, three `phase9`/`phase10` files, `extraction/ladder.rs`, and more) bootstrap their test database via `Connection::open_in_memory()` then called the old *synchronous* `run_migrations(&mut conn)` directly against that same connection. sqlx cannot reach an in-memory rusqlite connection at all — they're fundamentally separate, unshareable per-connection databases. **Asked the user again** given the true scope (touching most of Area 2's already-built test suite) was materially larger than what "rewrite the migration runner" had implied — **user chose to push through now.**

**What I did for the test suite:** added `src-tauri/src/db/test_helpers.rs` — `setup_test_db()` (sync, bridges to async via its own `tokio::runtime::Runtime::block_on`, for the ~150+ ordinary `#[test]` functions) and `setup_test_db_async()` (for the rare `#[tokio::test]` call site — the sync version's own nested `Runtime::new()` panics with "Cannot start a runtime from within a runtime" if called from inside one already). Converted all ~20 files' local `setup_db()`/`setup_test_db()` helpers to delegate to the shared one — most were a mechanical 1-line swap; a few (`statement_entries.rs`, `retention.rs`) had extra parent-row setup preserved verbatim after the swap; `extraction/ladder.rs` needed a new `dummy_migrated_pool()` (a real-temp-file variant of its existing `dummy_pool()`, which 20+ *other* call sites in the same file still use unmodified since they don't need a migrated schema) plus fixing its two async pool-returning helpers (`setup_db_with_rule`, `setup_drift_db`) to migrate before pool construction rather than inside the pool's `interact()` closure. Two genuinely new failures surfaced from the async/sync runtime mismatch (`reconciliation::engine_tests::test_missing_data_alert_worker_creates_alert`, `extraction::ladder::tests::test_drift_detected_for_changed_hdfc_template`, both `#[tokio::test]`) — fixed by giving each an async-aware setup path instead of the sync one.

**Verified:** `cargo check` and `cargo test --lib --no-run` both clean. Full `cargo test --lib`: **313 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures** as the TASK-DB-001 baseline (`commands::data::tests::test_seed_and_fetch` — unrelated DB column drift; three `phase10_rigorous_tests` — missing fixture files) — confirmed the sqlx rewrite introduces zero regressions against the pre-existing baseline. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: 18 errors (down from the 21 documented under TASK-DB-001 — incidental improvement from removing now-unused imports across the touched test files), none in any file this task added or touched. Also directly exercised the production `init_db()` path (not just test helpers) via `db::connection_tests::test_db_encryption_unreadable_without_key` and `test_auto_vacuum_is_incremental`, both of which route through the real `sqlx`-based migration flow end-to-end.

## TASK-DB-003: Migration — Create `local_profile` Table

**Found:** The table already exists exactly as spec'd (`id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK(id=1)`, converted faithfully into `migrations/20260101000001_initial_core_schema.sql` under TASK-DB-002), with the `updated_at` trigger (migration `...000005`) and later columns (`historical_scan_months`/`llm_model`/`statement_preference`, migration `...000019`) all present. `src-tauri/src/db/local_profile.rs` already has full CRUD (`insert`/`update`/`select_by_id`) — verified every query uses `rusqlite::params!`/`?N` placeholders, zero string interpolation anywhere, matching the spec's explicit requirement. All seven doc-named acceptance tests already exist in `db/tests.rs` and pass (`test_local_profile_insert_success`, `..._insert_duplicate_pk_fails`, `..._update_success`, `..._update_not_found`, `..._select_by_id_success`, `..._select_by_id_not_found`, `..._updated_at_trigger`).

**Real deviation found:** `db::init_db()`'s default-row seed statement (`INSERT OR IGNORE INTO local_profile ...`) hardcoded `timezone = 'UTC'` and `limit_thresholds = '[]'`. Document 30 explicitly specifies defaults of `Asia/Kolkata` and `[80,90,100]`, matching Document 18 §5.3 ("All month-boundary calculations rely on IST normalization to correctly align with Indian banking cycles" — the whole product is India-focused) and §4.1's documented 80/90/100% threshold default. A brand-new install would previously get UTC timestamps and an empty (functionally disabled) spending-limit-threshold config until the user manually reconfigured Settings.

**What I did:** Corrected the seed `INSERT` to `'Asia/Kolkata'` and `'[80,90,100]'`. Did not add a schema-level `DEFAULT` clause to the already-committed migration file — editing a shipped migration is bad practice (Document 18 §13.1's forward-only philosophy) and unnecessary here, since this singleton row is only ever created through this one seed statement, not through any other insert path.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. All 7 `local_profile`-related tests in `db::tests` pass, unaffected by the seed-value fix (they each construct their own explicit `LocalProfileRow`, not relying on the seed defaults). Full `cargo test --lib`: 313 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. The default-value fix itself isn't exercised by a dedicated automated test — it only matters on the real `init_db()` first-launch path, which none of the lightweight unit tests invoke (they call `run_migrations()` directly, bypassing the seed-insert step); verified by code inspection instead, consistent with how this seed statement had no prior test coverage either.

## TASK-DB-004: Migration — Create `connected_accounts` Table

**Found:** Table already exists (migration 001), CRUD already complete in `src-tauri/src/db/connected_accounts.rs` (`insert_account`/`update_account`/`get_account`/`get_all_accounts`/`delete_account`, all parameterized). Acceptance criteria ("full CRUD unit tests including FK violation cases") already satisfied by `db/tests.rs`'s existing `test_connected_accounts_crud`, `..._update_success`, `..._update_not_found`, `..._select_not_found`, `..._fk_violation`.

**Deviation found:** Document 30 specifies `email_address (unique)`; the migrated schema had `email_address TEXT` with no uniqueness constraint at all — the same Gmail account could be "connected" twice, wasting a slot against the 10-account cap (Document 03 §8.2) and creating ambiguous duplicate-account state.

**What I did:** Added `migrations/20260101000020_connected_accounts_email_unique.sql` — a `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` (not editing the already-committed migration 001, per the same forward-only convention as TASK-DB-003). Checked existing call sites first: `ingestion/polling_tests.rs` inserts one account with `email_address: None` — safe, since SQLite treats multiple `NULL`s in a `UNIQUE` index as distinct, never colliding with each other.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. Full `cargo test --lib`: 313 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures — the new unique index doesn't break any existing test (none insert the same non-null email twice within one database instance).

## TASK-DB-007: Migration — Create `transactions_fts` FTS5 Virtual Table

**Found:** A real "wrong table" deviation, not a cosmetic one. Migration 011 (`transactionsfts_schema_correction`, already in place before this task) replaced the table Document 18 §4.3a specifies (`transactions_fts`, indexing the real `merchant_display_name`/`merchant_normalized_name`/`reference_id`/`location` columns via FTS5's external-content mechanism) with a differently-named table `transactionsfts` (no underscore) indexing three *shadow* `GENERATED ALWAYS AS VIRTUAL` columns (`merchantdisplayname`, `merchantnormalizedname`, `referenceid`) added onto `transactions` purely to feed the FTS index.

**Verified empirically before touching anything:** wrote a throwaway Python/sqlite3 probe (scratchpad, not part of the repo) creating Document 18 §4.3a's exact DDL — an FTS5 external-content table indexing the real columns directly, no shadow columns — and confirmed insert/query both work with no error. This proves the shadow-column workaround wasn't solving a real SQLite/FTS5 constraint; it was unnecessary complexity introduced without a stated reason. Per Step C, "wrong table" is one of the explicit categories to fix, not accommodate.

**What I did:** Added `migrations/20260101000023_transactions_fts_rename_to_spec.sql`: drops the three sync triggers and the `transactionsfts` table, drops the three shadow generated columns from `transactions`, creates `transactions_fts` matching Document 18 §4.3a's DDL exactly, recreates the three sync triggers (same names as before — `transactions_ai`/`ad`/`au` — now targeting the real columns), and backfills from existing rows. Checked blast radius first (`grep` across `src`/`migrations`): only `transactions.rs`'s `search_transactions()` referenced the old table name — updated its `JOIN`/`MATCH` clause to `transactions_fts`. No `src-tauri/src/db/search.rs` file exists (the doc names one); `search_transactions` already lives sensibly inside `transactions.rs` — didn't create a separate file just to match a path that has no functional bearing.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. The migration itself only gets validated at runtime (via `sqlx::migrate!`'s embedded SQL, not at compile time) — ran `test_transactions_fts_stays_synced` (the existing integration test already covering both doc-named acceptance criteria: FTS-stays-in-sync across insert/update/delete, and keyword-ranking correctness via its exact-match-ranks-first assertion) standalone first to confirm the new migration SQL is actually valid, then the full suite: 314 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures.

## TASK-DB-008: Migration — Create `transaction_observations` Table

**Found:** All columns already match Document 18 §4.4 (converted faithfully under TASK-DB-002), `UNIQUE(source_pipeline, source_record_id)` idempotency guard and `UNIQUE(fingerprint)` already present, full CRUD in `transaction_observations.rs` (insert/update/get/list/paginated/soft-delete, all parameterized). Doc-named acceptance criteria (FK violation, duplicate-source-key suppression) already covered by `db/tests.rs`'s existing test. `idx_transaction_observations_fingerprint` isn't a separate gap — `UNIQUE(fingerprint)` already creates SQLite's implicit backing index, serving the same lookup purpose.

**Gap found:** no `CHECK` constraint on `source_pipeline`, despite Document 30 requiring one and `feedback_log`'s own `source_pipeline` column already enforcing the same conceptual enum. Added via the migration-013-style create/copy/drop/rename pattern (SQLite has no `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT`), using Document 18's authoritative values (`gmail_transaction`/`statement_pdf`/`manual`) rather than Document 30's own abbreviated paraphrase ("email/statement/manual").

**Adding the constraint surfaced a real, pre-existing bug outside Area 2 — stopped and asked before touching it, per your standing instruction to pause on conflicts.** `src-tauri/src/ingestion/historical_scan.rs:303` (Area 4, Gmail ingestion) constructed every historical-scan-derived `TransactionJob` with `source_pipeline: "historical_scan"` — a value that never matched Document 18's enum, silently accepted only because no constraint existed to catch it until now. This conflated *evidence origin* (what `source_pipeline` means) with *ingestion trigger mechanism* (already tracked correctly and separately by `processing_checkpoints.job_type`). Checked for any dedup/matching logic keyed on the literal string first (none found) before asking. **You said fix it now.**

**What I did:** (1) Added `migrations/20260101000024_transaction_observations_source_pipeline_check.sql`. (2) Fixed `historical_scan.rs:303`'s production bug: `source_pipeline` is now `"gmail_transaction"` (a historical scan still reads Gmail transaction-alert messages — same evidence origin as live polling, just a different trigger). (3) Fixed the test fixture at the same file's line ~592 to match. (4) Fixed four more test-only placeholder values the new constraint surfaced, none semantically meaningful, all safe to correct: `db/tests.rs` (`'email'` → `'gmail_transaction'`), `commands/data_tests.rs` (`'test'` ×3 → `'gmail_transaction'`), `phase8_telemetry_tests.rs` (`'email'` ×2 → `'gmail_transaction'`), and ~15 occurrences of `'gmail'` across `reconciliation/engine_tests.rs` (its own shared fixture function plus several inline structs/SQL strings) — confirmed none of these tests ever assert on the literal placeholder value itself, only using it to satisfy the `NOT NULL`-adjacent insert shape.

**Process note for future migration-adding tasks:** hit a transient build-caching quirk while diagnosing this — `sqlx::migrate!`'s directory scan didn't pick up a newly-added migration file on the very first `cargo test` after adding it (confirmed via a throwaway schema-dump diagnostic, deleted after use), silently running against the prior migration set instead of erroring. A full `cargo clean`/incremental-cache wipe reliably resolved it. Worth a `rm -rf target/debug/incremental` (or full `cargo clean`) as a sanity check any time a just-added migration doesn't seem to have taken effect, rather than assuming the SQL itself is wrong.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. Full `cargo test --lib` after a clean rebuild (to rule out the caching quirk above): 314 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures — zero regressions from either the constraint or the `historical_scan.rs` production fix.

## TASK-DB-009: Migration — Create `statements` Table

**Found:** Table (migration 001, `file_hash` added migration 009), CRUD in `statements.rs` largely present. Acceptance criteria only partially covered — the single combined `test_statements_crud` exercised insert/update/paginated/FK-violation/delete-success, but not insert-duplicate-PK, update-not-found, select-not-found, delete-not-found, or paginated-empty.

**Real gaps found:** (1) No `source_type` column anywhere — Document 18 §4.7 requires it (`gmail_email`/`manual_upload`) specifically so the UI can filter by entry point rather than inferring it from `source_message_id` nullability, which the doc explicitly says not to rely on. (2) No `CHECK` on `parse_status`, and Document 30's own text ("pending/parsed/failed/duplicate") doesn't even match Document 18 §4.7's authoritative values (`queued`/`processing`/`parsed`/`failed`) — checked every real usage first (production code only ever writes `'parsed'`; two test fixtures used `'success'`, fixed to `'parsed'` before adding the constraint, learning from the TASK-DB-008 near-miss). (3) Missing the `(billing_period_start, billing_period_end, instrument_id)` index Document 18 §6 requires for duplicate-cycle detection (TASK-STMT-002). (4) `update()` and `soft_delete()` never checked the affected-row count, so a "not found" case was silently indistinguishable from success — contradicting the doc's own acceptance criteria, which explicitly wants both tested.

**What I did:** Added `migrations/20260101000025_statements_source_type_and_checks.sql` (table recreate: adds `source_type` with its own CHECK, adds the `parse_status` CHECK, preserves `file_hash`) plus the index. Updated `StatementsRow`/`insert`/`update`/`row_to_statement` for `source_type` and `file_hash` (updated 4 struct-literal call sites accordingly). Fixed `update()`/`soft_delete()` to return `Err` on a zero-row match. Added the five missing tests.

**Flagged, not touched:** production code (`statements/duplicate_check.rs`, `statements/metadata_extractor.rs`) writes `statements` rows via raw SQL, bypassing this DAO entirely, and doesn't set `source_type` — retrofitting that is Area 5's (`TASK-STMT-001`) job, not this schema task's; the new column is nullable so this doesn't break anything now.

**Verified:** `cargo check` clean. New tests pass standalone and in the full suite: 319 passed (314 + 5 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures.

## TASK-DB-012: Migration — Create `reconciliation_clusters` + `reconciliation_cluster_members` Tables

**Found:** Three-way schema conflict, flagged to you before touching anything. The existing implementation (`db/reconciliation_clusters.rs`, `db/reconciliation_cluster_members.rs`, `reconciliation/cluster.rs`) was internally coherent and had working tests, but its shape matched neither Document 18 §4.6/§4.6a (authoritative for local schema per Document 49 §6) nor Document 30's own TASK-DB-012 text (which itself differs from Document 18): `reconciliation_clusters` stored the triggering `observation_id` directly on the cluster row and had a 7-value `status` enum of which only 2 values were ever actually written; `reconciliation_cluster_members` only recorded competing candidate transactions (via `transaction_id`), never the incoming observation itself, and had no `member_role` discriminator. You chose "Rewrite to match Document 18."

**What I did:** New migration `20260101000028_reconciliation_clusters_rewrite_to_spec.sql` recreates both tables to Document 18's exact shape: `reconciliation_clusters(id, cluster_status CHECK(open/resolved/deferred/rejected), reason, resolution_notes, created_at, resolved_at)`; `reconciliation_cluster_members(id, cluster_id ON DELETE CASCADE, observation_id, canonical_transaction_id, member_role CHECK(incoming/candidate_a/candidate_b/candidate_other), added_at)` plus its index. Data from the old `status` enum is migrated best-effort (`ambiguous_pending`→`open`, `resolved`→`resolved`, everything else→`open`); no production databases exist yet so this is precautionary, not load-bearing.

Rewrote `reconciliation/cluster.rs`: `create_ambiguity_cluster()` now inserts the incoming observation as its own member row (`member_role='incoming'`) alongside the competing candidates (`member_role` assigned `candidate_a`/`candidate_b`/`candidate_other` in order) — previously the observation was never a member row at all, only referenced via the now-removed `cluster.observation_id` column. `resolve_cluster()` now maps its four actions onto Document 18's real 4-state enum instead of a flattened always-`resolved`/status-untouched model: `confirm_match`/`keep_separate` → `cluster_status='resolved'`, `reject_candidate` → `cluster_status='rejected'` (previously indistinguishable from a real match resolution), `mark_unresolved` → `cluster_status='deferred'` (previously left whatever status was already there untouched, relying on it having never left `'ambiguous_pending'` in the first place).

**Necessary follow-on change, not scope creep:** the hard invariant Document 30 names for this task — "clusters in `ambiguous_pending`/`open` status must never appear in analytics totals" — cannot be expressed correctly under the old schema, which excluded a transaction from analytics if it was a cluster member *at all*, regardless of resolution state (i.e., a transaction whose ambiguity was already resolved would incorrectly stay excluded forever). Since this task rewrites the status model that the invariant depends on, I corrected `db/transactions.rs`'s `get_global_spend_current_month`/`get_category_spend_current_month` exclusion subqueries to join through `reconciliation_clusters` and scope the exclusion to `cluster_status = 'open'` only. Also updated `commands/data.rs`'s `do_fetch_unresolved_clusters` (renamed columns; the "unresolved" list now correctly means `cluster_status IN ('open', 'deferred')` rather than `status != 'resolved'`, since `deferred` clusters still need attention and `rejected`/`resolved` are both terminal) and `reconciliation/alert_worker.rs`'s missing-data-alert query (joins through the new member row to find the `incoming` observation instead of a direct `observation_id` column).

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Rewrote all dependent tests to the new schema: `db/tests.rs` (`test_other_tables_insert`, `test_reconciliation_clusters_crud`, `test_fk_cascade_rules`, and `test_cluster_excluded_from_analytics_when_pending` — the last one upgraded from a synthetic same-table `SUM...WHERE status IN (...)` query that didn't actually exercise any production code, into a real end-to-end test of the hard invariant via `get_global_spend_current_month`, including the resolved-cluster-rejoins-totals case Document 18's 4-state model newly makes possible to express), `reconciliation/engine_tests.rs` (5 tests: dashboard-totals exclusion, resolution merge/branch/reject/mark-unresolved/rejects-unknown-action, plus the raw-SQL missing-data-alert query), `commands/data.rs`'s `test_seed_and_fetch` fixture tables. One transcription bug caught by the test run itself: `reconciliation_cluster_members.added_at` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` per Document 18, but the Rust `insert()` bound `None` as a literal SQL `NULL` when the caller didn't supply a timestamp — fixed via `COALESCE(?, CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)` in the insert statement. Full `cargo test --lib`: 319 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in any file this task touched.

**Follow-up correction (same task):** Document 18 §6's indexing table lists `reconciliation_clusters: (cluster_status)` — missed in the migration above, which only added the `reconciliation_cluster_members(cluster_id)` index named explicitly in §4.6a's DDL. Added `migrations/20260101000029_reconciliation_clusters_status_index.sql`. Full `cargo test --lib`: 319 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures.

## TASK-DB-013: Migration — Create `merchants` and `merchant_aliases` Tables + Seed Data

**Found:** Both tables already existed (migration 003) with working CRUD (`db/merchants.rs`) and a real consumer (`reconciliation/post_processing.rs`'s merchant-based category/entity resolution), but the shape deviated from Document 18 §4.10/§4.11: `merchants` had `category_id`/`logo_url`/`website` (none specified by Document 18 — which, like TASK-DB-012's cluster tables, explicitly documents that a similar field, `approval_status`, was deliberately removed as dead code) but was missing `normalized_name`/`source`; `merchant_aliases` used a single `alias` column and a `merchant_id` FK instead of the `alias_raw`/`alias_normalized` split and the `merchant_entity_id` naming Document 18 uses consistently everywhere else merchants is referenced (`transactions.merchant_entity_id`, `recurring_payments.merchant_entity_id`). Checked every real usage first: `MerchantsRow`/`MerchantAliasesRow` are only referenced from `db/merchants.rs` and `db/tests.rs` — no frontend or cross-module consumer of the Rust types — so the rewrite itself was low-risk.

**What I did:** New migration `20260101000030_merchants_rewrite_to_spec.sql` drops and recreates both tables to Document 18's exact shape (no production databases exist yet, and migration 003's seed rows were the only rows either table has ever held, so a clean drop/reseed was simpler and safer than a carry-forward copy). Rewrote `db/merchants.rs`'s `MerchantsRow`/`MerchantAliasesRow` and every CRUD function to match. Kept the seed data itself as plain SQL `INSERT`s bundled in the migration (consistent with every other seed migration in this codebase — categories, instruments, the original merchants seed) rather than Document 30's suggested `assets/merchant_seed.json` file loaded at runtime; `sqlx::migrate!` only runs raw `.sql` files, so a JSON-seed mechanism would need a second, inconsistent code path outside the migration system for no functional benefit at this stage — flagged here rather than silently ignored.

**Real bug caught by removing `merchants.category_id`:** `reconciliation/post_processing.rs`'s merchant resolution query joined `merchant_aliases`/`merchants` and read `m.category_id` to auto-assign a transaction's category from its matched merchant — a real, working code path, not a stray reference. Document 18 doesn't give `merchants` a category column at all (category assignment is documented, per Document 30's TASK-API area, as living on `transactions` directly, set by the user or via `feedback_log`-driven learning), and there's no seed data that ever populated a merchant's category either — this looks like an undocumented MVP shortcut, not a spec'd feature. Rather than leave a query referencing a dropped column (which would silently stop matching at runtime — the code path is defensively wrapped in `if let Ok(...)`, so it wouldn't panic, just silently stop resolving categories from merchants), I updated the query to drop `category_id` from its `SELECT`/join entirely; merchant entity/name resolution is unaffected, and category assignment now falls through fully to the existing keyword-heuristic fallback that was already there as a secondary path (`uber`/`lyft`/`transit` → transportation, etc.) — verified via `test_category_assigned_from_merchant_entity`, which still passes because it happens to test an aliased merchant name that also matches a heuristic keyword.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Fixed 2 raw-SQL test fixtures in `reconciliation/engine_tests.rs` (`test_merchant_spike_alert`, `test_anomaly_detection_logic`) that inserted `merchants`/`merchant_aliases` rows using the old column names, and `db/tests.rs`'s two `MerchantsRow`/`MerchantAliasesRow` construction sites. One transcription bug caught by the test run itself: the migration first copied old rows forward by `id` into the new table shape, then separately re-inserted the same seed rows under the same `id`s — collided on both the primary key and the new `normalized_name` UNIQUE constraint, breaking the DB init path for the entire test suite (137 failures). Fixed by dropping the carry-forward copy in favor of a single clean reseed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 319 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in any touched file.

## TASK-DB-014: Migration — Create `categories` Table + Seed Standard Categories

**Found:** Table (migration 002) + full CRUD + a working system-category-protection check (`update`/`soft_delete` both reject renaming/reparenting/deleting a system category) + seed data (11 root categories, several subcategories) already existed and were already tested (`categories_tests.rs`: CRUD, seeding, protection, hierarchy traversal — all 4 of Document 30's named acceptance areas already covered). But the shape deviated from Document 18 §4.9: `color`/`icon`/`is_system` (boolean) + `updated_at` existed but aren't in Document 18's field list, while `source_type` (a `system`/`user`/`mcc_mapped` 3-value enum — a real distinction the `is_system` boolean can't express, since MCC-derived categories are neither hand-curated system categories nor user-created ones), `mcc_code`, and `monthly_budget_minor` (per-category spend limits) were missing entirely, with nowhere to live under the old schema. Checked every real usage first: `CategoriesRow`/`color`/`icon`/`is_system` are only referenced from `db/categories.rs` and its own two test files — no frontend consumer exists yet (Area 9 UI not reached) — so the rewrite was low-risk.

**What I did:** New migration `20260101000031_categories_rewrite_to_spec.sql` rewrites the table to Document 18's exact shape, carrying the existing seed hierarchy forward (unlike TASK-DB-013's merchants, which had no real data dependency forcing a carry-forward). Rewrote `db/categories.rs`'s `CategoriesRow` and CRUD, translating the boolean protection check (`is_system == true`) to the enum equivalent (`source_type == "system"`) — same protection scope as before (only genuine system categories are protected; `mcc_mapped` ones aren't, matching Document 30's acceptance criteria which only ever names `is_system = 1` rows, not a broader "non-user" rule).

**Real SQLite gotcha hit and fixed:** `categories` is self-referencing (`parent_id → categories.id`). The standard create-new/copy/drop-old/rename pattern used for every other table rewrite this session doesn't work here: populating the new table while the old table is still the live FK target means `DROP TABLE categories`'s implicit delete step orphans the new table's freshly-inserted rows, and SQLite raises `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` — this broke DB init for the entire test suite (137 failures) the first time I ran it. Fixed by copying through a plain `CREATE TEMP TABLE ... AS SELECT` first (a table with no FK constraint of its own is never treated as a live FK child), then dropping the real table, creating the new self-referencing one, and inserting root categories before their children (`ORDER BY CASE WHEN parent_id IS NULL THEN 0 ELSE 1 END`) so no row's `parent_id` is ever inserted ahead of the parent it points to.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Rewrote `categories_tests.rs`'s 4 tests (dropped the `color`/`icon` fields from construction sites; the "should be allowed" mutation in `test_system_category_protection` now sets `monthly_budget_minor` instead of `color`, since `color` no longer exists but the test's actual point — that non-name/parent_id fields remain editable on a system category — is unchanged) and `db/tests.rs`'s one `CategoriesRow` construction site. Full `cargo test --lib`: 319 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in any touched file.

## TASK-DB-018: Migration — Create `recurring_payments`, `sessions`, `audit_log`, `license_state`

**Found:** All four tables (migration 008, `billing_interval_cached` added later in migration 017) already matched Document 18 §4.14/§4.20/§4.21/§4.22 field-for-field, including the immutability trigger and its passing `test_audit_log_no_update_path` test. `recurring_payments` has one extra column beyond Document 18's field list, `updated_at` — unlike the untested cosmetic columns removed in TASK-DB-013/014, this one has a real, passing test (`test_recurring_payments_crud`) that sleeps 1 second and asserts the trigger-driven timestamp actually advances — left as-is; removing it would be pure churn against working, tested behavior for a documentation-completeness technicality.

**Gaps found:** (1) 5 missing indexes across the three simpler tables per Document 18 §6. (2) `license_state.billing_interval_cached` (added in migration 017) never got the `CHECK(monthly/annual)` Document 18's DDL specifies. (3) The real gap: `audit_log` is entirely missing Document 18 §4.21's tamper-evidence hash chain (`prev_hash`/`row_hash`) — a security feature ("Aditya's decision resolving Documentation Audit findings D-02/E-04") that Document 30's own task text doesn't even mention, but Document 18 describes as load-bearing: the existing immutability trigger only blocks mutation through the app's own IPC/rusqlite path, not an actor with direct SQLite file access, which the hash chain is specifically designed to catch.

**What I did:** `migrations/20260101000035_db018_indexes_and_audit_log_hash_chain.sql` adds the 5 indexes, adds the `billing_interval_cached` CHECK via table recreate (no self-reference issue here, unlike TASK-DB-014's categories), and adds `audit_log.prev_hash`/`row_hash` columns. Implemented the chain in `db/audit_log.rs`: `insert()` now looks up the previous row's `row_hash` (`ORDER BY rowid DESC LIMIT 1`, genesis `'0'.repeat(64)` for the first row) and computes `row_hash = SHA256(prev_hash || id || actor_type || actor_id || action || resource_type || resource_id || before_json || after_json || created_at)` per Document 18's exact formula, always computed fresh server-side — `AuditLogRow` itself gained no new fields, so every existing call site (7 files construct it) needed zero changes. Added `verify_chain(conn) -> Result<bool>`, walking the table in `rowid` order and recomputing/comparing each row's hash — this is the "verification pass" Document 18 describes as detecting tampering "even by an actor with direct SQLite file access."

**Real bug caught by the test itself:** the first version hashed `created_at.to_rfc3339()` (full nanosecond precision) at insert time, but SQLite's `DATETIME` column truncates to whole-second precision on storage — recomputing the hash after any read-back could never match, since the round-tripped `created_at` had lost precision the original hash was computed over. Fixed by hashing the same truncated `"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"` representation that's actually persisted, at both insert and verify time.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `test_audit_log_hash_chain_intact_across_multiple_rows` and `test_audit_log_hash_chain_detects_tampering` (the latter drops the immutability trigger mid-test to isolate `verify_chain`'s detection logic from how the tampering happened, then asserts the mutated row is caught). Full `cargo test --lib`: 321 passed (319 + 2 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in any touched file.

---

## TASK-DB-019/020/021/022: Integrity Check, Backup, Hardware Migration, Query Scoping

Grouping these four in one entry (and one commit) because they turned out to be genuinely intertwined: DB-019's and DB-020's daily-cadence steps live in the same background loop in `lib.rs`, and DB-021's toast wiring sits right next to DB-020's backup-completion wiring in the same `init_db` call site.

**TASK-DB-019 (Database Integrity Check and Retention Background Task):** Found steps 2 (raw-JSON retention) and 5 (5-year archival) already fully implemented in `db/retention.rs` (`sweep_raw_payloads`, `archive_old_transactions`) and already wired into a daily loop in `lib.rs` — deliberately additive-only archival, already tested. Steps 1/3/4 (integrity check, incremental vacuum, size warning) didn't exist at all. Added `db/maintenance.rs`: `run_integrity_check`/`check_integrity_and_report` (emits `db_corrupted` + writes `audit_log` on failure), `run_incremental_vacuum`, `check_db_size_warning` (emits `db_size_warning` past 2GB) — wired into the same daily loop, after the existing backup/retention/archive steps.

**TASK-DB-020 (Automated Daily Encrypted SQLite Backup):** The backup mechanism itself (`VACUUM INTO` a temp file, atomic rename, single rolling `finance.db.daily.bak`) already existed and already deliberately follows Document 28 §4.2/§4.8's "single rolling file" model over Document 30's own task text ("retaining the last 7 daily backups") — a documented prior decision (comments cite "J3 fix"), consistent with this session's established precedence rule that a domain-specific document (here, the Data Retention & Deletion Policy) outranks Document 30's task-plan paraphrase. Not re-litigated. Missing piece: completion wasn't logged to `audit_log` (`db_backup_completed`) or emitted as an event for Settings to show the last-backup timestamp — added both, on the same success path as the existing rename.

**TASK-DB-021 (Hardware UUID Migration):** The actual rekey mechanism (`db::crypto::try_migrate_hardware_uuid`) already existed, already wired into `init_db`'s key-mismatch handling, and already had a passing round-trip test. One real behavioral gap: no "Database migrated to new Mac" toast on success (Document 30's explicit requirement). Added `hw_uuid_marker_indicates_migration()` — peeks at the marker file before `init_db` runs (without deriving any keys) so `lib.rs` can emit a `db_hardware_migrated` event after a successful open, without threading a migration flag through `init_db`'s return type. **Flagged, not implemented:** the marker is a plaintext file, not Keychain-stored as Document 30 describes ("same access-control profile as the base key") — an existing, reasoned deviation (the code's own comment: IOPlatformUUID is already locally readable via `ioreg` by any process, so plaintext storage here doesn't weaken anything) that predates this task; not re-litigated given it doesn't compromise the actual security property. **Flagged, deferred:** "require license re-activation" (license_deactivate/license_activate) needs real Area 3 licensing-backend calls this session hasn't reached — genuinely out of scope for the DB layer.

**TASK-DB-022 (Application-Level Query Scoping):** Found a real, live instance of the exact anti-pattern this task exists to eliminate: `#[tauri::command] auth_google_start(profile_id: i64, ...)` accepted `profile_id` directly from the frontend/webview and passed it straight through to a `connected_accounts` row write. Since `local_profile.id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK(id = 1)` (TASK-DB-003) means exactly one profile can ever exist, the FK from `connected_accounts.profile_id` already made a forged non-1 value fail with a cryptic FK error rather than corrupt anything — but the parameter's mere existence on the IPC boundary contradicts Document 22 §13.1's "never accepted as a caller-supplied parameter." Added `db/scoping.rs` (`LOCAL_PROFILE_ID: i64 = 1`); removed `profile_id` from `auth_google_start`'s signature entirely — it now resolves the constant internally. Confirmed the frontend never needed a real dynamic value here (`src/lib/ipc.ts` already always passed the hardcoded `{ profileId: 1 }`) and updated that call site to match.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added tests: `db/maintenance.rs` (integrity check on a fresh DB, size-threshold boundary, vacuum no-op), `db::crypto::hardware_migration_tests::hw_uuid_marker_migration_detection`, `db::scoping::tests` (the `test_user_data_isolation`-equivalent pair: the constant matches TASK-DB-003's single allowed row, and the DB layer itself rejects a forged `profile_id` insert). Full `cargo test --lib`: 327 passed (319 + 8 new across these four tasks), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings (the one pre-existing "too many arguments" hit shifted line number only, from unrelated lines added earlier in the same file).

## TASK-DB-023: Implement Transaction Bounding for Background Writes (Prevent SQLITE_BUSY)

**Found:** Nothing existed — no `BatchWriter`, no bounded-transaction pattern anywhere in the ingestion/statement background tasks.

**What I did:** New `db/batch_writer.rs`. `BatchWriter` queues writes (each a boxed `Fn(&Connection) -> rusqlite::Result<()>`) and reports itself full once either bound is hit — `MAX_BATCH_ROWS = 20` or `MAX_BATCH_DURATION = 500ms` (Document 30's "max ~20 rows... max ~500ms"). `flush()` runs the whole queued batch inside one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE ... COMMIT` (via `rusqlite::Connection::transaction_with_behavior(Immediate)`), retrying the *entire* batch on `SQLITE_BUSY` with exponential backoff (50ms, 100ms, 200ms — `MAX_BUSY_RETRIES = 3`) before surfacing the error; any other error rolls back and returns immediately without retrying, since retrying a real constraint violation would just fail identically three more times. Deliberately synchronous (no `async`/`.await` anywhere in the type) so it's safe to call only from inside a `deadpool_sqlite::Connection::interact(...)` closure — the doc's "never holds a write transaction open across an `await` that could block on network I/O" is enforced by construction: there's no `await` in this file for a transaction to span.

**Not wired into consumers yet, flagged as deferred:** Document 30 names 3 call sites (historical scan, poll worker, statement parser) as `BatchWriter` users, but this task's own `Files:` line names only `batch_writer.rs`, and its acceptance criteria explicitly defer validation to TASK-QA-002's (not-yet-built) performance harness. Retrofitting the three consumers is a cross-cutting change into Area 4/6/7 code not yet reached in the execution order — left for whichever task owns each of those call sites to adopt, same treatment as TASK-DB-019's archival query-side integration (deferred to Area 9).

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. 5 new tests: batch-full reporting at the row limit, no-op below the limit, a 3-write batch committing atomically, an empty flush being a true no-op, and a duplicate-primary-key write rolling back the *entire* batch (not just the failing row) rather than partially committing. Full `cargo test --lib`: 332 passed (327 + 5 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in the new file.

# Area 3 — Authentication, Sessions, Tenant Isolation, Licensing Gate

## TASK-AUTH-001: Implement Google OAuth PKCE Flow via Tauri + System Browser

**Found:** A substantial, largely-correct pre-existing implementation in `src-tauri/src/ingestion/oauth.rs` (Document 30 names `src-tauri/src/auth/google_oauth.rs` — an organizational difference only, not touched, same class of decision as `db/batch_writer.rs`'s path earlier this session). PKCE S256 via the `oauth2` crate (`PkceCodeChallenge::new_random_sha256`), system browser via `tauri_plugin_opener::open_url` (never an embedded webview), a loopback listener on an OS-assigned ephemeral port (not a hardcoded one — an explicit prior fix, "H9 fix"), strict state-parameter validation (`validate_oauth_callback` rejects a state mismatch), code+verifier exchange via `reqwest`, tokens stored exclusively in Keychain (`keyring::Entry`, never in `ConnectedAccountsRow`), and a `connected_accounts` row write with a `None` baseline `last_history_id`. Both named acceptance tests (`test_oauth_callback_payload_validation`, `test_token_stored_in_keychain_not_sqlite`) already existed and passed.

**Gap found:** the "if the user closes the browser without completing consent, the loopback listener times out after 5 minutes with `AppError::Auth("oauth_timeout")`" requirement had no implementing code at all — the callback wait (`rx.await??` against a `tokio::oneshot` fed by a thread blocked in `server.incoming_requests()`) had no bound whatsoever; an abandoned browser tab would hang the connect flow (and leak the blocked thread) forever.

**What I did:** Extracted the wait logic into `wait_for_oauth_callback(server, expected_state, timeout) -> Result<String>`, using `tiny_http::Server::recv_timeout` (which bounds the blocking wait itself, so the thread terminates on its own rather than needing an external unblock signal) in a loop against a deadline. Times out with an error whose message is exactly `"oauth_timeout"` — the existing IPC boundary (`commands::auth_google_start`) already does `.map_err(|e| AppError::Auth(e.to_string()))`, so this produces `AppError::Auth("oauth_timeout")` exactly as specified with no changes needed there. Real timeout is 5 minutes (`OAUTH_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT`); the extracted function takes the duration as a parameter so the test below can use a short one instead of actually waiting 5 minutes.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `oauth_callback_wait_times_out_when_browser_never_completes` (a 200ms injected timeout, no request ever sent — simulates an abandoned browser). Full `cargo test --lib`: 333 passed (332 + 1 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in the touched file.

**Noted for later tasks, not touched here (out of TASK-AUTH-001's scope):** this same file already substantially implements pieces of TASK-AUTH-003 (`record_consent_event` call on successful connect), TASK-AUTH-004 (`get_valid_access_token`'s refresh + degradation handling, `mark_account_degraded_async`), and TASK-AUTH-006 (`auth_google_disconnect` — though it doesn't yet call Google's revoke endpoint per-account, only `revoke_gmail_access`'s full-wipe path does; naming/status-value differences from Document 30's exact text also need checking against those tasks' own authoritative sections when reached).
