## TASK-AUTH-002/003: Consent Disclosure Screen + Dedicated `consent_events` Table

Grouping these two: fixing AUTH-002's disclosure content properly required deciding where consent gets recorded, which is exactly AUTH-003's scope.

**TASK-AUTH-002 (Explicit Consent Disclosure Screen):** Found a substantial existing implementation — `src/pages/Onboarding.tsx` step 3 already renders a must-complete (not bypassable — gated behind steps 1/2, and OAuth only triggers from this screen's button) disclosure with the exact Gmail scope, a beta-program warning, and the full outbound-channels list (`OUTBOUND_CHANNEL_DISCLOSURE`). Document 30 names a dedicated `GmailConsentScreen.tsx` file — an organizational difference only, not touched (the existing 3-step wizard structure works and splitting it out is pure churn). Two real content gaps: (1) the Licensing Backend disclosure line didn't explicitly state it *never* receives financial data (only listed what it does receive) — Document 30 requires the explicit negative assurance; added it. (2) the CTA button read "Connect & Finish" rather than Document 30's exact quoted acknowledgment text "I Understand, Continue to Google" — renamed (and updated `e2e/Onboarding.spec.ts`'s locator to match).

**TASK-AUTH-003 (Consent Event Recording Table and Write Path):** This resolves a conflict flagged but deliberately left open at TASK-DB-009: Document 18 §4.21a specifies a dedicated `consent_events` table, separate from `audit_log`; the existing code instead recorded consent as `audit_log` rows with `resource_type = 'consent'`, with a comment claiming Document 18 said otherwise (it didn't — that comment was simply wrong, or referred to an earlier doc revision). Document 19 §5.6 independently confirms the same design (`auth_get_consent_history` reads `consent_events`), removing any ambiguity.

**What I did:** New migration `20260101000036_create_consent_events.sql` (`id`, `event_type`, `disclosure_text`, `consented_at`, `withdrawn_at`, matching Document 18 §4.21a exactly) and a new `src-tauri/src/auth/consent.rs` module (`insert_consent_event`, `withdraw_consent_event`, `fetch_consent_history`, `get_by_id`) — the first module under `src-tauri/src/auth/`, matching Document 30's file organization for real this time since there was no pre-existing equivalent home for it. Migrated all 4 real call sites from `db::audit_log::record_consent_event`/`fetch_consent_history` (now dead code, deleted) to the new module: `ingestion/oauth.rs`'s internal Gmail-connect recording, `diagnostics.rs`'s bundle-export recording, and `commands/data.rs`'s `settings_export_data` recording and the public `record_consent_event`/`fetch_consent_history` IPC commands (the latter renamed to `auth_get_consent_history`, matching Document 19 §5.6's exact naming). Checked the real UI consumer (`Settings.tsx`'s Consent History panel, which explicitly promises "Gmail authorization, onboarding disclosures, and diagnostic bundle exports") before deciding scope — this confirmed all 4 existing consent-ish event types (not just `gmail_oauth_consent`) needed to move together, or the panel would silently stop showing 2 of the 3 categories it advertises.

**Real behavioral fix, not just a rename:** removed the backend's own internal consent-recording inside `start_oauth_flow_async` (fired only *after* a successful token exchange) in favor of relying solely on the frontend's pre-flight recording (which already fires at the correct moment — "on consent-screen acknowledgment," per Document 30's exact phrasing — before the OAuth round-trip even starts). The backend version was both redundant and recorded at the wrong moment relative to spec. Renamed the frontend's Gmail-path event from the placeholder `onboarding_disclosure` to Document 30's exact `gmail_oauth_consent`, with `disclosure_text` now the actual verbatim disclosure copy (assembled from the same constants the screen renders, not a narrative description of what the user did) — satisfying "the exact verbatim text shown" literally instead of approximately.

**Real SQLite gotcha avoided (learned from TASK-DB-018):** `withdraw_consent_event`'s "most recent unwithdrawn row" lookup uses `ORDER BY rowid DESC`, not `consented_at DESC` — `DATETIME` storage's whole-second truncation would make same-second rows indistinguishable by timestamp alone.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Updated `Settings.tsx`'s Consent History rendering and `src/lib/ipc.ts`'s `ConsentEventRecord` type to the new field shape; fixed a stale command-name reference in `e2e/fixtures/tauriMock.ts`. 4 new Rust tests (`auth/consent.rs`): insert/fetch round-trip, withdraw-without-delete, no-op withdrawal with no matching event, and withdrawal only affecting the most recent row. Full `cargo test --lib`: 337 passed (333 + 4 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-004: Implement Token Refresh and Degradation Handling

**Found:** `get_valid_access_token`/`mark_account_degraded_async` in `ingestion/oauth.rs` already implement a solid *proactive* refresh (check `expires_at` before use, refresh if within 60s of expiry) and degradation handling (bad refresh → `account_status = 'degraded'` + `system_warning` event + `audit_log` entry, with a smart purge-vs-keep decision for the Keychain entry based on whether the failure reason indicates the credential itself is dead). `polling.rs`'s history-loop already retries `429`/5xx with exponential backoff and already handles `HISTORY_ID_TOO_OLD` via `handle_invalid_history_id`.

**Gaps found:** (1) Document 30's model is *reactive* — "wrap every outgoing Gmail API call in a retry-with-refresh interceptor: on HTTP 401 ... retry once" — which the proactive-only design doesn't cover: a token that looks unexpired by local clock math but was revoked externally (or invalidated by clock skew) would just fail outright on the actual API call, with no retry path at all. (2) `handle_invalid_history_id` only logged via `tracing::warn!`, never wrote the `history_checkpoint_reset` `audit_log` entry Document 30 explicitly names. (3) The refresh-failure path already logged and persisted the *raw* OAuth error text (via `tracing::error!("Failed to refresh token: {}", e)` and into `audit_log.after_json.reason`) — a direct violation of "Never expose the raw refresh error (may contain token fragments) to the React layer or logs," which is explicitly called out in this task's own spec text, not a minor style nit.

**What I did:** Extracted `force_refresh_access_token` (bypasses the cached-expiry check, always attempts a real refresh-token exchange) from the shared `refresh_token_store` helper `get_valid_access_token` already used internally. Added HTTP 401 handling to `polling.rs`'s history-loop retry: on 401, forces a refresh via the new function, rebuilds `GmailClient` with the new token, and retries the request exactly once (a second 401 surfaces as a hard error rather than looping) — the single largest, most-frequently-hit Gmail call site. Given this task's own effort rating is **S**, did not extend the same wrapper into `GmailClient`'s other methods (`fetch_message`/`search_messages`/`fetch_attachment`/`get_profile`) or `historical_scan.rs`'s separate call sites — that would be a materially larger refactor than an S-effort task implies; flagged below as a scoped, deliberate partial implementation rather than silently leaving it incomplete. Added the missing `history_checkpoint_reset` `audit_log` write to `handle_invalid_history_id`. Added `classify_refresh_error()`, reducing a refresh error's full `Display` text (inspected once, in memory, never itself logged/stored) to one of a fixed set of safe category strings (`invalid_grant`/`invalid_client`/`unauthorized`/`network_error`/`unknown_error`) — this is now the *only* representation of a refresh failure that ever reaches a log line, an `audit_log` row, or a caller-visible error; the existing `reason_indicates_invalid_token` purge-decision logic still works unchanged since its substring checks match the category strings too.

**Flagged, not fully implemented:** the reactive 401-retry wrapper only covers `polling.rs`'s history-loop, not every Gmail API call site as Document 30's text literally describes (`GmailClient`'s individual methods, and `historical_scan.rs`'s calls, still rely solely on the proactive pre-check). Extending it fully would touch `gmail_client.rs`'s architecture (which holds a fixed `access_token: String` per instance, not a refreshable reference) — a larger change than this task's S rating supports; left for whichever task in Area 4 (Gmail Ingestion) next touches that file's design.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `classify_refresh_error_never_echoes_raw_text` (5 cases: each real category, plus a raw-text input containing something secret-looking, confirming only the fixed category string — never the input — is ever returned). Full `cargo test --lib`: 338 passed (337 + 1 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-005: Implement Local Session State Management

**Found:** Only `db/sessions.rs`'s bare CRUD existed (`insert`/`get`/`revoke`) — no session-establishment logic, no Tauri-managed in-memory state, no `auth_logout` command. Genuinely nothing to verify against; implemented from scratch.

**What I did:** New `src-tauri/src/auth/session.rs`: `SessionState(Mutex<Option<String>>)` (Tauri-managed, registered via `app.manage(...)`), `compute_device_fingerprint` (`SHA-256(IOPlatformUUID + bundle_id)` exactly per Document 30, reusing `db::crypto::get_hardware_uuid` — deliberately *not* reusing `licensing::device::get_device_id`, which is a different UUID-v5 derivation serving a different purpose, license-device binding, not session tracking), `ensure_active_session` (idempotent: reuses any existing non-revoked session row, else creates one), `logout` (sets `revoked_at`, never deletes, clears in-memory state), and `current_session_id` (internal-only accessor — the session id is never returned to the frontend, satisfying "never sent to React as a raw string" by construction: no command exposes it). Wired `ensure_active_session` into `lib.rs`'s startup (spawned, best-effort — a session-establishment hiccup must not block app launch) and added the `auth_logout` IPC command (Document 19's catalog doesn't name this command at all — an omission, not a conflict, so used Document 30's exact name with nothing to reconcile against).

**Not implemented here, correctly deferred:** "requires re-auth before any Gmail/licensing IPC command executes" is TASK-AUTH-008's explicit job ("Enforce Tenant Isolation Pattern Across All IPC Handlers," which depends on this task) — AUTH-005 only had to provide the session primitive, not wire blanket enforcement across every command itself.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. 4 new tests: fingerprint determinism/hw-uuid-specificity, session row creation, revoke-without-delete, and `SessionState`'s default/set/clear lifecycle. Full `cargo test --lib`: 342 passed (338 + 4 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-006: Implement Gmail Disconnect (Revoke Access) Flow

**Found:** `auth_google_disconnect` (already correctly named per Doc 19 §5.3 — not in conflict with Document 30, which just doesn't list this command at all) already deleted the Keychain entry and updated the account row, per-account rather than a blanket update (correctly preserving Doc 03 §8.2's multi-account model). A separate `revoke_gmail_access` (the "Reset App Data" full-wipe path) already called Google's revoke endpoint, but only for the all-accounts wipe case — the single-account disconnect never called it at all.

**Gaps found:** (1) no Google revoke-endpoint call on single-account disconnect. (2) `account_status` was set to `'INACTIVE'` rather than Document 30's `'disconnected'` — checked every read site first (only 3 in the whole codebase, none reference `'INACTIVE'` specifically, all check for `'ACTIVE'`/`'degraded'`), zero-risk rename. (3) the audit_log action was `'disconnect_gmail'` rather than Document 30's `'gmail_revoked'`. (4) didn't withdraw the `gmail_oauth_consent` consent event at all (TASK-AUTH-003's `consent_events` table didn't exist until this session — now it does, and its `withdraw_consent_event` function was built specifically for this).

**Not implemented, judged unnecessary:** "signal the account's poll-worker CancellationToken to stop" — the actual polling architecture is a single shared loop that re-queries `connected_accounts WHERE account_status = 'ACTIVE'` every cycle (not one spawned task per account with its own token), so flipping `account_status` away from `'ACTIVE'` already excludes the account from the very next poll cycle (within 60s) with identical practical effect. Document 30's phrasing assumes a per-account-task architecture that isn't what's actually built; adding a redundant per-account cancellation mechanism on top would be pure ceremony with no behavioral difference.

**What I did:** Extracted `revoke_single_account_with_google` from the existing full-wipe loop (now shared by both paths, no duplicated logic) and call it from `auth_google_disconnect` before local cleanup — non-fatal by design (Doc 28 §4.4 Local Wipe Priority), matching the full-wipe path's existing behavior. Fixed the status/action naming. Added the `withdraw_consent_event` call. Extracted the DB-mutation portion into `apply_disconnect` for direct unit testing without a real Keychain/network round-trip.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `apply_disconnect_updates_status_withdraws_consent_and_logs` — inserts a connected_account + a `gmail_oauth_consent` event, runs the disconnect, asserts `account_status = 'disconnected'`, `email_address` cleared, the consent event still exists but has `withdrawn_at` set, and the audit_log entry's action is exactly `gmail_revoked`. Full `cargo test --lib`: 343 passed (342 + 1 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-007: Implement Secrets Management Audit (Integration Test Suite)

**Found:** No `src-tauri/tests/` integration test directory existed at all. Coverage of the same underlying invariants already existed, but as weak, source-grepping unit tests scattered across `phase9_rigorous_tests.rs`/`phase9_security_tests.rs`, under different names than Document 30's 5 named acceptance tests (e.g. `test_ipc_never_returns_tokens` checked only for the literal substrings `"pub token: String"`/`"pub password: String"` anywhere in a file — it would miss a field named e.g. `access_token: String`, or a token embedded one level deeper in a nested struct). One test, `test_sqlite_file_unreadable_without_keychain_key`, was already a genuine integration-style test (calls the real `db::init_db` with a real Keychain-derived key) — reused that pattern as the template for the rest.

**What I did:** New `src-tauri/tests/secrets_audit.rs`, a true integration test crate exercising `dinero_app_lib`'s public API exactly as an external consumer (or an attacker with only IPC access) would — not its internals. All 5 of Document 30's exact acceptance test names, each strengthened over the existing weaker equivalents:
- `test_sqlite_file_unreadable_without_keychain_key` — reused verbatim from the existing genuine test.
- `test_pdf_passwords_never_plaintext` — calls the real `encrypt_password`, asserts the output never contains the plaintext as a substring *and* is exactly nonce+ciphertext+GCM-tag length (ruling out a weaker reversible encoding, not just "isn't equal to the input string").
- `test_sqlite_key_never_written_to_disk` — derives the real key, then recursively scans every file actually written to the app's data directory during a real `init_db` for that key appearing anywhere in raw bytes (binary-safe, not a text search) — a real filesystem audit, not a source-code grep.
- `test_gmail_tokens_stored_only_in_keychain` — inserts a `ConnectedAccountsRow` via an *exhaustive*, no-`..`-shorthand struct literal (a new token-shaped field added to that struct without updating this literal is a compile error, not a silently-passing test), then scans every table's actual column names via `PRAGMA table_info` for anything token/secret-shaped — a real schema-wide audit that catches future additions anywhere, not just the one file a source-grep happens to point at.
- `test_tokens_never_returned_to_react` — scans every `#[tauri::command]`-annotated function's full signature (joining wrapped multi-line signatures, unlike the existing check which only inspects the single line immediately after the attribute) for `TokenStore`/`access_token`/`refresh_token`.

**Real false positive caught and fixed by the schema-wide column scan itself:** it flagged `network_activity_log.secret_fields_masked` — a legitimate metadata column (a list of *which* fields were redacted before logging, never a secret value itself) that just happens to contain "secret" in its name. Added an explicit, documented exemption rather than weakening the check generally.

**Not touched:** the existing weaker `phase9_rigorous_tests.rs`/`phase9_security_tests.rs` tests were left in place — they still serve their own regression-testing purpose under this codebase's established phase-numbered convention, and having both a same-crate unit test and an external-API integration test isn't true duplication (they test different boundaries).

**Verified:** `cargo test --test secrets_audit`: 6 passed. Full workspace `cargo test --no-fail-fast`: lib unit tests 343 passed (unchanged from TASK-AUTH-006, since this task added no unit tests) + secrets_audit 6 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated lib-test failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero warnings in the new file.

## TASK-AUTH-008: Enforce Tenant Isolation Pattern Across All IPC Handlers

**Found:** the codebase-wide check (extending the same scanning technique used in TASK-AUTH-007) turned up exactly one existing violation of "IPC argument structs must never carry a `profile_id`/`user_id`-style field" — `auth_google_start`'s `profile_id` parameter — and that was already fixed at TASK-DB-022, before this task was reached. No other `#[tauri::command]` anywhere in the codebase accepts a profile_id/user_id/session_id-style parameter.

**What I did:** New `src-tauri/src/ipc/middleware.rs` (`require_active_session`, resolving the current session exclusively from `auth::session::SessionState` — TASK-AUTH-005 — never from a caller-supplied argument) and wired it into the actual Gmail/licensing commands Document 30 names: `auth_google_disconnect`, `license_activate`, `license_deactivate`. This is the piece TASK-AUTH-005 explicitly deferred ("requires re-auth before any Gmail/licensing IPC command executes... enforced by TASK-AUTH-008"). Followed the same "primitive now, full retrofit later" pattern already established in this codebase for `ipc::with_panic_boundary` (its own doc comment explicitly defers wiring into all ~53 handlers to a later task) — wiring the session gate into literally every one of the ~53 commands (most of which are plain local-data reads/writes with no Gmail/licensing sensitivity at all) is disproportionate to this task's own scope and Effort M rating; the 3 commands wired are the ones Document 30's own text specifically calls out ("Gmail/licensing IPC command").

**Real conflict caught by the build itself:** `license_deactivate` is also called *directly as an internal Rust function* (not through Tauri's IPC layer) by the "Reset App Data" full-wipe flow in `commands/data.rs`, per Document 28 §4.4's "Local Wipe Priority" — the wipe must attempt license deactivation unconditionally, even if no session happens to be active at that exact moment (session state doesn't matter to a full local erasure). Adding a session-gate to `license_deactivate` unconditionally would have broken the wipe flow (and did — the build failed to compile with a mismatched-arity error until fixed). Resolved by extracting the actual deactivation logic into `deactivate_license_internal(pool: &Pool)` — session-check-free — with `license_deactivate` (the IPC-facing command) calling it after enforcing the session gate, and the wipe flow calling `deactivate_license_internal` directly, bypassing the gate as it must.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. New `src-tauri/tests/tenant_isolation.rs`: `test_data_isolation_ipc_parameter_attack` (Document 30's exact named acceptance test — the mechanical, codebase-wide scan for any command signature carrying `profile_id:`/`user_id:`/`session_id:`) plus a behavioral test proving `require_active_session` never falls back to a default/caller-suggested identity. 2 new unit tests in `middleware.rs` itself (empty-state errors, set-state returns exactly that value). Full workspace `cargo test --no-fail-fast`: lib 345 passed (343 + 2 new), `secrets_audit` 6 passed (unchanged), `tenant_isolation` 3 passed (new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated lib-test failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-009: Implement Local License State Machine

**Found:** `license_state`'s schema, the `LicenseStatus` enum (already all 8 Document 18-spec'd values), the write-gate (`licensing::gate::assert_write_allowed`, already comprehensively enforcing "every write-path IPC command checks `license_state.state` and returns `AppError::LicenseLocked` when `LOCKED`" — already wired into most commands across earlier tasks), and a substantial background validation worker (`licensing::worker::start_background_validation`, already implementing grace transitions, clock-skew detection with a 1-hour correction grace, and JWT-verified trust-on-success) all already existed and were already largely correct.

**Gap found:** the one thing genuinely missing was a *state machine* in the literal sense — nothing enforced transition legality. Every local status change (`worker.rs`'s grace/lock transitions, `state.rs`'s `transition_to_locked`) was a raw, unconditional `UPDATE`, with no shared mechanism checking "is moving from the current status to this new one actually legal." The existing `test_license_state_machine_transitions` (in `phase9_security_tests.rs`) only exercised the raw-SQL/legacy path forward through legal states — it never attempted or asserted rejection of an illegal transition (e.g. `LOCKED → ACTIVE`), so "illegal ones are rejected" (this task's own acceptance criterion) had zero coverage.

**What I did:** New `licensing/state_machine.rs`: a pure `is_legal_transition(from, to)` graph matching Document 30's exact transition list (`AnonymousEval→Trial`, `Trial→Active`, `Active→Grace`, `Grace→Active`, `Grace→Locked`, `Active→Locked`, `Trial→Locked`), plus one addition not named in Document 30's text but preserving existing behavior rather than introducing a new rejection: `AnonymousEval→Locked` (the clock-skew-detection code path in `worker.rs` isn't guarded against running on a never-activated install, and rejecting that transition would be a behavior change the docs don't discuss either way). `transition(conn, to)` enforces the graph, is a no-op if already in the target state, and errors otherwise. Routed `state.rs`'s `transition_to_locked` and `worker.rs`'s `Active→Grace` write through it (its return type changed `SqliteResult` → `anyhow::Result` to carry the "illegal transition" error — every caller already used `?`/`.unwrap()`, so this was transparent). Deliberately did **not** route the "trust a freshly server-verified JWT" writes (a successful periodic validation, or `license_activate`) through this graph — those establish wholesale-fresh authoritative state from an externally-verified source, a different kind of write than a local graph transition, and must never be blocked by it.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Renamed the old `phase9_security_tests.rs` test (`test_license_state_machine_transitions` → `test_active_grace_locked_basic_sql_transitions`, since it now collided in name with the new, real acceptance test and no longer represented the actual state-machine enforcement) — kept as a basic CRUD smoke test, not deleted. Added 4 new tests in `state_machine.rs`, including the exact acceptance-named `test_license_state_machine_transitions` (walks every legal edge in sequence, then asserts `LOCKED → ACTIVE` — Document 30's own named illegal example — is rejected and the row stays `LOCKED`). Full `cargo test --lib`: 349 passed (345 + 4 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-010: Implement Periodic License Validation Background Task

**Found:** `licensing::worker::start_background_validation` already implements nearly all of this task: a Tokio interval loop (whose first `tick()` resolves immediately, satisfying "plus once on cold-start"), JWT-signature verification before trusting a successful response, network-failure → `GRACE` (not immediate lock), and clock-skew detection with the exact 1-hour correction grace named in the spec. `ValidateRequest` already sends only `device_id` — checked this against the payload-minimization requirement directly (`client.rs`'s own comment cites Document 19 §14.2/§14.4: this system's actual design has no separate "license_key" concept at all post-activation, revalidation is device_id-only since one device binds to exactly one license per §11.3 — Document 30's "license_key, device_id, email" is a generic paraphrase that doesn't match this system's already-established, more specific design, not a real gap to fix).

**Doc-vs-doc, already resolved, not re-litigated:** the interval is 6 hours, not Document 30's "every 24 hours" — the code already cites Document 40 §7's "roughly every 6 hours" explicitly as the reason, and also runs on the same cadence while `LOCKED` (so a healed payment auto-unlocks without a manual refresh) — a deliberate prior decision, left as-is.

**Real gap found, flagged not implemented:** "plus once on... resume" (waking from sleep) has no implementation — there's no macOS sleep/wake event listener anywhere in the codebase, and Tauri has no built-in cross-platform primitive for it; genuine resume detection would need native `NSWorkspace` notification bindings (a new FFI dependency and platform-specific code), a materially larger undertaking than this task's Effort M suggests for what's otherwise a near-complete task. Worst case impact is narrow: a stale validation persists for up to 6 hours after a long sleep (the next interval tick catches up), well within the 7-day grace window — not silently ignored, but a real, acknowledged gap.

**What I did:** Added the acceptance-named `test_licensing_backend_receives_no_financial_data` for the *validate* call specifically (the existing test under this name in `phase9_security_tests.rs` only covers the *activate* endpoint) — mocks `/api/license/validate`, asserts the request body is exactly `{"device_id": "..."}`, nothing else.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. 1 new test. Full `cargo test --lib`: 350 passed (349 + 1 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-011: Implement License Activate/Deactivate/Validate IPC Commands

**Found:** `license_activate`/`license_deactivate`/`license_refresh` (the "`license_validate()`/`license_refresh`" pair Document 30 describes as one concept — confirmed `license_refresh` already calls `client.validate()`, verifies the JWT, and updates state, exactly matching the spec) all already existed, already correctly bound `device_id`, already verified JWT signatures before trusting responses, and (from TASK-AUTH-008, done earlier this session) already required an active session — except `license_refresh`, which didn't have that gate wired in; added it for consistency, since Document 30's own text groups all three commands together as sharing the same constraints.

**Real gap found:** `DEVICE_ALREADY_BOUND` (Document 19 §14.2/§4's documented activation error code) was never surfaced distinctly — `client.rs`'s `activate()` only called `res.error_for_status_ref()`, which inspects the HTTP status and discards the response body entirely, so the specific error code the backend actually returns was thrown away in favor of a generic "request failed" message. No "guidance to deactivate elsewhere first" could ever reach the user, regardless of what the backend actually said.

**What I did:** `activate()` now checks for a non-success status *before* discarding the body, attempts to parse it against Document 19 §3.4's `{code, message, details}` error shape, and propagates the `code` value directly if present. `commands.rs`'s `license_activate` catches the specific `"DEVICE_ALREADY_BOUND"` string and returns a clear, actionable message ("Deactivate it there first (Settings → License → Deactivate), then activate here.") instead of a generic network error.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `activate_surfaces_device_already_bound_distinctly` (mocks a 409 response with the real error shape, asserts the propagated error is exactly `"DEVICE_ALREADY_BOUND"`, not a generic wrapped string). Full `cargo test --lib`: 351 passed (350 + 1 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-012: Embed License Public Key for Local JWT Verification

**Found:** Already fully implemented and well-tested in `licensing/jwt.rs`. The RSA-256 public key is embedded via `include_str!` (Document 30's exact mechanism), `verify_license_jwt(jwt) -> Result<LicenseClaims>` validates signature and expiry (`validation.validate_exp = true`), and the test suite already goes well beyond the bare acceptance requirement: valid-signature/valid-claims round-trip, expired-JWT rejection, wrong-key rejection (proving verification isn't a no-op), tampered-payload rejection, and a sanity check that the embedded placeholder key is at least well-formed PEM. Document 30 names a slightly different path (`src-tauri/assets/license_public_key.pem` vs the actual `src-tauri/keys/license_public.pem`, alongside an existing `keys/README.md`) — an organizational difference only, not touched.

**Gap found, deliberately not guessed at:** "validating signature, expiry, and issuer" — no issuer (`iss` claim) validation exists. Checked every document that could define what this should even be (Document 22 §10.3, Document 19's JWT/error catalog sections): none of them define an `iss` claim as part of this JWT's shape at all, and `LicenseClaims` itself (`sub`/`device_id`/`plan`/`billing_interval`/`exp`) has no issuer field. Unlike the `license_key`/`email` non-issues at TASK-AUTH-010/011 (which had an explicit, already-established resolution to point to), there is nothing here to resolve against — Document 30's phrasing reads as boilerplate "how to verify a JWT" advice, not a requirement grounded in this system's actual claim shape. Implementing a check against a guessed issuer string risks either silently no-op'ing (if left unconfigured) or actively rejecting every legitimate JWT the real Licensing Backend issues (if the guessed value doesn't match). Left unimplemented and flagged here rather than guessed at.

**Verified (no code changes needed):** `cargo build --lib` clean (no changes made). Existing test suite already covers this task's substance thoroughly; no new tests added since nothing was changed.

## TASK-AUTH-013: Implement Two-Step Confirmation for Delete Account / Full Data Wipe

**Found:** Nearly everything already existed and was already correct. `commands::data::settings_delete_account` (the "Reset App Data" flow, matching Doc 19's `settings_delete_account` naming — this app has no login/account concept, so a full local wipe is the documented equivalent) already: writes `account_deletion_requested` to `audit_log` *before* any destructive step; revokes Gmail tokens; calls `deactivate_license_internal` (Local Wipe Priority — proceeds even if that call fails); clears the SQLCipher base key and PDF password AES key from Keychain; deletes `finance.db` + its `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars + the daily-backup directory; and restarts the app into fresh onboarding. Contrary to a stale comment claiming the two-step typed-confirmation UI was "tracked separately by M45" (implying it didn't exist), `Settings.tsx` already has a complete two-step modal — a warning listing exactly what's deleted, then a type-to-confirm input gated on the *exact* phrase Document 30 quotes, `RESET_CONFIRM_PHRASE = "DELETE MY DATA"`. Corrected the stale comment to reflect this.

**Real gap found:** "delete finance.db and all `.bak` files" wasn't fully satisfied. `db::migrations::create_pre_migration_backup` writes `finance.db.bak.<timestamp>` snapshots directly into `app_dir` (its target directory is the *database's own parent*, not the `backups/` subdirectory the daily rolling backup uses) — the wipe flow only ever swept `app_dir/backups/`, silently leaving up to `PRE_MIGRATION_BACKUP_RETENTION`-many full snapshots of the user's financial history sitting in the app data directory after a "delete all my data" request.

**Second gap found:** no final `account_deletion_completed` audit trail entry existed at all (Document 30: "write a final audit_log entry ... as the last write before the file is removed"). Writing it via `db::audit_log::insert` would be self-defeating — `audit_log` lives *inside* `finance.db`, which is deleted in the very next step, so a row written there is destroyed before it could ever serve as an audit trail. Logged via `tracing::info!` instead, which persists in `app-logs.log`, a separate file that survives the wipe — the only mechanism that could actually make this event durable.

**What I did:** Extracted the file-deletion logic into `delete_finance_db_and_all_backups(app_dir)`, adding the missing pre-migration-backup sweep (matching the exact `finance.db.bak.` prefix `prune_old_pre_migration_backups` itself filters on) alongside the existing sidecar/daily-backup cleanup. Added the `tracing::info!("account_deletion_completed: ...")` line as the last step before `app.restart()`.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `sweeps_finance_db_sidecars_daily_backups_and_pre_migration_backups` — creates real files matching every category (main DB, both sidecars, two pre-migration backups with different timestamps, one daily backup, and one deliberately unrelated file), runs the sweep, and asserts every target file is gone while the unrelated file survives (proving this isn't just wiping the whole directory). Full `cargo test --lib`: 352 passed (351 + 1 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-AUTH-014: Implement Incident Response — Suspicious Activity Detection

**Found:** Nothing existed — no `security/` module at all. The underlying response *mechanisms* this task needs (session revoke, Gmail disconnect, audit logging, `PRAGMA integrity_check`, the OAuth degradation path) all already existed from earlier tasks this session; this task's actual job is the detection/counting layer that watches for repeated occurrences and invokes them.

**What I did:** New `src-tauri/src/security/incident_response.rs`: `IncidentMonitor` (Tauri-managed, in-memory-only counters per `TriggerKind` — deliberately not persisted, since a monitor watching for *this process's own* DB corruption/decryption failures has no reason to trust a counter it would need to read back from the very store it's watching), `record_trigger` (fires exactly once per threshold crossing, then resets — so a sustained failure condition doesn't re-fire on every single occurrence), and `respond_to_incident` (writes a `security_incident_detected` `audit_log` entry, emits a `security_incident` event for the frontend, always revokes the current session, and additionally disconnects every connected Gmail account for OAuth-specific triggers — "disable the affected integration ... if token theft suspected" only applies when the trigger is actually OAuth-related, not e.g. a DB integrity failure).

Wired into 2 of the 4 named trigger points, both with real existing hooks: `PRAGMA integrity_check` failures (fires on the very first occurrence — a threshold of 1, since a single corrupt database is already an incident, unlike a transient network blip) inside the existing daily maintenance loop in `lib.rs`; and the OAuth degradation path's `should_purge` branch (`ingestion::oauth::mark_account_degraded_async`) — a Keychain-confirmed invalid/revoked token is the closest existing signal to "repeated OAuth failure," at the same threshold-of-3 as the doc's "repeated" framing loosely implies (no specific count is named anywhere; 3 is an engineering default, same class of judgment call as Document 18 §4.21b's retention-window default).

**Not wired, flagged as such rather than silently incomplete:** "unexpected refresh spikes" has no existing rate-tracking to hook into without a larger addition to `ingestion::polling`'s request cadence. "Audit-log write failure" is closer to unimplementable-by-construction than merely unwired: recording that `audit_log::insert` failed by calling `audit_log::insert` again is circular — if the table/connection is genuinely broken, the incident-response write would fail identically.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. 3 new tests: threshold-crossing-then-reset behavior, the integrity-check-fires-on-first-occurrence special case, and independent per-`TriggerKind` counters (caught and fixed a miscounted assertion in my own first draft of this last test via the test run itself — confirms the harness is doing real work, not rubber-stamping). Full `cargo test --lib`: 355 passed (352 + 3 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

---

## TASK-AUTH-015: Implement Diagnostic Bundle Sanitization for Support Export

**Found:** `diagnostics::generate_diagnostic_bundle` already existed and was already substantially correct — manifest (app/OS version, no more), table row counts (`COUNT(*)` only, never `SELECT *`), redacted ERROR-level log lines, redacted crash reports, and a `bundle_export` consent event (TASK-AUTH-003). An aggressive-whitelist design already excludes raw transaction/statement content, Gmail content, OAuth tokens, and PDFs by construction (never queried in the first place).

**Gaps found:** (1) "pipeline health metrics" wasn't in the bundle at all — the Debug page's `do_get_debug_metrics` (extraction-layer distribution, reconciliation-decision distribution, LLM fallback rate, queue depth — all aggregate, no raw content) existed but was never reused here. (2) "redacted audit-log entries" wasn't included at all — only aggregate table *counts*, never any actual `audit_log` content. (3) The redaction regex's number pattern (`\d{6,}`) only caught 6+-digit runs — the acceptance criteria's own named patterns are "email/16-digit-card/₹-amount," and a real transaction amount like "₹499.00" has just 3 digits before the decimal point, sailing straight through unredacted. (4) The acceptance criterion's "blocking export on any match" implies a real runtime gate, not only a CI-time test — no such gate existed; a bug in `redact()` would have shipped PII with nothing catching it before the ZIP was finalized and handed back to the caller.

**What I did:** Added explicit card-number (`\d{4}[\s-]?\d{4}[\s-]?\d{4}[\s-]?\d{4}`) and ₹-amount (`₹\s?[\d,]+(\.\d{1,2})?`) patterns to `redact()`, matching the acceptance criteria's exact three-pattern list. Added `collect_pipeline_health` (reuses `do_get_debug_metrics`) and `collect_redacted_audit_log` (only `actor_type`/`action`/`resource_type`/`created_at` verbatim — all category/timestamp fields, never raw content — plus `after_json` passed through `redact` rather than included raw or omitted outright, since a redacted shape is still useful context for support). Added `scan_for_pii`, run over every assembled section *before* the ZIP is written — a real blocking gate: `generate_diagnostic_bundle` now returns `Err` and writes nothing if any of the three patterns is still present anywhere, independent of whatever `redact()` did or didn't catch.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. 4 new tests, including the literal acceptance criterion as its own dedicated test: `generated_bundle_contains_zero_pii_matches` seeds a real database with a real email, a real 16-digit card number, and a real ₹ amount (via `local_profile`, `transactions`, and an `audit_log` row with all three embedded in `after_json`), generates a real bundle, then unzips every file the function actually wrote to disk and scans the real bytes — not just the in-memory strings the function assembled — for all three patterns. Full `cargo test --lib`: 359 passed (355 + 4 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: zero new warnings.

## TASK-GMAIL-001: Implement Smart Polling Worker (Area 4 start)

**Found:** `ingestion::polling::start_polling_loop`/`poll_single_account` already existed and got most of the algorithm right: fixed 60s interval (within Document 30's 30–90s band, so not a deviation — the doc only requires the loop cadence sit in that range, not that it vary), `last_history_id` read from `processing_checkpoints` with fallback to `connected_accounts.last_history_id`, the historyId checkpoint saved **only once, after the entire history+pagination loop completes** (real crash-safety property, enforced structurally by early-return-via-`?` on any mid-cycle error), `HISTORY_ID_TOO_OLD` handling via `handle_invalid_history_id` on 404/400, and (from TASK-AUTH-004, already logged) a one-shot 401-triggered token refresh-and-retry.

**Gap found (real algorithmic deviation, not cosmetic):** Document 30's exact spec is "exponential backoff on 429/5xx (1s initial, 60s max, jittered)". The existing retry loop doubled correctly from 1s but had no cap (would have kept doubling past 60s had `max_retries` been higher) and no jitter at all, and `max_retries = 3` meant it gave up at 4s — never anywhere near the stated 60s ceiling. Fixed: extracted `next_backoff()` (pure, capped `min(x*2, 60s)`) and `jittered()` (+/-15%, no new crate — derived from `SystemTime` sub-second nanos rather than pulling in `rand`, since nothing else in the codebase already depends on it); raised `max_retries` to 8 so the sequence (1,2,4,8,16,32,60,60) actually reaches and holds the cap before giving up for this cycle (the next scheduled 60s poll retries regardless). Both 429/5xx and generic-network-error retry sites now use `jittered(backoff)` for the actual sleep and `next_backoff(backoff)` for the nominal progression, keeping the doubling sequence itself deterministic and testable independent of jitter.

**Test naming:** renamed the existing `test_history_id_checkpoint_saved_after_poll` → `test_history_id_checkpoint_saved_after_full_cycle` (Document 30's exact acceptance-test name) — it already asserted the right thing (checkpoint persisted via `save_history_id`), just under a different name. Added the missing `test_backoff_increases_on_429` as a deterministic unit test against `next_backoff` (asserts the exact 1,2,4,8,16,32,60,60 sequence and that further doubling never exceeds 60s) rather than a full network-mocked integration test — `mockito` exists as a dev-dependency elsewhere in the codebase but `polling.rs`'s Gmail history URL is hardcoded (not base-URL-injectable), and refactoring that to support end-to-end HTTP mocking is disproportionate to this task's effort rating; flagged here rather than silently skipped.

**Verified:** `cargo test --lib ingestion::polling` — 3 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 360 passed (359 + 1 net new test), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures (`test_seed_and_fetch` schema drift, 2 `phase10_rigorous_tests` missing-fixture-file failures — none touched by this change). `cargo clippy --lib -- -A warnings` on `polling.rs`: zero hits.

---

## TASK-GMAIL-002: Implement Message Fetch with Metadata-First Strategy

**Found:** `message_processor::process_message` already implemented the core metadata-first shape correctly — Gate 1 (sender verification) evaluated against a `format=metadata` fetch, full body (`format=FULL`) only fetched after Gate 1 passes. Cross-checked Document 30's one-line claim that "Gates 1–2" both run pre-full-fetch against Document 12 §6.2's own Gate 2 definition ("verified sender + financial movement verb + amount + merchant/payee/payor") and TASK-GMAIL-005's own text ("classify sanitized body/subject") — both require full body content Gate 2 cannot evaluate on metadata alone. Treated this as the same class of task-file imprecision already flagged and resolved elsewhere in this doc set (Document 49 §4's "resolvable engineering-convention decision, no business tradeoff"), not a real conflict to stop and ask about: Gate 1 pre-full-fetch, Gate 2 post-full-fetch is what the codebase already does and is what Document 12/TASK-GMAIL-005 actually require.

**Real gaps found:**
1. **Attachment metadata incomplete.** Document 30 names four fields — filename, mimeType, attachmentId, size — but `ExtractedMessage.pdf_attachments` only ever captured `(attachment_id, filename)` as a bare tuple; `mimeType` and `size` were sitting unread on the already-parsed `MessagePart`/`MessagePartBody` the whole time. Replaced the tuple with a named `PdfAttachmentMeta { attachment_id, filename, mime_type, size }` struct, populated from data already in hand (no new fetch). Updated the two call sites (`historical_scan.rs`, `polling.rs`) that destructured the old tuple.
2. **No quota-throttling semaphore existed at all** for full-message fetches — Document 30's explicit "max 50 concurrent full-message fetches/second" requirement had no enforcement anywhere; `historical_scan.rs`'s own `Semaphore::new(10)` is a different, tighter concurrency bound for a different task (TASK-GMAIL-007's own batching concern), not a substitute. Added a process-wide `full_fetch_semaphore()` (50 permits) inside `gmail_client.rs`, acquired by `fetch_message` only for `FetchFormat::Full` — metadata fetches (1 quota unit) aren't gated, matching the doc's own "cheap to reject" framing.
3. **`GmailClient` had no injectable base URL**, unlike `LicensingClient` (`licensing/client.rs`) which already establishes this exact pattern for mockito-testability. All four Gmail endpoint methods hardcoded `https://gmail.googleapis.com` directly, making the metadata-first behavior untestable without a live Gmail account. Added `base_url` field + `GmailClient::new_with_base_url`, keeping `GmailClient::new` as a thin wrapper defaulting to the real `GMAIL_API_BASE_URL` constant — mirrors `LicensingClient::new(base_url, pool)` exactly. Updated all 4 URL-building call sites inside `gmail_client.rs`; the 6 external `GmailClient::new(...)` call sites needed no changes since the 2-arg constructor is preserved.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added `test_metadata_fetch_before_full_fetch` (mockito, in `message_processor_tests.rs`) — asserts a real zero-hit expectation on the `format=full` mock when Gate 1 rejects on metadata alone, proving the cost-saving property directly rather than by proxy. Added `test_quota_throttling_caps_concurrent_fetches` (new `gmail_client_tests.rs`) — 60 tasks contend for the real semaphore; asserts max concurrent holders is always ≤50 and genuinely reaches >40 (not accidentally capped much lower). Full `cargo test --lib`: 362 passed (360 + 2 new), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on any touched file.

## TASK-GMAIL-003: Implement MIME Sanitization

**Found:** `mime_sanitization::extract_body_and_attachments`/`sanitize_html`/`sanitize_plain_text` already implemented all four named acceptance behaviors correctly: plain-text preferred over HTML (first `text/plain` found wins; HTML used only as fallback when no plain part exists anywhere in the tree — correct for both `multipart/mixed` and `multipart/alternative`, since RFC 2046 lists the plain variant first and the walk takes the first match); `<script>`/`<style>` stripped while `<span>`/`<td>`/`<b>`/`<strong>` text is preserved (these aren't in the block-newline list, so the generic tag-strip regex removes only the markup, keeping inner text — inline event attributes are removed for free as part of the same tag markup, no separate handling needed); quoted-reply lines (`>`-prefixed, any nesting depth) stripped; signature block (bare `--` delimiter line) stripped.

**Real issues found (both minor, no behavior change to production output):**
1. Dead code: `line.trim() == "--" || line.trim() == "-- "` — the second arm can never match, since `.trim()` already strips the trailing space before the comparison runs. Removed the unreachable arm.
2. Test-naming/coverage gap: existing tests covered the right behavior but didn't match Document 30's 4 named acceptance tests exactly — `test_mime_plain_text_preferred_over_html` (extra underscore) renamed to `test_mime_plaintext_preferred_over_html`; `test_html_sanitized_preserves_merchant_spans` already matched exactly, untouched; `test_strip_quoted_replies_and_signatures` tested both quote-stripping and signature-stripping in one combined test — split into two independent tests, `test_quoted_reply_stripped` and `test_signature_block_stripped`, each isolating its own behavior rather than relying on the other's side effect to prove the result.

**Verified:** `cargo test --lib ingestion::mime_sanitization` — 5 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 363 passed (362 + 1 net new test), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on `mime_sanitization.rs`.

## TASK-GMAIL-004: Implement Sender Verification Gate (Gate 1)

**Found:** `verified_senders::SenderValidator` already covered exact-domain-match (no substring risk at that stage), subdomain-nesting spoof detection, and display-name-mismatch spoof detection correctly. The 202-entry bundled registry (`verified_senders_registry.json`) already covers major Indian bank domains as required. Registry "signing": no detached signature exists, but the file is `include_str!`'d into the compiled binary the same way the embedded license public key is (TASK-AUTH-012) — trust comes from Apple code-signing/notarization of the whole binary (TASK-DESK-006, architecture-level, not yet reached), not a separate per-asset signature scheme nothing else in this codebase uses either. Judged satisfied structurally, not a gap to invent new crypto for.

**Real gaps found (2 of the spec's 4 named spoof heuristics):**
1. **Wrong algorithm, not just missing test:** Document 30 explicitly says typo-squat detection should go "via `strsim`" — `strsim = "0.11"` was already a Cargo dependency (added at TASK-SETUP-005) but never used; a hand-rolled `levenshtein_distance` function did the job instead. Replaced it with `strsim::levenshtein` and deleted the hand-rolled implementation — same behavior, matches spec's named mechanism exactly.
2. **Missing entirely:** "homoglyph/look-alike Unicode domains" — the fourth named spoof heuristic — had zero implementation anywhere. A single-character Cyrillic-for-Latin substitution (e.g. "hdfcbаnk.net" with Cyrillic а U+0430) would happen to already get caught by the Levenshtein check (distance 1 from the real domain), but that's incidental, not a dedicated defense — a domain substituting 3+ characters would slip past a distance-≤2 threshold entirely. Added `normalize_confusables()` (Cyrillic а/е/о/р/с/х/у/і/ѕ/ј/ԁ → their Latin look-alikes) run against any non-ASCII domain before the exact-match re-check, plus a separate punycode (`xn--`) label check — none of the registry's legitimate domains are internationalized, so an ACE-encoded label is inherently suspicious here regardless of what it decodes to.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Split the existing combined spoof test to add the spec's exact `test_typo_squatted_domain_rejected` (previously folded into `test_spoofed_hdfc_sender_rejected`); added `test_homoglyph_domain_rejected` and `test_punycode_domain_rejected` for the two new checks. `cargo test --lib ingestion::verified_senders`: 7 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 366 passed (363 + 3 net new tests), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on `verified_senders.rs`.

## TASK-GMAIL-005: Implement Content Classification Gate (Gate 2)

**Found:** `content_classifier::ContentClassifier::classify` already handled OTP/KYC hard-reject (unconditional, checked first), statement-priority-over-transaction, and basic keyword-based Marketing/Reminder/TransactionAlert/BalanceUpdate/Noise/Unknown classification. All 8 existing classes were reachable and the 3 acceptance tests with exact spec names (`test_transaction_alert_classified_correctly`, `test_statement_email_routed_to_statement_pipeline`, `test_otp_hard_rejected`) already passed.

**Real algorithmic gap found:** Document 30's own task text specifies two conditional exceptions the existing code never implemented: "marketing keywords **absent a settled-transaction amount** hard-reject" and "reminder language **without a completed-transaction verb** routes separately." The existing code hard-rejected on marketing/reminder keywords alone, with no check for whether a genuine transaction signal (amount + verb) was also present — a real email like "Cashback Offer Inside: You spent Rs. 499 today" would have been swallowed as pure Marketing and never reached Gate 3, silently dropping a real transaction. Added `has_amount_pattern()` (currency-marked amount regex — deliberately narrower than bare digits, matching the precision-over-recall principle, Document 12 §6.2) and `has_transaction_verb()` (the same verb keyword set already used for TransactionAlert), computed once as a `settled_transaction` signal and used to gate both the Marketing and Reminder hard-reject branches.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Split the old combined `test_other_classes` to add the two missing exact-named acceptance tests, `test_kyc_hard_rejected` and `test_marketing_hard_rejected`; added 2 new regression tests for the fixed nuance (`test_marketing_keyword_with_settled_transaction_still_classified_as_transaction`, `test_reminder_keyword_with_completed_verb_still_classified_as_transaction`) proving the exact failure mode described above is now fixed. `cargo test --lib ingestion::content_classifier`: 8 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 370 passed (366 + 4 net new tests), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on `content_classifier.rs`.

## TASK-GMAIL-006: Implement Mandatory Field Gate (Gate 3)

**Found:** `MessageProcessor::evaluate_mandatory_field_gate` already implemented the exact pass condition Document 30 specifies — `(has_amount && has_entity) || has_balance` — and 2 of 3 named acceptance tests (`test_gate3_passes_with_amount_and_merchant`, `test_gate3_fails_amount_only`) already existed and passed.

**Real gap found:** Document 30 requires the rejection to be "a structured `gate3_failed` log (`missing_amount`/`missing_counterparty`)" — but the call site always logged the bare literal string `"gate3_failed"` regardless of which field was actually missing, losing exactly the distinction the spec asks for. Added `gate3_failure_reason()`, a companion function (kept separate from the existing boolean `evaluate_mandatory_field_gate` so its 2 already-passing tests didn't need to change shape) that inspects the same two fields and returns `"gate3_failed:missing_amount"` or `"gate3_failed:missing_counterparty"`; wired into both the `audit_log` write and the local scan-log line at the one call site in `process_message`.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Added the missing third acceptance test, `test_gate3_fails_merchant_only`, plus reason-string assertions on both existing failing-case tests to lock in the new structured codes. `cargo test --lib ingestion::message_processor`: 8 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 371 passed (370 + 1 net new test), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on `message_processor.rs`.

## TASK-GMAIL-007: Implement Historical Scan IPC Command and Worker

**Found:** `scans_historical`/`run_scan`/`run_scan_batches` already covered checkpoint creation, resumability (loads `ScanCheckpointState` from a `paused`/`failed` checkpoint rather than restarting), Gmail search-API pagination, and `scan_progress`/`scan_completed`/`scan_failed` event emission. 2 of 4 named acceptance tests already existed and passed.

**Real gaps found, all substantive:**
1. **Missing entirely, contradicts Doc 19 §3.6:** "no other scan already in_progress for this account" was never enforced — the code detected an `in_progress` checkpoint, logged a warning, and started a *second* concurrent scan task against the same `processing_checkpoints` row anyway. Extracted `reject_if_scan_in_progress()` (pure, testable without the OAuth/Keychain access `scans_historical` itself requires) and wired it in as an early return. Uses `AppError::Validation` since Document 19's dedicated `SCAN_ALREADY_RUNNING` code isn't in the `AppError` enum yet — that catalog-wide mapping is TASK-API-010's explicit scope per `error.rs`'s own doc comment, not invented here. Flagged, not fixed: a narrow TOCTOU race remains (the `in_progress` checkpoint row is written asynchronously inside the spawned scan task, not synchronously before `scans_historical` returns, so two calls within that same tiny window could both pass the check) — closing it fully would need a DB-level unique-in-progress-row constraint or transactional check-and-set, disproportionate to what's being fixed here.
2. **Wrong number, not just untested:** Document 30 says "checkpoint every 5 processed messages" — the code checkpointed every 10. Extracted `CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL = 5`/`should_checkpoint()`.
3. **Real concurrency bug — the semaphore did nothing:** the loop spawned exactly one task onto the `JoinSet`, then immediately drained the `JoinSet` to empty via a `while let Some(...) = join_set.join_next().await` *inside the same iteration*, before the outer `for` loop could spawn a second task. Effective concurrency was always 1, regardless of the `Semaphore::new(10)` gating each task — Document 30's "fetch in bounded concurrent batches (max 50/second)" was never actually happening. Rewrote as a proper pipeline: prime up to `MAX_CONCURRENT_FETCHES` (50, matching TASK-GMAIL-002's shared quota figure) tasks up front, then refill one-for-one as each completes, keeping the pipe full until the ID list is exhausted. Removed the now-pointless local `Semaphore` — concurrency is bounded directly by how many tasks are ever in the `JoinSet` at once; the *global* Gmail-quota semaphore added at TASK-GMAIL-002 (inside `gmail_client.rs`) still provides the real cross-cutting safety net shared with the live poll worker.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean. Renamed the old DB-integration test (which only checked the *final* completed state, never actually distinguishing a 5-cadence from a 10-cadence) to `test_historical_scan_completes_and_checkpoints_final_state`; added the spec-exact `test_historical_scan_checkpoints_every_5` as a deterministic unit test against `should_checkpoint()` directly — wall-clock/DB-timing assertions can't reliably catch an intermediate checkpoint once fetches run concurrently, so this checks the actual threshold value instead of trying to catch it mid-flight. Added `test_concurrent_scan_rejected` against `reject_if_scan_in_progress()` directly (in_progress → Err, completed/None → Ok), sidestepping the OAuth/Keychain dependency a full IPC-level test would need. `cargo test --lib ingestion::historical_scan`: 5 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 373 passed (371 + 2 net new tests), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on `historical_scan.rs`.

## TASK-GMAIL-008: Implement PDF Attachment Detection and In-Memory Routing

**Found:** the actual routing behavior was already fully correct and built as a side effect of `TASK-GMAIL-002`'s work: `gmail_client::fetch_attachment` decodes straight into an in-memory `Vec<u8>` (no intermediate file), both call sites (`polling.rs`'s real-time path and `historical_scan.rs`'s batch path) hand those bytes directly into a `StatementJob` struct sent over an in-process Tokio `mpsc` channel to the Statement Queue — never a file path — and both iterate multiple attachments on one email sequentially, not concurrently. Audited `message_processor.rs`'s `append_to_scan_log` (the only `std::fs`/`tokio::fs` call anywhere in this task's code path) and confirmed it only ever writes extracted `snippet`/header text to a local debug log, never the raw message JSON or attachment bytes — even for the rare case for a Gmail-inlined small-attachment `body.data`, `append_to_scan_log` never serializes the full payload, only two named string fields.

**Real gap:** no acceptance test existed at all for this task's one named criterion, `test_pdf_bytes_never_touch_filesystem`. Added it (`gmail_client_tests.rs`) using mockito to mock the attachment-fetch endpoint (now testable thanks to TASK-GMAIL-002's `new_with_base_url`) and fetching real bytes through `fetch_attachment`. First attempt asserted "no file besides the known DB file" existed afterward, which is too strict — `init_db`'s own side effects (a pre-migration backup file, the hardware-UUID migration marker) legitimately write other files in the same directory, unrelated to this test's actual concern. Rewrote to scan every file's *contents* for the exact PDF byte sequence instead of asserting on filenames, which tests the real invariant precisely without being fragile to unrelated DB-init implementation details.

**Verified:** `cargo test --lib ingestion::gmail_client`: 2 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 374 passed (373 + 1 net new test), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits.

## TASK-GMAIL-009: Implement Multi-Account Aggregation for Poll and Historical Scan

**Found:** `connected_accounts` already supports up to 10 accounts (Document 03 §8.2, already-built per the note this task's own text carries about `TASK-RD-002` being stale). `poll_all_accounts` already iterates every active account independently with its own `job_key = account_id` checkpoint, and its `for account in active_accounts { if let Err(e) = poll_single_account(...).await { tracing::error!(...) } }` shape (catch-and-continue, not `?`) already structurally guarantees one account's failure can't stop the loop from reaching the next. `scans_historical` already takes a single `account_id` and keys its own checkpoint the same way, so per-account (or multiple-simultaneous-via-multiple-calls) historical scans already work; Document 19's IPC catalog has no "scan all accounts" bulk command, so none was invented here. `TASK-TXN-008`'s fingerprint-incorporates-`connected_accounts.id` requirement is correctly deferred — Area 6 hasn't started yet, nothing to check yet.

**Gap:** neither of the 2 named acceptance tests existed. Writing them honestly required navigating a real testability constraint: `poll_single_account`'s first step, `get_valid_access_token`, reads the real macOS Keychain — a dependency this codebase deliberately never touches in automated tests anywhere (confirmed by grepping every existing test file: none write real Keychain entries). Made `poll_all_accounts`/`poll_single_account` `pub(crate)` and threaded a `base_url` parameter through both (plus the one previously-hardcoded `/history` URL in `poll_single_account`, which bypassed `GmailClient`'s own URL entirely) — mirroring the exact `new_with_base_url` pattern from TASK-GMAIL-002, and independently useful regardless of this task (the production call site passes the real `GMAIL_API_BASE_URL` constant, no behavior change there).

**Test design:** `test_independent_checkpoint_per_account` calls `save_history_id` for two accounts directly and confirms neither's checkpoint clobbers the other's — fast, real, no Keychain needed. `test_one_account_failure_does_not_block_others` still can't avoid the Keychain dependency (both accounts hit it inescapably as `poll_single_account`'s first step), so rather than write to real Keychain or add a new public introspection API to `IncidentMonitor` (Area 3, already signed off — out of scope to extend for this test), it uses a capturing `tracing_subscriber::Layer` to observe the real `poll_all_accounts` call logging both accounts' independent token-fetch failures — directly proving neither one's failure stopped the loop from reaching the other, without mocking anything Keychain-shaped. Needed `IncidentMonitor::default()`/`SessionState::default()` `.manage()`'d on the mock app (the real `mark_account_degraded_async` path touches both) and `#[tokio::test(flavor = "current_thread")]` so the thread-local tracing subscriber survives across `.await` points. Verified non-flaky over 5 repeated runs.

**Verified:** `cargo test --lib ingestion::polling`: 5 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 376 passed (374 + 2 net new tests), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on `polling.rs`/`polling_tests.rs`.

## TASK-GMAIL-010: Implement Gmail API Rate Limit and Error Telemetry Hooks

**Found:** nothing existed for any of the 4 named metrics anywhere — `network_activity_log` (Document 18 §4.21b) records per-request status codes for the Network Activity transparency view, but nothing aggregates 429s, 5xx counts, poll-cycle latency, or per-gate rejection rates. `DebugMetrics` (`commands/data.rs`) has extraction/reconciliation distributions but nothing Gmail-specific.

**Built from scratch:** new `gmail_telemetry.rs` — `GmailTelemetry` (4 counters: `quota_exhausted_count` atomic, `error_5xx_by_status` a status-code-keyed map, `poll_cycle_count`/`poll_cycle_total_ms` atomics exposed as an average, `gate_rejections` a gate-name-keyed map) behind a process-wide `OnceLock` singleton — the same pattern as `gmail_client::full_fetch_semaphore()`, chosen for the same reason: `MessageProcessor::process_message` (called identically by both the poll path and the historical-scan path) has no `AppHandle` parameter to hang Tauri-managed state off without a cascading signature change across both call sites. Wired at the 4 natural call sites: 429/5xx branch in `polling.rs`'s retry loop; poll-cycle duration wrapped around the `poll_all_accounts` call in `start_polling_loop` (timed unconditionally, since a stalled cycle — success or failure — is exactly what this latency signal exists to catch); Gate 1 (`VerifiedNoise`/`UnverifiedReject`/`SpoofReject`), Gate 2 (`Otp`/`Marketing`/`Kyc`/`Reminder`/`Noise`/`Unknown`), and Gate 3 (mandatory-field failure) rejection branches in `message_processor.rs` — the shared code path both poll and historical scan already route through, so instrumenting it once covers "message fetch" and "historical scan" simultaneously without duplicating hooks. The `extraction_failed` branch (ladder produced no result) was deliberately left uncounted in `gate_rejections` — it's an extraction failure prior to Gate 3 evaluation, not literally one of the 3 named gates, and conflating it would blur the "per-gate" signal the metric exists to provide.

**Privacy shape:** no acceptance test is named in the source (a telemetry-shape task, per its own text — correctness is code review / TASK-QA-007's privacy suite, not reached yet). Wrote tests anyway, consistent with Document 34's testing obligation applying to every task regardless of named criteria: 4 tests proving each counter behaves correctly in isolation, plus `test_snapshot_shape_carries_no_content_fields`, which serializes a populated snapshot to JSON and walks every string value asserting none exceeds the length of the longest legitimate field name — a real, if approximate, structural proof that nothing content-shaped (an email subject, sender address, merchant name) could be hiding in the payload, not just a design-review claim.

**Verified:** `cargo test --lib ingestion::gmail_telemetry`: 5 passed. Full `cargo test --lib`: 381 passed (376 + 5 net new tests), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on any touched file.

**Area 4 (Gmail Ingestion) complete: all 10 tasks (TASK-GMAIL-001 through TASK-GMAIL-010) done.**

## TASK-STMT-001: Implement Statement Intake and Validation

**Found:** `validator.rs`/`duplicate_check.rs` already covered MIME-magic-byte validation, size cap, SHA-256 hashing, and the filename billing-period heuristic well — 2 of 4 named acceptance tests (`test_non_pdf_rejected`, `test_pdf_over_5mb_rejected`) already existed and passed.

**Real conflicts found and resolved with user confirmation (3 separate check-ins, all "proceed with the fuller fix"):**

1. **IPC shape.** The working `statements_upload(file_paths: Vec<String>, ...)` had the Rust backend `std::fs::read()` a path the frontend would supply. Document 19 §9.1 (authoritative for IPC shape, Doc 49 §6) is explicit and detailed: the frontend reads bytes via the browser File API and sends `files: [{file_bytes, filename}]` — no path ever crosses the boundary. Document 30's own STMT-001 text (`{file_path}`, TCC-file-access-denial handling) predates Document 19's later revision and is stale here. Rewrote the command to accept `Vec<UploadFile>` (`file_bytes`/`filename`, camelCase-mapped for the JS caller), removed the disk-read step and its `FileAccessDenied` branch entirely (nothing in this path ever touches a filesystem path now), and changed the return shape to Document 19's `{results: [{statement_id, filename, status}]}` (was a bare array).

2. **Sync vs. async response model.** The command blocked on a oneshot channel until the *entire* PDF pipeline (password resolution, extraction, possible OCR) finished, returning a terminal status synchronously. Document 19 §3.6/§9.1 describes fire-and-forget: return immediately with an intake status (`queued`), real outcome via the already-existing `statement_parsed`/`statement_parse_failed` events. Removed `StatementJob.respond_to` (the oneshot machinery) entirely — nothing awaits it anymore, so it was dead weight, not just unused for this one path — and moved event emission into the queue dispatcher itself (`ingestion/queues.rs`), since that's now the only place that observes real completion.

3. **Crash-recovery invariant (Doc 18 §4.7).** Digging into the async rewrite surfaced a deeper, pre-existing gap: no `statements` row was ever written until metadata extraction succeeded deep inside `run_parse_pipeline` — meaning a crash during password resolution or parsing left nothing to recover from, for *either* entry point (manual upload or Gmail-attachment, Area 4, already completed/committed). Fixed both: new `db::statements::insert_queued()` writes a `queued`-status row immediately at intake (placeholder billing-period dates — today's date — since those columns are `NOT NULL` in the schema and a migration to make them nullable would be a separate, bigger, unauthorized change; overwritten once real metadata extraction succeeds). `write_statement_row` now upserts by a caller-supplied `stmt_id` (`UPDATE` if the queued row exists — the normal case for both entry points now; falls back to a fresh `INSERT` for the resumed-after-block paths, which correctly mint a new ID exactly as before, since `unprocessed_statements` already owns crash-recovery for the blocked window via its own separate ID — Doc 18 §4.16's `resolved_statement_id` is a deliberate one-way link, not a shared ID, confirmed by reading `statements_confirm_instrument`'s actual linking logic before touching it). Added `delete_orphaned_queued_row()`, a best-effort cleanup for the two cases where the pipeline diverges away from ever completing a pre-created queued row (Instrument Gate block, post-metadata duplicate reject) so it doesn't sit at `queued` forever. Wired the same `insert_queued` call into `polling.rs` and `historical_scan.rs`'s PDF-attachment handoff (Area 4 files, touched with narrow, additive changes only).

**Real bug this surfaced, fixed in the same pass:** `check_file_hash_duplicate` checked `statements.source_message_id` for a hash match — a proxy-hack from before a dedicated `file_hash` column existed on the table (confirmed via `db/tests.rs`: the real column has full CRUD support in `db::statements::insert`/`update`, but no *pipeline* code had ever actually used it — `write_statement_row` still only knew the old proxy). Since my `insert_queued` now correctly writes the real hash into `file_hash`, the pre-existing check would have silently stopped matching new uploads entirely. Fixed the query to check `file_hash`. Also fixed `write_statement_row` storing `instrument_type` ("credit_card") directly into the `statement_type` column, when Document 18 §4.7 specifies a distinct vocabulary there (`credit_card_statement`/`bank_account_statement`) — added the mapping.

**Not fixed, flagged:** `StatementJob.file_hash` is overloaded — the manual-upload path puts a real SHA-256 there, the Gmail-attachment path puts the message ID. This predates this session and isn't exercised by any of STMT-001's 4 named acceptance tests (all manual-upload-path); disentangling it would mean redesigning `StatementJob`'s fields, out of proportion to this task.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` clean throughout the multi-step rewrite. Added `test_duplicate_hash_rejected` and `test_filename_billing_period_heuristic` (the 2 remaining named tests, in `duplicate_check.rs`) and fixed `metadata_extractor.rs`'s existing `test_write_statement_row_partial_metadata` for the new upsert-by-id signature. Full `cargo test --lib`: 383 passed (381 + 2 net new tests), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `cargo clippy --all-targets --lib -- -A warnings`: zero hits on any of the 7 touched files (`commands/mod.rs`, `db/statements.rs`, `statements/metadata_extractor.rs`, `statements/duplicate_check.rs`, `ingestion/queues.rs`, `ingestion/polling.rs`, `ingestion/historical_scan.rs`).

## TASK-STMT-003: Implement PDF Password Resolution with `pdfium-render` Sidecar

**Found:** stored-password ordering (`success_count DESC, last_used_at DESC`), in-memory AES-256-GCM encryption/decryption via the Keychain-derived key, success-count/`last_used_at` updates on a working password, the wrong-password/re-prompt-without-losing-context path, and the 2.5-minute-timeout-to-`pending_retry` transition all already existed and were already correct — 3 of 5 named acceptance tests already passed.

**Real gap — the task's entire namesake — found and fixed (user-confirmed architecture, "New Cargo bin target... Recommended"):** every `pdfium-render` call in the codebase ran in-process, in the main Tauri process, with no execution timeout at all. This is the exact failure mode Document 30 names: a malformed/malicious PDF could crash or exhaust memory in the main process (a macOS Jetsam OOM kill of the whole app), not just fail to parse. No `sidecar/` module or sidecar binary existed anywhere.

Built the isolation from scratch:
- New standalone binary `src/bin/pdf_sidecar.rs` (Cargo auto-discovers `src/bin/*.rs`, no `Cargo.toml` change needed) — deliberately has zero dependency on the main `dinero_app_lib` crate (no DB, no Tauri, no Keychain access); it only ever sees raw PDF bytes over stdin and writes a JSON result to stdout. Protocol: a newline-terminated JSON metadata line, then a 4-byte big-endian length prefix, then the raw PDF bytes — never a temp file, preserving Document 15's "raw PDFs in memory only" invariant identically to the in-process code it replaces. Supports `unlock_check` (this task) and `extract_text` (used below).
- New `statements/sidecar/mod.rs` — parent-side driver: spawns the sidecar per call, writes the request, reads the response, and wraps the whole round trip in a `tokio::time::timeout` that kills the child on expiry rather than waiting indefinitely. The spawn/timeout/kill mechanism (`run_with_timeout`) is deliberately generic and separate from the pdfium-specific request builders, so the actual isolation property is testable with `/bin/cat`/`/bin/sleep` rather than needing a real PDF or a working `pdf_sidecar` binary.
- Rewired `password.rs`'s `try_pdfium_unlock`/`is_pdf_unencrypted` (now `async`) to call the sidecar's `unlock_check` instead of binding `pdfium-render` directly — cascaded `.await` through `try_stored_passwords`, `try_user_password`, and the one caller in `commands/mod.rs`.
- Also moved `parser.rs`'s primary pdfium text-extraction call (`try_pdfium_parse`, used unconditionally for every statement, not just password-protected ones) into the sidecar via the new `extract_text` operation, deleting the now-dead in-process function — this is the same "pdf_parser.rs" concern Document 30 names for this task's file scope, and the highest-frequency pdfium call in the whole pipeline. Left the OCR-fallback render call (`render_page_to_png`, only exercised for scanned/image PDFs) running in-process, explicitly deferred to TASK-STMT-006 ("Implement OCR Fallback for Scanned/Image-Based PDFs"), which already owns its own RAII-cleanup requirement — a coherent, narrower place to extend the sidecar for that specific operation rather than pre-building it now.
- Fixed a real, separate naming bug while touching this file: the existing test was `test_pdfium_unlock_failure_re_prompts` (extra underscore) against the spec's exact `test_pdfium_unlock_failure_reprompts`.

**Security invariant verified, not just claimed:** grepped both new files (`pdf_sidecar.rs`, `sidecar/mod.rs`) for any logging call at all — neither contains a single `tracing::`/`println!`/`eprintln!` statement, so the password can't be logged there even by accident; `password.rs`'s existing `tracing::info!(success = ..., "PDF password attempt")` already only ever logged a boolean, unchanged.

**Verified:** `cargo build --lib` and `cargo build --bin pdf_sidecar` clean. Manually smoke-tested the wire protocol directly (`python3` piping a length-prefixed request into the built binary) before writing the Rust-side driver, catching a real bug early: an initial `BufReader`-then-raw-`Stdin` read split would have silently dropped buffered-ahead bytes — fixed to use one `BufReader` for the whole request. `cargo test --lib statements::sidecar`: 2 passed, including 5 repeated runs confirming the timeout test isn't flaky. `cargo test --lib statements::`: 59 passed.
