## TASK-FE-003: Configure React Query for IPC Data Fetching

**Found:** No `@tanstack/react-query` usage anywhere; every page hand-rolled its own `useState`/`useEffect` fetch. Dependency was already added alongside zustand in TASK-FE-002's `pnpm add`.

**What I did:** `src/lib/queryClient.ts` (30s `staleTime`, `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`, matching Doc30's "background Tokio workers mutate independently of user action" rationale). `src/lib/queryKeys.ts`: a centralized key factory (`all()`/`list()`/`detail()` per domain) so hooks and the invalidator can't drift apart on key shape. `src/hooks/queries/`: `useDashboardSummary`, `useTransactionsList`, `useTransactionDetail`, `useInstrumentsList` — Doc30's own named examples; more will be added as each downstream FE-008–015 task actually needs a new one, not pre-built speculatively. `src/hooks/useIpcQueryInvalidation.ts`: subscribes to the real event names (`transaction_created`/`_updated`/`_deleted`, `scan_completed`, `reconciliation_cluster`, `statement_upcoming_bill_set`) and invalidates the matching query-key domains — Doc30's `transaction.created`/`sync.completed` are illustrative paraphrases, not real event strings (same precedent as TASK-FE-002). Wired `QueryClientProvider` + a mount-once `IpcEventBridge` component into `App.tsx` (a hook needs a component inside the provider it's reading from, not the component creating the provider).

**Regression check (important given `App.tsx` is app-root wiring):** first e2e pass looked like a severe regression (1/12 passing, `aside` not found at all) — turned out to be a stale dev server left running on port 1420 from my own manual debug session, which Playwright's `reuseExistingServer: true` silently reused. A clean rerun after killing the port confirmed **zero regression**: `AppShell.spec.ts` + `Transactions.spec.ts` back to the exact known baseline (7 pass / 4 fail, all 4 the same pre-existing `__TAURI_LISTENERS__` mock-registration races from TASK-FE-001).

**Found, not fixed (out of scope, flagged for TASK-FE-008):** `e2e/fixtures/tauriMock.ts`'s `dashboard_summary` mock still returns the pre-TASK-API-006 response shape (`{total_spend, income, upcoming_bills, limit}`); the real `DashboardSummary` type (`src/lib/ipc.ts`) has been `{month_to_date_spend, limit, utilization_pct, recent_transactions_count, upcoming_bills_count, income}` since that task. `Dashboard.spec.ts` never completes its loading state against this stale mock — pre-existing drift, not something this task's scope (state/query infrastructure, not the Dashboard page itself) should silently patch.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean on all new/touched files. New Vitest suites (`queryKeys.test.ts`, `useIpcQueryInvalidation.test.tsx`): pass, bringing the frontend suite to 22/22. e2e regression check above.

## TASK-FE-004: Build Onboarding Flow — Welcome and Privacy Disclosure Screens

**Found:** Onboarding already existed (`src/pages/Onboarding.tsx`, 429 lines) but as one monolithic 3-step wizard (Preferences → Historical Scan → Gmail Consent) — no dedicated Welcome screen at all, and the network-call disclosure was buried inside the Gmail-consent step (mixed with Gmail-specific scope/beta text) rather than its own standalone, gating screen. Doc30 names 5 separate onboarding screens across FE-004–007; this task covers the first two.

**What I did:** New `src/routes/onboarding/WelcomeScreen.tsx` (app intro + 4 value props grounded in Document 01 §6.1/§6.2's vision statement, "Get Started" button) and `NetworkDisclosureScreen.tsx` (renders Document 01 §10.4's 5-row disclosure table verbatim via a new structured `NETWORK_DISCLOSURE_TABLE` constant in `src/constants/privacy.ts` — the existing `OUTBOUND_CHANNEL_DISCLOSURE` flat-string array stays as-is, it backs the `disclosure_text` audit column, not a rendered table; explicit "Continue" button, no auto-advance). New `OnboardingFlow.tsx` orchestrator (`welcome` → `disclosure` → `setup` phase state) at the `/onboarding` route, replacing the old direct `<Onboarding />` mount; the old component (renamed `LegacyOnboardingSteps` at its import site) still handles the remaining 3 steps unchanged — TASK-FE-005/006/007 will extract those into their own named screens one at a time, same pattern as this task's two extractions. Renamed the old component's now-duplicate `CardTitle` from "Welcome to Dinero" (correctly belongs to the new WelcomeScreen now) to "Set Up Your Preferences".

**e2e fallout from the rename (expected, fixed in the same commit):** `e2e/Onboarding.spec.ts` asserted `"Welcome to Dinero"` immediately reachable — updated to click through Welcome (`Get Started`) and NetworkDisclosure (`Continue`) first. Its invalid-limit assertion's fallback-OR clause also referenced the old title (and its primary text, `"Must be a positive number"`, never matched the real `limitError` string `"Must be > 0"` even before this change — a pre-existing latent bug only exposed once the fallback stopped silently covering for it); fixed both to the real text.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint` on all touched+new files: clean (3 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` hits confirmed on unrelated, untouched lines via full lint-log inspection, not introduced here). `npx vitest run`: 22/22. e2e: `Onboarding.spec.ts` full flow (Welcome → Disclosure → Preferences → Scan → Gmail-connect-with-failure-then-success → Dashboard) passes end-to-end; `a11y.spec.ts` 9/10 (1 known pre-existing `__TAURI_LISTENERS__['db.corrupted']` race, unrelated to onboarding).

## TASK-FE-005: Build Onboarding Flow — Gmail Consent and OAuth Trigger Screen

**Found:** Gmail consent UI (scopes, beta disclosure, outbound-channels disclosure, error display, manual-mode skip link) already existed but inline as step 3 of `Onboarding.tsx`'s monolith, not its own file. Real gap: the 5-minute OAuth loopback-listener timeout (`src-tauri/src/ingestion/oauth.rs`'s `OAUTH_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT`, bails with the literal string `"oauth_timeout"`) reached the frontend only as whatever `AppError::Auth`'s `Display` produces (`"Authentication error: oauth_timeout"`) — shown verbatim as a raw internal string, not Doc30's required "Connection timed out, try again" message.

**What I did:** Extracted step 3's JSX into `src/routes/onboarding/GmailConsentScreen.tsx` (presentational only — the parent still owns the OAuth call, loading state, and the shared `CardFooter` submit button, same placement as every other step's Continue button, to avoid a duplicate-button a11y smell). New `mapOauthError.ts` pure function (`oauth_timeout` substring → friendly message, else pass through, else generic fallback) wired into `handleConnectGmail`'s catch block, with its own unit tests — extracted specifically so this real branch of logic is unit-testable without driving a real 5-minute OAuth round trip end-to-end.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean on all touched/new files (1 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` on the untouched `catch (e: any)` line, confirmed via lint source dump, not introduced here). `npx vitest run`: 25/25 (3 new `mapOauthError` tests). e2e `Onboarding.spec.ts` full flow: passes unchanged (extraction is behavior-preserving for the non-timeout path, which is what the mock fixture exercises).

## TASK-FE-006: Build Onboarding Flow — Historical Scan Range Picker

**Found:** A months-count dropdown ("Historical Scan Range": 1/3/6/12 months) already existed as old step 2, positioned *before* Gmail consent — but its value was only ever saved as a `historicalScanMonths` preference field, never actually used to call `scans_historical`/`startHistoricalScan`. Structurally this couldn't have worked anyway: triggering a scan needs a real connected `account_id`, which doesn't exist until *after* Gmail OAuth succeeds — the doc's own task ordering (FE-005 Gmail Consent, then FE-006 Historical Scan) reflects this, the shipped code didn't. Net effect: new users picked a scan range that was silently discarded, and no historical scan ever ran automatically during onboarding.

**What I did:** New `HistoricalScanScreen.tsx`, moved to *after* a successful Gmail connect (old step 3 slot; `handleConnectGmail` now calls `setStep(3)` on success instead of navigating straight to the dashboard). Real date-range picker (default last 3 months, min-date clamped to 2 years back per TASK-GMAIL-007), reusing `GlobalStateContext`'s existing `scanStartDate`/`scanEndDate`/`scanStatus`/`scanProgress`/`handleStartScan` (the same machinery Settings' manual "Sync Now" already drives) rather than duplicating the `scan_progress`/`scan_completed`/`scan_failed` listener wiring a second time — calls `refreshConnectedAccounts()` on mount to avoid racing the 3s poll for the account that just connected. Renders a determinate progress bar while running, a completion summary, and a "Skip for now" link that proceeds straight to the dashboard. `scanProgressPercent` (divide-by-zero guard) extracted to its own module (co-locating it in the component file triggered `react-refresh/only-export-components`) with unit tests.

**Real e2e mock bug found+fixed in passing:** `GlobalStateContext`'s `refreshConnectedAccounts` calls the real `settings_get_connected_accounts` command (renamed from `list_connected_accounts` by TASK-API-008), but `tauriMock.ts` still only handled the pre-rename name — every real caller of this function (not just this test) silently got `{}` back from the fixture's unhandled-command fallback. Added the correct command name as a second match (kept the old dead one too, harmless).

**e2e fallout (expected, fixed in the same commit):** `Onboarding.spec.ts`'s middle section drove the now-removed dropdown before the Gmail-consent step; removed. Final assertions updated to expect `HistoricalScanScreen` ("Scan From" label) after a successful connect, click "Skip for now", then assert Dashboard — same pattern as FE-004/005's fallout fixes.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean. `npx vitest run`: 27/27 (3 new `scanProgressPercent` tests). e2e: `Onboarding.spec.ts` full flow passes end-to-end (through the Skip path — the Start Scan path with live `scan_progress` events isn't separately e2e-covered, out of proportion for this task; the percentage math itself is unit-tested). `AppShell.spec.ts` run alongside: same 4 pre-existing `__TAURI_LISTENERS__` failures, nothing new.

## TASK-FE-007: Build Onboarding Flow — Trial/License Activation Screen

**Found:** No trial/license confirmation screen existed at all — onboarding finished immediately after Gmail connect (or after skipping it). The 14-day trial itself already auto-starts correctly with zero explicit trigger needed: it's derived server-side from `local_profile.created_at` (`licensing::gate::trial_days_remaining`) whenever no `license_state` row exists yet, exactly matching Doc30's "auto-starts... no user action needed" — nothing to build there beyond surfacing it.

**Real conflict surfaced and resolved with user input:** Doc30's "Already have a license key?' link revealing an input calling `license_activate`" doesn't match the real backend — `license_activate` (Document 19 §14.2, intentionally) takes Razorpay payment-confirmation fields (`email`/`razorpay_payment_id`/`razorpay_signature`/`billing_interval`), not a license key, and no Razorpay checkout flow exists anywhere yet (Area 12/BILL entirely unbuilt). Building a form for fields no real user could ever obtain would be either fake or misleading. **User chose:** build the trial confirmation now (fully real), and for the "already have a subscription" path, point to Settings → License (which — checked — already has this exact real Razorpay-field activation form built, from an earlier ad-hoc "C9" fix) rather than duplicating a non-functional form; revisit when Area 12 ships.

**What I did:** New `LicenseActivationScreen.tsx` — hydrates `useLicenseStore` (TASK-FE-002) on mount, shows "Your 14-day free trial has started" + formatted "Trial ends on [date]" (or "You already have an active subscription" for the ACTIVE/GRACE edge case — e.g. a Mac-to-Mac hardware migration re-running onboarding), plus a plain-language note pointing to Settings for the deferred activation path. Wired as onboarding's new step 4 (bumped `TOTAL_STEPS` to 4) — both the Gmail-success path (`handleConnectGmail`) and the manual-skip path (`handleSkipGmail`) now land here (the trial confirmation is universal, not Gmail-specific) before finishing.

**Real e2e mock bugs found+fixed in passing:** (1) `tauriMock.ts`'s `license_get_status` returned lowercase `state: 'active'` — the real backend always uppercases this field (`compute_license_status`/`trial_status_response` in `commands.rs`) — and fabricated an already-paid subscription (`pro`/`monthly`/170 days) for what should realistically be a fresh trial in an onboarding-flow test; fixed to `state: 'TRIAL'` with realistic values (confirmed via grep that no other spec depends on the old fabricated shape). (2) While editing the mock, a stray inline-backtick in one of my own comments inside the fixture's giant template-literal init script silently truncated it mid-file — caught via a `SyntaxError: Missing semicolon` on the very next test run, fixed before it was ever committed.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean (1 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` unrelated). `npx vitest run`: 27/27. e2e: `Onboarding.spec.ts` full flow passes end-to-end through the new step 4. `PrivacySettings.spec.ts` run alongside surfaced 3 pre-existing, unrelated failures (network-activity-table mock always empty / no error-injection support, nothing to do with license status) — flagged, not fixed, out of this task's scope.

## TASK-FE-008: Build Dashboard Page

**Found:** Dashboard already existed (326 lines) but hand-rolled `useState`/`useEffect` fetching + manual `transaction_created`/`scan_completed`/`statement_parsed` Tauri listeners (the last of which doesn't exist as a real backend event — dead code). None of Doc30's 5 named sub-components existed. 6 backend commands (`dashboard_upcoming_bills`, `dashboard_categories`, `analytics_spend_trend`, `analytics_pending_review_count`, plus 2 unused-by-this-task `analytics_top_merchants`/`analytics_recurring_payments_summary`) were built by TASK-API-006 with zero frontend call sites at all.

**What I did:** Added `pnpm add recharts`. Wired `ipc.ts` types/wrappers + `queryKeys`/query hooks for the 4 commands this task needs. Built all 5 Doc30-named components (`SpendSummaryCard`, `CategoryBreakdownChart`, `SpendTrendChart`, `UpcomingBillsWidget`, `PendingReviewBanner`) in `src/components/dashboard/`, each backed by extracted pure logic + unit tests (`computeMonthOverMonthDelta` — `DashboardSummary` has no delta field, derived from the monthly spend-trend series instead; `groupCategoriesForChart` — folds a 9th+ category into "Other" per the dataviz skill's non-negotiable, never a generated hue; `classifyBillUrgency` — overdue/critical/warning/normal). Invoked the `dataviz` skill before writing any chart color code: used its validated default categorical palette (`node validate_palette.js` confirmed worst adjacent CVD ΔE 24.2, well clear of the >=12 target; 3 slots fail 3:1 contrast so those pie slices always carry a visible direct label, never color alone) and its single-hue sequential blue for the one-series trend line — no dual-axis, fixed hue order, legend present for the pie (>=2 series) and absent for the line (1 series, card title names it). Deliberately no dark-mode chart tokens: `App.css` explicitly says Doc 14 §4.2's dark-mode tokens are "defined now, activation deferred to Phase 3 — do not wire these into any component," so chart colors stay light-mode-only like the rest of the app. Rebuilt `Dashboard.tsx` as an orchestrator on React Query hooks, dropping the page-local event listeners entirely — `App.tsx`'s globally-mounted `useIpcQueryInvalidation` (TASK-FE-003) already invalidates the same query keys on the real events, so the old per-page subscriptions were duplicate work (and one listened for a Tauri event that's never emitted). Preserved the pre-existing Recent Transactions feed, Monthly Limit progress bar, and per-instrument-utilization Accounts & Cards panel — real, tested, valuable functionality Doc30's file list doesn't name but doesn't forbid either.

**Real regression caught by e2e before commit:** the React Query rewrite silently dropped the original "auto-expand the first instrument group on load" behavior (previously done inside the manual `fetchData`'s success branch) — `e2e/Dashboard.spec.ts`'s utilization test failed because the credit-card group started collapsed. Fixed with a `useRef`-guarded `useEffect` that auto-expands once on first load without clobbering a user's later manual toggles on background refetch.

**Real e2e mock bug fixed (flagged back in TASK-FE-003, fixed here as promised):** `dashboard_summary`'s mock still returned the pre-TASK-API-006 shape (`total_spend`/`upcoming_bills`), so `Dashboard.spec.ts` never actually left its loading skeleton against this fixture before today. Fixed to the real `DashboardSummary` shape, and added mocks for the 3 newly-wired commands (`dashboard_upcoming_bills`, `dashboard_categories`, `analytics_spend_trend`) plus `analytics_pending_review_count` (0, so `PendingReviewBanner` renders nothing by default — matches "no hardcoded subscription alert" behavior the old page's own comment already established as the right default).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean across all new/touched files. `npx vitest run`: 40/40 (13 new tests across the 3 extracted pure-logic modules). e2e: `Dashboard.spec.ts` 5/5 (up from the FE-003-era 0-and-timing-out state). `AppShell.spec.ts` + `Onboarding.spec.ts` + `Transactions.spec.ts` + `a11y.spec.ts` run together: 17 pass, same 5 pre-existing `__TAURI_LISTENERS__`-family timeouts, nothing new.

## TASK-FE-009: Build Transactions List Page with Filters

**Found:** `Transactions.tsx` already existed (711 lines) but as a single monolith: client-side-only category filtering (over whatever page happened to be loaded, not server-side), Enter-triggered non-debounced search, Previous/Next paging via manual `useState`, an inline detail drawer doing double duty as both "row detail" and "correction form," and manual `useState`/`useEffect` fetching with 3 hand-rolled event listeners (one for a `statement_parsed` event that doesn't exist in the real `AppEvent` enum -- dead code, same pattern found in Dashboard.tsx during TASK-FE-008). The real `transactions_list` command already supported `TransactionListFilters` (from_date/to_date/instrument_id/direction/category_id/status per Document 19 Section 8.1) but `ipc.ts`'s wrapper only ever sent `page`, silently dropping every filter argument.

**Real backend gap found+fixed:** Doc30 wants a per-row "instrument badge," but `TransactionRecord` never selected `instrument_id` at all (`do_fetch_transactions`'s SELECT and `do_transactions_search`'s mapping both omitted it) -- there was no way for the frontend to know which instrument a transaction belonged to without a separate per-row fetch. Added `instrument_id: Option<String>` to the struct and both construction sites.

**Doc30-vs-Document-19 resolution (no escalation needed, established precedent):** Doc30's task text lists instrument/category/direction/date/amount/tags as AND-combined filters, but Document 19 Section 8.1 only documents from_date/to_date/instrument_id/direction/category_id/status -- amount-range and tag filtering were never part of the real contract. Built the filter bar against the real 6, not the paraphrased 8.

**Real cross-command gap found, not fixed (flagged, documented in code):** `transactions_add_tag`/`_remove_tag` require a real tag UUID, but `tags_list` (the only tag catalog exposed to the frontend) discards ids and returns names only. No frontend-reachable way exists to resolve a tag name to its id. Routed the new tag-add quick action through `transactions_update`'s bulk `tags: string[]` replace convenience instead (fetch-current-then-append) -- the same mechanism the old drawer already used successfully. Fixing `tags_list`'s shape is Area 8/API-007 scope.

**What I did:** `TransactionFilterBar.tsx` (instrument/category/direction/date, all AND-combined, real server-side filtering). `TransactionSearchBox.tsx` (300ms debounced via new `useDebouncedValue` hook, with unit tests). `TransactionRow.tsx` (merchant, color-coded amount, category badge+icon, date, instrument badge, source-pipeline icon). `useTransactionsInfiniteList` (React Query useInfiniteQuery, filters in the query key). Inline quick actions (`TransactionQuickActions.tsx`): category change, tag add, soft-delete with confirmation -- all optimistic via shared `optimisticTransactionPatch.ts` helpers. `CategoryBreakdownChart` (TASK-FE-008) links here via `?category=<id>`; this page reads it as the initial filter.

**Architecture split from Detail:** row click now navigates to `/transactions/:id` instead of the old inline drawer; the drawer's content is superseded by TASK-FE-010's real detail page, done immediately next in this session. Added a minimal `TransactionDetail.tsx` placeholder + route now so nothing 404s in between.

**e2e fallout (fixed in the same commit):** `Transactions.spec.ts`'s drawer/pagination tests, `TransactionSearch.spec.ts`'s tag test, and `a11y.spec.ts`'s focus-trap test all targeted the removed drawer -- rewritten to the new navigation/infinite-scroll/quick-action flow. Added a missing `categories_list` e2e mock (no real frontend call site existed before this task).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `cargo check`/`clippy`/`test --lib`: clean, 551 passed, same 4 pre-existing failures. e2e: Transactions+TransactionSearch 8/8; a11y 9/10 (baseline); broader regression at baseline. **Not verified:** `npx vitest`/`npx eslint` hit a sustained tool-availability outage this session (repeated Bash-safety-classifier unavailability specific to those two commands, while tsc/playwright/cargo/plain-shell all worked) -- retried repeatedly without success; substituted tsc+playwright as verification instead. Flagged honestly; will retry at the start of the next task.

## TASK-FE-010: Build Transaction Detail Page

**Found:** No dedicated detail page existed before this task — TASK-FE-009 left a minimal placeholder (raw JSON dump) explicitly flagged as temporary. `API.transactions.get()`/`getObservations()` were typed `any`/`any[]` with zero frontend consumers, so no prior code would have caught a contract mismatch.

**Real backend gap found+fixed:** `EmiGroupSummary` (backing `transactions_get_emi_group`) only ever returned an aggregate `{installments_paid, total_paid_minor}` — no total-installments denominator and no per-installment list, so "visualizing all installments with paid/remaining progress" (Doc30's exact requirement) was architecturally impossible against the existing contract. Extended it with `total_installments` (resolved via `MAX(emi_total_installments)` across the group's linked observations — the EMI detector's own parsed "X of Y" phrasing, TASK-TXN-012) and `installments: Vec<EmiInstallmentDetail>` (id/amount/date per paid installment, chronological). New Rust unit test (`test_emi_group_summary_includes_total_installments_and_installment_list`) covers both additions.

**What I did:** Full editable-field correction form (merchant display name, category select, notes, tags — Document 19 §8.3's exact set) via new `useUpdateTransactionFields` mutation hook. Tag add/remove reuse TASK-FE-009's bulk-replace workaround (`useRemoveTransactionTag` mirrors `useAddTransactionTag`, same `tags_list`-returns-names-not-ids gap noted there). `SourceEvidencePanel.tsx`: renders every linked observation with its pipeline, extraction method, confidence score, and — for `source_pipeline === 'statement_pdf'` — an explicit "This value was confirmed by your bank statement" note (the statement-overrides-email transparency requirement). `EmiInstallmentTimeline.tsx`: rendered only when `emi_group_id` is present, progress bar + paid-installment list + placeholder rows for the remaining count. "Thanks, we'll remember this" confirmation shown for 3s after a successful save (the backend already writes merchant corrections into `merchant_aliases` via TASK-API-003's existing feedback-log/learning-loop wiring — this is purely the UI acknowledgment Doc30 asks for, not a new write path). Kept the pre-existing "View Raw Source" dialog + `JsonViewer` (from the old inline drawer) since it's real, valuable, untouched-by-Doc30 functionality.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean. `cargo check`/`clippy`/`test --lib`: clean, 552 passed (+1 new EMI test), same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `npx vitest run`: 43/43 (the earlier session-wide `npx vitest`/`npx eslint` tool-availability outage flagged in TASK-FE-009 resolved partway through this task — both now run normally). e2e: added 2 new detail-page content tests (edit-merchant-and-save shows the confirmation; source evidence panel + view-source dialog render) — `Transactions.spec.ts` 6/6. Full regression (`AppShell`/`Dashboard`/`Onboarding`/`TransactionSearch`/`a11y`) at the established baseline, nothing new. EMI timeline's real render path isn't separately e2e-covered (no mock transaction carries an `emi_group_id`) — the Rust unit test is the real coverage there, proportionate given the frontend component itself is presentational over an already-tested backend shape (same precedent as TASK-FE-006's Start-Scan progress path).

## TASK-FE-011: Build Instruments List and Detail Pages

**Found:** `Instruments.tsx` already existed (407 lines) with real add/edit/delete modals and type-conditional fields (billing cycle day, IFSC) already mostly correct — but grouped by issuer name (a reasonable partition, but not Doc30's explicit "grid grouped by type" dimension), and its own comment already flagged a real bug for this exact task to fix: the shared add/edit form rendered editable issuer_name/masked_identifier inputs in edit mode even though the backend has never accepted them post-creation (identity fields, Document 15 §2.8's `resolve_instrument()` matching key) — silently discarded server-side. No `InstrumentDetail` page/route existed at all.

**Real backend gap found+fixed:** `StatementRecord` (backing `statements_list`) never selected `instrument_id` despite the `statements` table having had the column since the initial schema — instrument-scoped statement history (Doc30's explicit requirement) was impossible to build. Added it to the struct and the `do_fetch_statement_history` SELECT (same pattern as TASK-FE-009's `TransactionRecord.instrument_id` fix).

**What I did:** Rebuilt `InstrumentsList` (`Instruments.tsx`) as a grid grouped by type via `INSTRUMENT_TYPES`, using `InstrumentCard.tsx` (click → `/instruments/:id`) and extracted `AddInstrumentModal.tsx` (type-conditional fields; VPA-relabeled masked-identifier field for `upi_vpa`, no new backend column since `masked_identifier` already serves that role). New `InstrumentDetail.tsx`: editable fields correctly narrowed to `full_identifier`/`billing_cycle_day`/`bank_ifsc` only (fixing the flagged identity-field bug — issuer/masked-identifier now shown read-only), instrument-scoped transaction history (reuses TASK-FE-009's `useTransactionsInfiniteList` filtered by `instrument_id`), statement history (client-side filtered by the newly-exposed `instrument_id`), and a "forget saved password" action (`useForgetPdfPassword`, filtered `PdfPasswordSummary` list by instrument — that type already carried `instrument_id`, just never had a delete UI wired to it). Added `?instrument=<id>` as a second deep-link query param `Transactions.tsx` reads on mount (alongside TASK-FE-008's `?category=<id>`), so InstrumentDetail's "View All" link works.

**Real, broader e2e-infra gap found+fixed while testing delete:** no e2e test in this entire suite had ever actually driven a `confirm()`/`ask()`-based delete-confirmation flow (TransactionQuickActions, TransactionDetail, and now InstrumentDetail all use this exact pattern) to completion. Root cause: `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog`'s `ask()`/`confirm()` route through a single `plugin:dialog|message` IPC command and compare the resolved value against the OK-side button's *exact label string* ("Yes"/"Ok"/a custom `okLabel`) — not a boolean. The command was entirely unmocked (fell through to the fixture's `{}` fallback, an object that's truthy but not `=== 'Yes'`, so `confirmed` was always `false` and every delete silently no-opped past the confirm step). Fixed by mocking `plugin:dialog|message` to resolve to the correct OK-side label based on the real `buttons` argument shape. This unblocks e2e coverage for every existing and future delete-confirmation flow in the app, not just this task's.

**Also fixed in passing:** `statements_list`'s e2e mock still returned a bare array (pre-TASK-API-004 shape); `API.statements.listHistory()`'s `.then(r => r.records)` was silently unwrapping to `undefined` against it. Fixed to the real `{records, total}` page shape (same stale-mock pattern found and fixed several times already this session).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx eslint`: clean. `cargo check`/`clippy`/`test --lib`: clean, 552 passed, same 4 pre-existing unrelated failures. `npx vitest run`: 43/43. e2e: `Instruments.spec.ts` 3/3 (up from 1 pass/1 fail/1 permanently-skipped before the dialog-mock fix — the deletion-constraint test would have silently skipped forever against the new grid-card layout otherwise, since its old selector targeted a list-row delete button that no longer exists). Full regression (`Transactions`/`TransactionSearch`/`Dashboard`/`AppShell`/`Onboarding`/`a11y`) at the established baseline (28 pass, same 5 pre-existing failures), nothing new.

## TASK-FE-012: Statements List and Upload Flow

**Found:** `Statements.tsx` already existed (640 lines) and was unusually complete: dropzone, password-prompt modal with a real 2.5-minute countdown, a Statement Instrument Gate confirmation modal, an access-denied modal, and an ad-hoc "unprocessed statements" section — all backed by `GlobalStateContext`'s real event-driven state (`statement_password_required` etc.). Non-PDF validation only ran on the drag path, not the file-picker path; no file-size check existed at all; duplicate uploads were lumped into a generic failure toast instead of being surfaced distinctly.

**Real backend gap found+fixed (major):** TASK-STMT-010 built a complete, dedicated unprocessed-items retry/recovery backend — `statements_list_unprocessed` (real 3-bucket grouping: `awaiting_password`/`pending_retry`/`failed`, each entry carrying a structured `failure_type`/`failure_reason`, never a raw exception), `statements_retry_unprocessed`, and `statements_discard` — with **zero frontend call sites**. The pre-existing page instead derived its own ad-hoc 2-status list by filtering general statement history, missing the real grouping entirely and **discard didn't exist as a UI action at all**. Wired all three for the first time.

**What I did:** Extracted `StatementUploadDropzone.tsx` (immediate non-PDF check on both drag and picker paths now, not just drag; new client-side 25MB size guard — no server-side limit exists, this is a UX-only immediate-feedback addition; duplicate uploads (`error: duplicate_file_hash...`) surfaced as a distinct "Already Uploaded" toast instead of the generic failure bucket). `PasswordPromptModal.tsx` (extracted, still consuming `GlobalStateContext`'s existing correct event-driven countdown/state — not rebuilt, since it's genuinely global, real, and already correct). New `UnprocessedItemsQueue.tsx` against the real TASK-STMT-010 backend with retry + discard actions per group. Rebuilt `Statements.tsx` as the orchestrator on `useStatementsList` (TASK-FE-011's hook, replacing `GlobalStateContext`'s parallel/now-dead statement-history fetch) plus the three new components; kept the Instrument Gate confirmation and access-denied modals inline (real, necessary, not named by Doc30 — same precedent as Dashboard/Instruments preserving un-named real UI).

**Real e2e-infra gaps found+fixed (several, in the course of actually driving this flow for the first time):**
1. `statements_submit_password`'s mock **threw** for wrong-password and returned a bare string `'success'` — the real backend (and both old and new frontend code, confirmed via `PasswordPromptModal`'s own "I9 fix" comment) always *resolves* with a `{status, statement_id, attempts_remaining?}` object. Fixed to match.
2. `statement_password_required` was dispatched by the test under the illustrative dotted name (`statement.password_required`) — `GlobalStateContext`'s real listener is the snake_case `AppEvent::StatementPasswordRequired` string; the dispatch silently no-opped, so this test had never actually opened the password modal.
3. No mocks existed at all for `statements_upload`, `statements_confirm_instrument`, `statements_list_unprocessed`, `statements_retry_unprocessed`, `statements_discard` — added.
4. Discovered mid-fix that a hash-only `page.goto()` to the same route doesn't trigger a real navigation (so `addInitScript` overrides never re-execute), and `page.reload()` re-runs the fixture's *own* `addInitScript` too, silently reverting any seeded mock state — spent real debugging time isolating this before landing on in-app navigation (clicking sidebar links) plus a new `refetchOnMount: 'always'` on `useUnprocessedStatements` (a reasonable product default on its own: this queue drives real discard/retry decisions and shouldn't show a stale snapshot from a prior visit).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. e2e: `StatementUpload.spec.ts` 3/3 (up from a state where the password-modal test never actually opened the modal and the unprocessed-queue test exercised no real backend data). Full regression (`Instruments`/`Transactions`/`TransactionSearch`/`Dashboard`/`AppShell`/`Onboarding`/`a11y`) at the established baseline (34 pass, same 5 pre-existing failures), nothing new. **Not verified this pass:** `npx vitest run` / `npx eslint` hit the same sustained tool-availability outage documented in TASK-FE-009/010's fix-log entries (repeated Bash-safety-classifier unavailability specific to those two commands across many retries, while `tsc`, `playwright`, and `git` all worked normally throughout this task). No new pure-logic modules were added this task requiring new unit tests (React Query hooks + presentational components only, covered by the e2e above) — flagging honestly per established precedent rather than silently skipping.

## TASK-FE-013: Reconciliation Review Queue and Cluster Detail Pages

**Found:** `Reconciliation.tsx` existed as a single monolithic page: one flat list of clusters, each card rendering its members inline with a hardcoded, fabricated `Confidence: 75%` badge on every single cluster regardless of its real data, and resolution actions (Merge/Reject/Keep Separate/Review Later) firing directly from the list with no confirmation-message content beyond the button label itself. No separate cluster detail page existed, no `ClusterMemberComparison` component existed, and there was no "Unassigned Transactions" section at all -- `reconciliation_get_unassigned_transactions` (TASK-API-005) had zero frontend call site.

**Real backend gap found+fixed (major, data-correctness bug, not just missing wiring):** `fetch_cluster_members`'s SQL only `LEFT JOIN`ed `transactions` via `canonical_transaction_id`. Per Document 18 §4.6a, the cluster member that actually triggered the ambiguity (`member_role = 'incoming'`) carries `observation_id`, not `canonical_transaction_id` -- so for every cluster, the primary "new evidence" card's merchant/amount/date silently fell through to the COALESCE fallbacks ('Unknown'/0/'Unknown') on every single cluster, in production, not just in a test fixture. Fixed by also joining `transaction_observations` and coalescing across both sides. Also added `member_role`, `observation_id`, and `canonical_transaction_id` to the `ClusterMember` struct/query -- `observation_id` is required by `reconciliation_clusters_resolve` (Document 12's `resolve_cluster`), and the pre-existing `ipc.ts` wrapper never sent it at all (a missing required argument on every resolve call). Also wired `reconciliation_clusters_get` (single-cluster detail) and `reconciliation_get_unassigned_transactions`, both of which existed on the backend with zero frontend call sites before this task.

**Confidence field:** dropped entirely rather than fixed -- Document 18 §4.6/§4.6a has no confidence column on either `reconciliation_clusters` or `reconciliation_cluster_members`, so the pre-existing static "75%" badge was never real data. Consistent with this session's established precedent (Document 18/19's real, authoritative schema/contract wins over Doc 30's prose, which lists "confidence" as a comparison column with nothing backing it).

**What I did:** Split into `Reconciliation.tsx` (the review queue: two clearly-labeled sections, "Pending Clusters" and "Unassigned Transactions", each cluster a compact preview card with a "Review Cluster" button; a shared positive "All Caught Up!" empty state when both sections are empty, reusing the pre-existing design), `ReconciliationClusterDetail.tsx` (new route `/reconciliation/:clusterId`: full `ClusterMemberComparison`, candidate selection for `confirm_match`, and the four resolution actions -- `confirm_match`/`reject_candidate`/`keep_separate`/`mark_unresolved` -- each gated behind this codebase's established `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` `ask()` confirmation pattern (first wired for delete flows in TASK-FE-011) with a plain-language explanation of what the action actually does before confirming, since Doc 30 explicitly calls these "financial-record-altering decisions"), and `ClusterMemberComparison.tsx` (new, reusable side-by-side member cards: merchant/amount/date/source-pipeline via the existing `SourcePipelineIcon`, no confidence column, selectable candidate cards when a selection callback is passed).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint` (back online this task): clean on all new/changed files. `npx vitest run`: 43/43 passing, no new unit tests needed (hooks/components covered by e2e; no new pure-logic modules added). e2e: `ReconciliationConsole.spec.ts` rewritten for the new two-page structure, 4/4 passing (queue sections render; cluster detail renders comparison + resolves via `confirm_match`; resolution failure keeps the user on the detail page with an error toast; empty state shows "All Caught Up"). Full regression suite: same 5 pre-existing `__TAURI_LISTENERS__`-race-condition failures as every prior task's baseline, plus 3 newly-observed pre-existing failures in `PrivacySettings.spec.ts` (`settings_network_activity_list` mock always returns `[]`, no seeded row/empty-state/error-state ever driven) that are unrelated to this task's diff (confirmed via `git status` -- I never touched that spec file or its mock command) and predate it; not fixed here as out of scope, flagged for whichever task owns that page.

**Also fixed (opportunistic, same file):** the legacy `commands::data::tests::test_seed_and_fetch` integration test was already failing before this task (confirmed via a `git stash` A/B comparison) on a `statements` ad-hoc test table missing the `instrument_id` column added to the real `StatementRecord` SELECT back in TASK-FE-011/012. Added the column since it's a one-line fix in the same `setup_db()` helper this task's `reconciliation_cluster_members`/`transaction_observations` test-table columns needed touching anyway. That same test then failed on a *second*, unrelated pre-existing gap (`instruments` ad-hoc table missing `bank_ifsc`, from a different earlier task) -- did not chase that one down, since walking every stale column across this one sprawling legacy test is unrelated to FE-013's scope; `cargo test --lib`'s total failure count is unchanged (4 before and after, same test names, just a different missing-column message).

Area 9 (FE) now 12/19 done.

## TASK-FE-014: Settings — Privacy and Consent History Page

**Found:** No dedicated Privacy sub-page existed -- `Settings.tsx` is a single 1264-line monolith already covering Integrations, Mail Scan, Recovery Phrase, License & Billing, Spending Limits nav, Appearance, LLM Config, Network Activity, Consent History, Pattern Rules, PDF Passwords, and Data & Privacy inline. Consent History was already correctly wired to the real backend (`auth_get_consent_history`) but inline, not a named component. The "Reset Local Database" two-step typed-phrase modal was, functionally, already exactly Doc 30's `DeleteAccountSection` spec (warning modal, then type "DELETE MY DATA" to confirm) and already wired to the real `settings_delete_account` command (Doc 30's own task text says `auth_delete_account`, which doesn't exist -- this app has no login/account concept, and Document 19 §13/§18's real, documented name is `settings_delete_account`, a full local wipe; the real contract wins per this session's established precedent) -- just not under the doc's file/section naming. Two real gaps: `handleDisconnectGmail` had **zero confirmation of any kind** (a misclick silently cut off a connected account), and the "Export Diagnostic Data" button had no copy anywhere stating the bundle stays local and is never auto-uploaded, which Doc 30 explicitly requires.

**Major pre-existing bug found+fixed (blocks the entire Settings page, not just this task):** while getting my own new e2e coverage running, discovered every single e2e test that ever visited `/#/settings` was silently hitting a React error boundary ("Something went wrong" / `patternRules.map is not a function`) and rendering none of the real page content. Root cause: `API.patternRules.list()` calls the real `settings_pattern_rules_list` (TASK-API-008), but the e2e mock only had the older debug-only `debug_fetch_pattern_rule_health` and a stale `pattern_rule_set_status` name -- `settings_pattern_rules_list` fell through to the `{}` unhandled-command fallback, `patternRules.map` crashed. This had been silently mis-attributed: the AppShell.spec.ts baseline failure "Settings has a Spending Limits subsection that navigates to /spending-limits" (carried in every fix-log's "known baseline" list since TASK-FE-008) was assumed to be a `__TAURI_LISTENERS__` race condition like its four siblings, but it was actually this crash the whole time -- the button it waits for never rendered because the page never got past the error boundary. Fixed by adding real mocks for `settings_pattern_rules_list` (seeded array) and `settings_pattern_rules_update`; that baseline failure is now gone (confirmed in the full regression run below).

**Also fixed while touching the same mock area (both directly exercised by my new `PrivacySettings.tsx`):** `settings_get_network_activity` was mocked under its pre-rename name `settings_network_activity_list` returning a bare array instead of `{ entries: [...] }` -- `NetworkActivity.tsx`'s real call site always fell through to the fallback. `export_logs` resolved a bare string `'ok'` instead of the real `{ success, file_path }` shape -- `PrivacySettings`'s new "Saved locally to: ..." confirmation always showed `undefined`. `auth_google_disconnect` had no mock at all -- `RevokeGmailButton`'s disconnect flow had never been driven to completion. Converted the pre-existing `e2e/PrivacySettings.spec.ts` (which tests `NetworkActivity.tsx`, not my new same-named component -- a confusing but pre-existing name collision) from a raw `page.route('**/api/ipc', ...)` HTTP interception -- which can never match a real request, since Tauri IPC isn't HTTP -- to the standard `tauriMock` fixture every other spec in this suite uses; its 3 failing tests (flagged as a newly-observed, out-of-scope baseline gap in TASK-FE-013's fix-log) now pass.

**What I did:** Extracted `src/components/settings/ConsentHistoryList.tsx` (verbatim structural extraction, no logic change), `DeleteAccountSection.tsx` (verbatim extraction of the already-correct two-step modal, relabeled "Delete My Data" to match Doc 30's naming), `RevokeGmailButton.tsx` (new: wraps the existing disconnect call in a `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` `ask()` confirmation -- "New transactions from this account will stop syncing immediately" -- reusing this codebase's established confirm-dialog pattern), and `PrivacySettings.tsx` (composes the three above plus a new "Export Diagnostic Bundle" action with the required "saved locally... never automatically uploaded" copy). `Settings.tsx` now renders `<PrivacySettings />` in place of the old inline Consent History + Data & Privacy sections, and `<RevokeGmailButton>` in place of the old bare unconfirmed Disconnect button; kept the full-data export/restore-from-backup buttons in Settings.tsx itself under a slimmer "Data Management" heading (distinct from the diagnostic bundle, per the pre-existing J7 comment, and not named by Doc 30's FE-014 file list).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint` on all touched/new files: clean (the pre-existing 14 `no-explicit-any`/`no-useless-assignment` issues in `Settings.tsx` are all in code this task didn't touch -- confirmed by line number against the diff -- left alone, consistent with "fix only what this task touched"). `npx vitest run`: 43/43, no new unit tests needed (presentational components + one e2e-covered confirmation flow). e2e: new `SettingsPrivacy.spec.ts` (5/5: consent history renders seeded events; positive empty state; diagnostic bundle copy + export flow; Gmail disconnect requires confirmation and actually removes the account; delete-my-data two-step type-to-confirm gating) and the rewritten `PrivacySettings.spec.ts` (4/4). Full regression: **55 passed / 4 failed**, down from the prior baseline's 46 passed / 8 failed (5 known + 3 `PrivacySettings.spec.ts`) -- both the `PrivacySettings.spec.ts` failures and the misdiagnosed "Spending Limits subsection" failure are gone; the remaining 4 are the same genuine `__TAURI_LISTENERS__`-race-condition gaps (a11y modal roles, `CorruptedDatabaseRecovery`, `LOCKED` license state, background task indicator) that map to not-yet-built Area 9/10 tasks (FE-016, FE-018, DESK-003).

## TASK-FE-015: Settings — Connected Accounts and Password Management Pages

**Found:** Both concerns already lived inline in the `Settings.tsx` monolith. The "Integrations" section already had a working multi-account list, "Connect another Gmail account" (re-triggering OAuth), and per-account revoke (with the confirmation TASK-FE-014 added). "Stored Statement Passwords" already listed instrument-scoped saved-password metadata (issuer/masked identifier, never the password itself) with a working "Forget" action. Functionally close to spec, just not under Doc 30's component naming, and missing the one thing Doc 30 explicitly calls out: **per-account status badges**.

**Real backend gap found+fixed:** `connected_accounts.account_status` (Document 18's real column) exists and is actively written by the token-refresh-failure lifecycle (`gmail_token_refresh_failed` sets it to `'degraded'` -- Gmail sync has silently stopped until the user reconnects), but `settings_get_connected_accounts`'s query filtered to `WHERE account_status = 'ACTIVE'` and the `ConnectedAccountInfo` struct never selected the column at all. A degraded account -- exactly the case a status badge exists to surface -- was invisible to the frontend entirely, with no way for the user to even discover their sync had stopped short of noticing missing transactions. Fixed: `ConnectedAccountInfo` now carries `account_status`; the query now includes any non-`'disconnected'` status (a fully revoked/disconnected account, whose `email_address` is also cleared on disconnect, stays excluded since there's nothing useful to show for it).

**What I did:** New `src/components/settings/ConnectedAccountsSettings.tsx` (self-contained: owns connect/disconnect state, reads `connectedAccounts` from the existing `GlobalStateContext`, renders a status badge -- green "Gmail Connected" / amber "Needs Reconnection" with an explanatory line -- and a "Reconnect" action for degraded accounts that re-runs the same OAuth flow). New `src/components/settings/StatementPasswordSettings.tsx` (extracted verbatim from the pre-existing "Stored Statement Passwords" section -- already correct, no logic change beyond making it self-contained). `Settings.tsx` now renders both in place of the old inline "Integrations" and "PDF Password Management" sections; removed the now-dead local state/handlers those sections owned (`isConnecting`, `connectError`, `handleConnectGmail`, `handleDisconnectGmail`, `pdfPasswords`, `handleDeletePassword`, etc.) along with their now-unused `Lock`/`Trash2` icon imports and 7 of the file's pre-existing `no-explicit-any`/`no-useless-assignment` lint violations (moved out with the code, not fixed in place -- confirmed via `npx eslint` count dropping from 14 to 10, all 10 remaining in code this task never touched).

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint` on all touched/new files: clean. `npx vitest run`: 43/43. `cargo check --lib` / `cargo test --lib oauth`: clean, 6/6. `cargo test --lib` (full): 552 passed / 4 failed, identical failing tests to TASK-FE-013/014's baseline (all pre-existing and unrelated -- this task touched no other Rust code). e2e: new `SettingsConnectedAccounts.spec.ts` (2/2: a seeded degraded account shows its badge + explanation and a working Reconnect action that clears it; stored passwords render with a working Forget action and the mock's password value never appears in the rendered page text). Full regression: 57 passed / 4 failed (up from TASK-FE-014's 55/4 by exactly this task's 2 new tests), same 4 known pre-existing `__TAURI_LISTENERS__`-race failures, zero new regressions.

Area 9 (FE) now 14/19 done.

## TASK-FE-016: License Lock Overlay and Grace Period Banner

**Found:** `useLicenseStore` (TASK-FE-002) already existed and correctly mirrors the real `license_state_changed` broadcast, but had zero UI consumers besides the onboarding screen -- nothing rendered its `isLocked` state at the app-shell level, and `GracePeriodBanner` didn't exist at all. `AppLayout.tsx` did have an existing ad-hoc "License Locked" dialog, but it was **entirely dead code**: it only rendered on a `license_clock_skew` event, which is defined in the backend's `AppEvent` enum but is never actually emitted anywhere in the crate (grepped the whole backend) -- clock-skew detection genuinely happens (`worker.rs`'s 6-hour tick, `transition_to_locked(c, true)`) but flows through the general `license_state_changed` broadcast like every other lock cause, not this specific dead event. So the dialog could never appear in production for *any* reason a license actually locks (clock skew, grace expiry, invalid JWT). It also `return`ed early and replaced the entire component tree (sidebar included) rather than allowing read-only navigation, directly contradicting this task's own spec ("blocking write interactions but explicitly still allowing navigation to read-only views ... and to the reactivation flow").

**Also found:** nothing ever called `useLicenseStore().hydrate()` outside the onboarding flow -- a returning already-locked user would see no overlay at app launch and stay unlocked-looking until the next `license_state_changed` broadcast, which could be up to 6 hours away (the background worker's tick interval).

**What I did:** New `src/components/licensing/LicenseLockOverlay.tsx` and `GracePeriodBanner.tsx`, both reactive off `useLicenseStore` (no local polling). `LicenseLockOverlay` is mounted *inside* `<main>` (absolutely positioned, `inset: 0` relative to that element, not the page root) so it covers only the routed content pane -- the sidebar stays outside its bounds and fully clickable, satisfying "still allowing navigation to read-only views and to the reactivation flow" for real, with a "Reactivate in Settings" button that navigates there directly. No lock *reason* is fabricated in the copy (clock skew vs. grace expiry vs. invalid JWT) since `LicenseStatusResponse` only ever exposes `state: "LOCKED"`, never a reason -- consistent with this session's "don't invent data the backend doesn't provide" precedent. `GracePeriodBanner` renders above routed content, shows real `days_remaining`, and a "Retry validation now" button wired to the real `license_refresh` command (Doc 30's own task text says `license_validate`, which doesn't exist -- Document 19 §14.4's real, documented name is `license_refresh`, the same action the background worker itself calls). Dismissal is local component state, not persisted, so it reappears on next launch or the next time the license re-enters GRACE, matching "dismissable-but-recurring." Removed the dead `license_clock_skew` listener, the dead full-screen dialog branch, and `handleRecheckStatus` from `AppLayout.tsx`; added a one-time `hydrateLicenseStore()` call alongside the existing initial backend-status check so the real current state is reflected immediately at launch.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint` on all new/changed files: clean (4 pre-existing `no-explicit-any`/unused-var issues remain in `AppLayout.tsx`, all in code this task never touched). `npx vitest run`: 43/43 (`useLicenseStore.test.ts`, pre-existing, unaffected). e2e: rewrote the pre-existing `AppShell.spec.ts` "LOCKED license state" test, which had been dispatching an illustrative dotted event name (`license.clock_skew`) AppLayout never actually listened for and asserting the sidebar left the viewport -- directly contradicting this task's own "still allow navigation" spec. Now dispatches the real `license_state_changed` event and asserts the opposite (sidebar stays reachable, Reactivate button navigates to Settings). Added 2 new tests: overlay dismisses reactively on a background revalidation back to ACTIVE; Grace Period banner shows real days-remaining and its Retry action actually clears it via the real `license_refresh` contract. Full regression: 60 passed / 3 failed (up from the prior baseline's 57/4) -- the misdiagnosed "LOCKED license state" failure is gone; the remaining 3 (a11y modal roles, `CorruptedDatabaseRecovery` x2) are a *different*, unrelated pre-existing gap in the same illustrative-dotted-event-name family (`db.corrupted` vs. the real `db_corrupted`) that belongs to whichever task owns the corrupted-DB recovery flow, not this one -- flagged, not fixed, to stay in scope.

Area 9 (FE) now 15/19 done.

## TASK-FE-017: Statement-Only Mode Fallback UI

**Found:** No such banner existed anywhere. Doc 30 describes detecting the fallback condition via "a specific lock-reason flag from the backend," sourced from this task's own dependency TASK-AUTH-016 -- which is a documentation/compliance-readiness task with no code deliverable at all (its own acceptance criteria: "none ... not unit-testable"). No such flag exists anywhere in the backend (confirmed via grep). The real, already-available signal is `connectedAccounts` from `GlobalStateContext` (backed by TASK-FE-015's real per-account status): zero connected accounts means either the user chose "Skip — I'll upload statements manually" at onboarding (`GmailConsentScreen`, already real and pre-existing) or every previously-connected account has since fully disconnected. CASA failure/revocation and plain user choice are indistinguishable from the frontend either way, but the correct UI response is identical, so no reason is fabricated.

**What I did:** New `src/components/shell/StatementOnlyModeBanner.tsx` -- dismissable-but-recurring (local state, not persisted, same pattern as `GracePeriodBanner`), suppressed on the Statements route itself (telling a user already there to go upload statements is just noise), with a direct "Upload Statements" CTA. Mounted in `AppLayout.tsx` alongside `GracePeriodBanner`, inside the routed content pane so it never blocks any other part of the app, per spec.

**Regression caught and fixed:** the banner's default copy ("Gmail sync isn't connected") is visible on nearly every page by default in the e2e fixture (`gmail_connected` starts `false`), and collided with a pre-existing unscoped `text=Connected` locator in `AppShell.spec.ts`'s "should display healthy core engine status" test (a real, different "Connected" -- the core-engine health indicator). Playwright's `text=` substring-matched both. Fixed by scoping that locator to the `core-engine-status` test id.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint`: clean (same 4 pre-existing `AppLayout.tsx` issues, untouched by this task). `npx vitest run`: 43/43. e2e: new `StatementOnlyMode.spec.ts` (4/4: shows by default with no Gmail connected and its CTA navigates to Statements; suppressed on the Statements route; hidden once a real connected account exists; dismissing hides it without blocking navigation elsewhere). Full regression: 64 passed / 3 failed, same 3 pre-existing `db.corrupted`-family failures as TASK-FE-016's baseline (unrelated, out of scope), zero net regressions once the `text=Connected` collision above was fixed.

## TASK-FE-018: Global Error Boundary and Toast Notification System

**Found:** `ErrorBoundary` already wrapped the routed app (both at the `App.tsx` root and per-route in `AppLayout.tsx`) and was already catching real crashes -- discovered actually firing in production for the `patternRules.map is not a function` bug (TASK-FE-014). But it showed the raw `error.message` and only a "Return to Dashboard" action (`setState` reset, not a reload) -- Doc 30 wants a friendly generic message, a "Reload" action, and a diagnostic-bundle export option, none of which existed. The toast primitive (`src/hooks/use-toast.ts`, standard shadcn pattern) and `<Toaster />` already existed and were used correctly throughout every page built this session via manual `onError` calls -- but no `errorMapping.ts` (each call site invented its own copy per `AppError` code) and no shared "toast queue triggered automatically by any failed [...] call" existed at all. `useIpcInvoke` (TASK-SETUP-013) existed with zero real consumers anywhere in the app -- it exposed error state but never toasted.

**What I did:** `src/lib/errorMapping.ts` -- the single source of truth mapping every real `AppErrorCode` (`src/types/ipc.ts`'s discriminated union, mirroring `src-tauri/src/error.rs`) to toast copy, including the three Doc 30 names explicitly (`LICENSE_LOCKED`/`LICENSE_INVALID` → "Your subscription needs attention" + a Settings action; `VALIDATION_ERROR` → the backend's message verbatim, since it's already the specific field-level detail; `NETWORK_ERROR` → generic connectivity copy) plus the rest of the real code catalog. `src/lib/toastAppError.tsx` -- the dispatcher turning an `AppError` into a queued toast via the mapping, with a clickable `ToastAction` when a code has a relevant navigation target (uses `window.location.hash` since this app is hash-routed, not `useNavigate`, so it works from non-component call sites too). `src/components/ToastProvider.tsx` -- Doc 30 names this file, but this app's toast store is already a module-level singleton, not React Context, so `toast()` needs no provider to be reachable from anywhere; kept honest as a thin wrapper around `<Toaster />` rather than inventing a Context this codebase doesn't need, with a doc comment explaining why. Wired `useIpcInvoke`'s catch block to call `toastAppError` automatically -- verified via `git grep` that it has zero real consumers yet, so this is genuinely additive with no double-toast risk against the ~15 already-correct, already-tested manual `onError` toast call sites built earlier this session (not mass-migrated; this is the shared entry point for new call sites, per the hook's own existing doc comment that it's "additive... not a required migration"). Rebuilt `ErrorBoundary.tsx`: generic friendly copy (no more raw `error.message` leaking to users), "Reload" (`window.location.reload()` -- a genuine full reload is more reliable than resetting boundary state via `setState` when the underlying app/query state that caused the crash is often still corrupted) and "Export Diagnostic Bundle" (`API.support.exportLogs()`, same real command `PrivacySettings` already uses).

**Opportunistic fix (same illustrative-dotted-event-name bug class, flagged in three separate prior fix-log entries this session):** while verifying the full regression suite, fixed the 3 remaining pre-existing baseline failures directly -- `AppShell.spec.ts`'s "CorruptedDatabaseRecovery UI" and "background task indicator" tests, plus `a11y.spec.ts`'s "modals enforce proper aria roles" test, all dispatched illustrative dotted event names (`db.corrupted`, `task.started`, `task.completed`) that `AppLayout.tsx` never listens for -- the real, already-correctly-wired listeners are `db_corrupted`/`task_started`/`task_completed` (snake_case, matching `AppEvent`'s real string values in `events.rs`). Purely test-string fixes in files already open for this task's own AppShell.spec.ts edits, zero production code changes, exactly the same bug class already fixed for `statement_password_required`/`license_state_changed` etc. this session -- unlike TASK-FE-013's deliberately-deferred `instruments.bank_ifsc` gap, this was a single trivial string fix with no ambiguity.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint` on all new/changed files: clean (one pre-existing, unrelated `use-toast.ts` warning left untouched). `npx vitest run`: 51/51 (+8 new: `errorMapping.test.ts` covers the mapping table including the LICENSE_LOCKED/VALIDATION_ERROR/NETWORK_ERROR cases named by Doc 30 and the unmapped-code fallback; `useIpcInvoke.test.ts` uses `renderHook` + mocked `invokeCommand`/`toast` to prove the auto-toast wiring end-to-end, including that the error is still exposed/rethrown for local handling, not just swallowed into a toast). e2e: new `ErrorBoundary.spec.ts` forces a real render crash via a dedicated mock escape hatch and asserts the friendly screen, no leaked raw error text, a working diagnostic-bundle export, and the Reload action. **Full regression: 68 passed / 0 failed** -- first fully-green run of the entire suite this session (was 65/3 before the opportunistic db_corrupted/task_started fixes above).

Area 9 (FE) now 17/19 done.

## TASK-FE-019: Accessibility and Keyboard Navigation Pass

**Found:** Substantial axe-core infrastructure already existed (`e2e/a11y.spec.ts`, `@axe-core/playwright`, `wcag2a`/`wcag2aa`/`wcag21aa` tags, filtering to critical/serious violations) covering 7 top-level pages, plus dedicated transaction-detail and critical-error-modal tests -- Doc 30's acceptance criterion ("an axe-core automated scan integrated into the E2E test suite") was already substantially met. `/#/settings` -- the single largest, most complex page in the app -- had never once been included in the scanned-pages list despite every other top-level route being covered since this file's introduction.

**Major real bug found+fixed (systemic, codebase-wide):** adding Settings to the scan immediately surfaced a real, critical accessibility violation: `color-contrast` at a computed ratio of **1.04** (near-invisible) on every `.text-muted` element -- foreground `#f9fafb` (near-white) on background `#ffffff` (white). Root cause: `className="text-muted"` was used throughout the app intending "muted secondary text," but Tailwind's `colors.muted` (`tailwind.config.js`) only defines `DEFAULT: hsl(var(--muted))` (a near-white *surface* tone, `--muted: 210 20% 98%`, meant for `bg-muted`) and `foreground: hsl(var(--muted-foreground))` (the actual readable gray text tone, `--muted-foreground: 220 8.9% 46.1%`). `text-muted` therefore applied the *background* tone as text color -- nearly invisible on a white/light surface. This wasn't a one-off typo: it appeared **111 times across 55 files**, spanning every area built this session (Dashboard, Transactions, Instruments, Statements, Reconciliation, Settings, onboarding, shadcn UI primitives themselves) and earlier sessions alike -- a single systemic root cause, not many unrelated issues. Fixed with a scoped, verified find-replace (`text-muted\b(?!-foreground)` -> `text-muted-foreground`, careful not to double-suffix already-correct `text-muted-foreground` occurrences) across all 55 files. Also caught and reverted a self-inflicted collateral mistake from the first pass: the same literal substring also matched inside unrelated inline `style={{ color: 'var(--text-muted)' }}` CSS-custom-property references (a completely different, already-correct `#6b7280` gray defined in `App.css`, unrelated to the Tailwind-class bug) — the initial regex incorrectly rewrote those to `var(--text-muted-foreground)`, a CSS variable that doesn't exist anywhere; caught during verification and reverted in the same pass before this was ever committed.

**Also found+fixed:** two `<select>` elements in `Settings.tsx` (LLM model picker, always visible; billing-interval picker, conditionally visible) with no accessible name (`select-name`, critical) -- added `aria-label` to both.

**Verified via the scan itself (no separate manual audit needed for these):** "correct semantic HTML/ARIA on custom components," "screen-reader-friendly labels for icon-only buttons" (Doc 30's own named example, `SourcePipelineIcon` in `TransactionRow`, already had `aria-label` since TASK-FE-009), and "WCAG AA color contrast for financial status indicators" are all covered by axe's `color-contrast`/`button-name`/`aria-*` rules under the `wcag2a`/`wcag2aa` tags already in use -- the scan passing cleanly across all 8 pages after the fixes above is the actual verification, consistent with the acceptance criterion's own instruction ("no per-component named unit tests beyond the scan itself").

**What I did (keyboard navigation):** added one e2e test proving "Escape closes modals" for an ordinary modal (Add Instrument, Radix `Dialog`) as the explicit counterpoint to the existing test proving the critical-error dialog deliberately does *not* close on Escape -- every non-critical modal in this app already uses Radix's `Dialog` primitive, which handles Escape-to-close by default; this proves it end-to-end for a real one rather than trusting the library blindly.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx eslint` across all 55 touched files (in batches, to avoid an OOM on a whole-project lint run in this environment): zero new issues -- every remaining error/warning is pre-existing `no-explicit-any`/`no-useless-assignment` debt in files this task's className-only edit could not have introduced (confirmed by line number against known prior fix-log entries for the same files). `npx vitest run`: 51/51, unaffected (a CSS class rename touches no logic). e2e: `a11y.spec.ts` now 12/12 (was 10/11 -- the new Settings scan test, the new Escape-on-normal-modal test, both passing; the pre-existing "modals enforce proper aria roles" test already fixed in TASK-FE-018). **Full regression: 70 passed / 0 failed** -- the entire e2e suite fully green, holding from TASK-FE-018's first-ever fully-green run.

Area 9 (FE) now 19/19 done -- Area 9 complete.

## Area 9 Verification Pass — Fix 1: `tags_list` discarded ids, breaking the documented add/remove-tag contract

**Found:** Both `useAddTransactionTag.ts` (TASK-FE-009) and `useRemoveTransactionTag.ts` (TASK-FE-010) bypassed the dedicated `transactions_add_tag`/`transactions_remove_tag` commands that Document 19 §8.7/§8.8 explicitly defines (`{ transaction_id, tag_id } -> "tag_added"/"tag_removed"`), routing instead through `transactions_update`'s undocumented bulk `tags: string[]` name-based replace field. Root cause, honestly self-flagged in both files' own doc comments at the time: `tags_list` (`commands/mod.rs`) discarded tag ids entirely (`tags.into_iter().map(|t| t.name).collect()`), so the frontend had no way to resolve a typed tag name to the UUID the real commands require. This is a genuine IPC-contract deviation (wrong shape, wrong command) per the execution protocol's Step C, not a stylistic issue.

**What I did:** Changed `tags_list` to return the full `TagsRow` (`id`, `name`, `color_hex`, `created_at`) instead of bare names. Added `TagRecord` to `src/lib/ipc.ts` and an `API.tags.create` wrapper for `tags_create`. Rewrote both mutation hooks to resolve the typed tag name against the id-bearing `tags_list` cache (case-insensitive), creating the tag via `tags_create` if new, then calling the real `API.transactions.addTag`/`removeTag` (which already existed, correctly wired, just unused). Fixed `TransactionDetail.tsx`'s tag-suggestion `<datalist>` for the new `TagRecord[]` shape. Fixed `e2e/fixtures/tauriMock.ts`: `tags_list` returned bare strings and `tags_create`/`transactions_add_tag`/`transactions_remove_tag` had zero mocks at all (would have silently no-opped/fallback-{}'d in every e2e run touching tags).

**Verified:** `cargo check --lib` and `cargo test --lib tags`: clean, 3/3. `pnpm tsc --noEmit`: clean. `pnpm exec vitest run`: 51/51. `pnpm exec playwright test`: 70/70 (full suite, zero regressions).

## Area 9 Verification Pass — Fix 2: Statement-Only Mode banner missed the degraded-account (BR-01) case

**Found:** Flagged honestly back in TASK-AUTH-016's own fix-log entry ("the already-connected-user-loses-access case does not [get covered]"). `StatementOnlyModeBanner.tsx` (TASK-FE-017) triggered only on `connectedAccounts.length === 0`. But `settings_get_connected_accounts` (`oauth.rs`) keeps a token-refresh-failed account in the returned list with `account_status = 'degraded'` rather than dropping it — only a full disconnect removes the row — and `polling.rs` only polls accounts with `account_status` `ACTIVE`/`active`. So the actual Document 01 BR-01 scenario this task exists for (Gmail access revoked/restricted while previously connected) left `connectedAccounts.length` at 1, and the banner silently never fired — exactly the gap TASK-AUTH-016 named.

**What I did:** Changed the trigger from `connectedAccounts.length === 0` to `connectedAccounts.every(a => a.account_status?.toLowerCase() !== 'active')`, covering both the zero-accounts case and the degraded-only case in one check. Added a new e2e test (`StatementOnlyMode.spec.ts`) proving the banner shows when the sole connected account is `degraded`.

**Verified:** `pnpm tsc --noEmit`: clean. `pnpm exec playwright test`: 71/71 (full suite, +1 new test, zero regressions).

## Area 9 Verification Pass — Fix 3: License Lock Overlay still blocked read-only navigation, just less obviously

**Found:** TASK-FE-016's own fix-log entry already fixed one version of this bug (the overlay used to replace the entire app, sidebar included). But the fix stopped short: `LicenseLockOverlay` was an opaque `absolute inset-0 backdrop-blur-sm` scrim mounted inside `<main>`, wrapping `<Outlet />` at the `AppLayout` level -- meaning it re-rendered on top of *every* routed page's content, not just the page open when the lock first appeared. Clicking a sidebar link did change the URL (the existing e2e test only checked this), but the destination page's real content was still hidden behind the same opaque overlay. Doc 30's explicit requirement -- "blocking write interactions but explicitly still allowing navigation to read-only views" -- was violated in substance while appearing satisfied by the existing (insufficient) test.

**What I did:** Since the actual write-gate is already enforced backend-side (`assert_write_allowed` fails closed on every mutating command, independent of what the frontend renders), the overlay's job is purely communicative. Converted it from a full-pane blocking scrim to a persistent, non-dismissable banner (same positioning pattern as `GracePeriodBanner`, rendered above `<Outlet />` rather than over it) so every route's real content stays visible and readable while locked. Moved it from `<main>`'s overlay slot into the banner stack in `AppLayout.tsx`. Strengthened `AppShell.spec.ts`'s LOCKED test to navigate to `/transactions` while locked and assert the real page content (`h1:has-text("Transactions")`), not just the URL, is visible alongside the still-present lock banner.

**Verified:** `pnpm tsc --noEmit`: clean. `pnpm exec playwright test`: 71/71 (full suite, zero regressions).

## Area 9 Verification Pass — Fix 4: batch statement uploads gave no feedback while queued behind the 5-concurrent-parser cap

**Found:** Doc 30's TASK-FE-012 explicitly asks for "a queued state for items beyond the backend's 5-concurrent-parser cap (TASK-STMT-009)." The real signal for this already existed and was already correct: `statement_batch_progress` (`queues.rs`'s `BatchProgressTracker`, emitted with `{parsed, total, eta_seconds}` for batches over 10 files as each one clears the `Semaphore(5)`) -- but had zero frontend listeners anywhere in the app. `statements_upload`'s IPC round trip returns as soon as each file is intake-validated and handed to the queue (fire-and-forget, per the backend's own doc comment), long before the actual parsing finishes, so `StatementUploadDropzone`'s `isUploading` spinner cleared almost immediately and a large batch then gave zero feedback for the entire multi-minute wait while files sat behind the concurrency cap.

**What I did:** Added a `batchProgress` field to `GlobalStateContext` (matching the existing `statement_parsed`/`statement_password_required`/etc. listener pattern already in the same file), subscribed to `statement_batch_progress`, auto-clearing once `parsed >= total`. `StatementUploadDropzone` now shows "Parsing X of Y statements (max 5 at a time)… ~Zs remaining" instead of a static idle prompt whenever a batch is still processing, independent of the (already-resolved) upload round trip.

**Verified:** `pnpm tsc --noEmit`: clean. New e2e test drives the real event end-to-end (progress text appears, then clears at parsed>=total). `pnpm exec vitest run`: 51/51. `pnpm exec playwright test`: 72/72 (full suite, +1 new test, zero regressions).
